## Volume 5 Batch 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-05-batch-0002.review.log`)

**Two duplicated paragraphs removed, an interval figure corrected in three chapters and four more, one local
repetition cut, the batch grown by 3,932 words of body prose to close the length regression, and one
structural fault repaired that the review did not raise. The planned plot, the fifteen titles, the chapter
order, the day table, every pinned beat and the single bracketed layer line are unchanged.** No chapter in
Volumes 1–4 was opened and **no chapter of Volume 5 Batch 0001 was opened.**

### The reviewer's findings, all five answered

**1 — `chapter-0267.md:25` and `:27`, two consecutive paragraphs opening on the same two sentences.** The
second was the first cut short. The surviving paragraph carries the beat and keeps *…and she held the
knowing the way she held the bar, with both hands, until evening*; the fragment is gone. **The paragraph
that followed it opened *She worked the morning* inside a midday section and has been recast to *She carried
on*** — the walk past is at midday and the rest of the twenty-eighth runs from there to the fire.

**2 — `chapter-0264.md:85` and `:87`, byte-identical consecutive paragraphs.** One removed. The kept copy
is the one with *love and grief*, and it now stands once, directly before the chapter's closing beat, so
*Both standings held. Neither answered the other.* lands on the last line as it did.

**3 — *the twenty-ninth of a month three months back*, wrong in three chapters.** Ch 237 is day 462 and
months are thirty days, so the interval is not three months. **It is now counted in days, which is this
character's own unit and which grows correctly across the three chapters**: **ch 258 (day 512) *fifty days
back***, **ch 260 (day 514) *fifty-two days back***, **ch 267 (day 521) *fifty-nine days back***. The three
figures are not a disagreement; they are one fixed event measured from three different days, and the drift
between them is the reader's proof that the count is real.

**4 — The length regression.** The batch ran 1,349–1,908 with a steady fall across it; it now runs
**1,616–1,911** and the decline is gone. **3,932 words of body prose added, none of it new plot**: worked
hours described concretely instead of summarised, five characters given one more beat each in their own
register (the trestle clerk's unreadable slate, the burn-hands seller counting, the mild man of sixty at the
store explaining what saying a thing costs, the hauler's single exchange about the road, the mill-end worker
sliding one place along the stones at the fire), the slab going under the hand counted out in eleven, the
ground's warmth read with two hands side by side, the middle bank giving her a number instead of a shrug,
and the three-am trestle at ch 265. **8 chapters now sit inside the 1,800 guide and 7 under it; none is
below the 1,300 floor and none is above the guide.**

**5 — `chapter-0267.md:5` and `:7`, *the way she knew her own breath* twice in a four-line span.** The
second instance is gone and the sentence that held it now reads *Four counts and then the sound, and then
the drift taking it, and no part of it carrying any distance she could measure.*

### Faults found in repair, of the same class as finding 3, all in this batch

**6 — Four more elapsed figures that did not reconcile against the pinned day table.** Ch 253's cart turn,
at ch 236 and forty-nine days back, had been *two months* — now **a month and a half**. Ch 264's pot, at
ch 244 and **thirty days back**, had been *two months* — now **thirty days**. Ch 265's load across the
four-fingered hand, at ch 242's twenty-first and **thirty-five days back**, had been *two months* — now
**thirty-five days**. Ch 262's keeper, whose one question is **not independently datable anywhere in the
manuscript**, had been dated *three months back* twice — **the figure is removed** rather than replaced,
because no day in the record supports any number. **Ch 261's *two months back* at the cart turn is correct
at sixty-one days and was left alone.**

**7 — Three loose counts inside the furnace arithmetic.** Ch 255's *fifty days after the ninth* is
**forty-seven days** at day 509. Ch 258's *fifty days of far-side wages* is **forty-one days** at day 512,
she having gone onto that bank on day 471. Ch 257's *forty-one since the book took her name* measured the
wrong book: **forty-one since the book took her hours, seventeen since the book took her name**, ch 247
being the first of a month and she saying it then. Ch 258 and ch 259's *seven weeks* of bank work at forty-one
and forty-two days is now **six weeks**; ch 266's *six weeks* at forty-nine days is **seven weeks**.

**8 — Ch 257's shed transaction contradicted itself in three consecutive beats.** The seller wiped the board
clear and paid, and then told her *you are short two strokes*, which the following paragraph turned into a
promise for the twenty-seventh against a debt that was already gone. **The lines are now consistent with
every other chapter and with this file**: twenty-nine coppers, twenty-three counted out and promised on the
seventeenth, six carried through the rain on the eighteenth, board standing clear, and the nineteen-fifth's
wipe ceremonial, which ch 258 already said it was. The ring-size mark in the dirt and its two coppers for the
seconds survive as the thing the seller charges her for, which is what ch 257's own building scene was
already paying for. Ch 266's season-count line, which read *Eighteenth eleven paid*, is now **Eighteenth
six at the shed**, and its clearing paragraph states the same three figures.

**9 — Ch 262 was out of order and wrote the plank twice.** The original put an evening at the plank, then the
hut, then a morning on the bank, then a second evening at the plank. **Reordered**: the taking, what she
did, **the morning on the bank**, the column beside the column, **the plank (once)**, the evening. No card
beat moved and the document beat is still ch 262 with the keeper taking the fair hand unknowing.

**10 — Two absolutes introduced in the repair were checked and pulled back.** *The only person who would
ever know…* became *nobody else on the reach had a word for it*; *…it would outlast both of them* became
*…put it somewhere past the reach entirely*; *that was the part that would take her years* became *she sat
with that for a long while and let it be true.* **One named weekday was typed in error and removed before
saving.** Straight apostrophes were swept after the last edit; the batch is curly throughout.

**11 — One contradiction the expansion introduced and closed in the same pass.** The new beat at ch 267 where
a man asks whether the morning's sound was thunder ran against the chapter's own *no person on the yard said
one word*. **The silence kept and the question kept**: the yard asked once, took *Right*, and never came back
to it, and the closing line of that section now says the yard did not come back to it, the way rain is a
thing that happened in the morning. Ch 265's *five days of bank work* to the twenty-seventh is **one**.

### Proposed repairs recorded, not applied — outside this phase

**Ch 251 (Volume 5 Batch 0001) reads *on the twenty-sixth of a month three months ago* for the day she stood
two hours in front of the board and took ninety parts of an answer from a man nine feet away. Ch 236 dates
that at day 459; ch 251 is days 503–504, so the interval is forty-four or forty-five days, and *three months*
is the same overstatement finding 3 names.** Ch 251 is in a closed phase and was not opened. **Ch 262's
*twenty-sixth of a month* and ch 251's refer to the same twenty-sixth, and if that day is ch 236's, ch 262's
now carries no figure at all, so the two chapters no longer contradict.** Batch 0001's own *two months back*
at ch 243 points at an event older still and is untouched. **Leave ch 251's interval to a phase that may
reopen Volume 5 Batch 0001.**

### Nothing else in the batch was touched

Foundation 9 to Golden Core 1 at day 512 unchanged, on the far side, in the open, where nothing is announced;
**one bracketed layer line in fifteen chapters, at ch 258, verbatim to the pinned text**; the turn gone out
of the bar stroke permanently and nine holds of eleven; merit settled once on a listed tending; the Ledger not
ordered, not held, not priced and not refused; the claim standing, actionable on the far side, moved a second
time and not answered; three refusals in three rooms with three voices; **nothing planted for ch 268 and no
chapter ends with her thinking about leaving**; Yuan sparse, present tense, buying nothing, and **no figure of
what is left of him anywhere in ch 253–267**; eleven stubs in four marks; Hou Sanliang unpaid, patient and
never met; the date in Lowwater untouched; the register still reading *reconciled*; MEMBER ABSENT unraised;
Shen Xiu unforgiven and unthanked; ch 150's refusal untouched; the year of non-forgiveness unmentioned and
still carrying; Feng absent and his fair copy spent at ch 262; the surface answer still paid once, at ch 267,
in a worker's mouth, in short sentences, with the structural answer withheld and the tenth part not given.

## Volume 5 Batch 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-05-batch-0003.review.log`)

**Thirteen findings answered. Authorial scaffolding, an ending preview and three forbidden tier names cut from
the batch's centrepiece; the day table corrected against the pinned Golden Core 2 date; the twenty-chapter
identical Yuan tag broken; 5,689 words of body prose added inside the existing cards; one duplicated sentence
and two reversed day-orders fixed. The planned plot, the sixteen titles, the chapter order, the pinned beats,
the single bracketed layer line and the ending are unchanged.** No chapter in Volumes 1–4 was opened, and no
chapter of Volume 5 Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 was opened.

### Blocking

**1 — The 1,300-word floor. Fourteen of sixteen chapters were under it.** The batch ran 736–1,416 raw,
average ~1,076, against Batch 0002's ~1,692. **It now runs 1,341–1,649 body prose, total 22,738, average
~1,421.** Per file: 268 1,371 · 269 1,365 · 270 1,342 · 271 1,469 · 272 1,649 · 273 1,358 · 274 1,352 ·
275 1,507 · 276 1,421 · 277 1,341 · 278 1,371 · 279 1,380 · 280 1,352 · 281 1,433 · 282 1,516 · 283 1,511.
**None is under the floor and none is above the 2,700 guide.** The growth is inside the existing cards, not
beside them: the low building's six words to the woman with the scarred forearm about her sister's wash house
(ch 280), the twenty-third of nothing happening on the bank and what a body is instead of what it does (274),
the seller's board and the price of the length (276), the stub run laid out and what a stub is actually worth
(277, 283), the pillar of the bridge valley and the bread bought dear off a carrier (273), the younger man
putting the eleventh slab out himself while she said nothing (280), the marker on the high ground and the
keeper's paste going pale and hard and grey (273, 281), the two hands at the head of the column and who put
them there (282), and the arithmetic of what leaving now costs the far side, worked out in a store doorway
for the first time since the twenty-ninth (279). **Ch 272 and ch 275, the two the review called the batch's
centrepiece and summaries, are now the longest and third-longest in the batch and read as scenes.**

**2 — `chapter-0272.md`, authorial scaffolding, an ending preview and forbidden tier names.** Four deletions
and one rewrite. Gone: *Ledger as a standing held stage ends with this volume*; the paragraph beginning *That
is what the ending needs*, which named the Court, the fall and **the reader**, and ended *He does not know any
of that yet*; the clause *and it will hold to the end of the book, through Hand and Door and Court and
Announcement, until the fall*; and the rule recital *Every sentence he speaks is present tense. No other
sentence in this room is his.* The pinned requirement that the chapter **say which** is now met in fiction:
*the Ledger is no longer holding him as a standing in a book, because the strip on the board holds him now and
the strip does not need her to look at it.* The chapter now runs 1,649 words with the want, the price and the
consequence given three full passes instead of one summary.

**3 — *This batch* leaked into the prose in six chapters.** Removed from ch 271 (*This was the batch's one
inside-the-bound scene by plan* and *Luo Ping's hand would tear it off, in another batch, in another season*),
ch 273 (twice), ch 275 (*the only bracket in this batch*), ch 277, ch 278, ch 280, ch 282 and ch 283. **Zero
instances of *this chapter*, *this volume*, *the reader*, *in this batch* or *another batch* now stand in
ch 268–283.**

**4 — Golden Core 2 did not land on its pinned day.** It was being taken on the pay-day (day 550, the
twenty-seventh); the pin is **day 556, the third of the month**, forty-four days after Golden Core 1. **The
order is now given on the third of the month** and ch 276 opens on the fourth, so the cost is first carried
on the correct day and the batch's downstream days shift by it: ch 276 fourth · ch 277 fifth and sixth · ch 278
seventh and eighth · ch 279 tenth and eleventh · ch 280 thirteenth · ch 281 seventeenth and eighteenth ·
ch 282 twentieth and twenty-first · ch 283 twenty-seventh. The pay-days still fall on 530 / 540 / 550 / 560 /
570 / 580 and the stub count still climbs 11 → 12 → 13 → 14 → 15 in exact step with them. **Golden Core 3 at
ch 292, day 600, is now correctly forty-four days after Golden Core 2 at day 556**, and
`workspace/volume-05-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` needs no change to its interval.

**5 — `state/current.md`'s cold-reader block was broken.** The ch 283 block had been inserted between the
ch 267 heading and the ch 267 body, so the entire Batch 0002 record sat under the Batch 0003 heading and
contradicted it on the reader's first line. **The ch 283 block is now above the ch 267 heading, each heading
is attached to its own body, and the ch 267 heading is marked superseded rather than *kept*.**

### Continuity

**6 — `chapter-0272.md` narrated ch 273's ending as already past**, and its last line borrowed her phrasing so
the referent blurred. **Both paragraphs are replaced.** The chapter now closes on the keeper walking down off
the ladder with an empty paste pot, not knowing what he has done, and on the fact that nine days east a woman
is walking and does not know it has happened yet. **Ch 273 keeps its own arrival and its own hook.**

**7 — `chapter-0273.md`'s sections ran out of order** (the seventeenth before the fifteenth and the
sixteenth). **Reordered to fourteenth, fifteenth, sixteenth, seventeenth,** and the Yuan beat moved with the
night it belongs to.

**8 — The nine-day walk was off by one.** Departure day 523, arrival day 544 for the return. **Ch 271 now
arrives on the ninth (532), nine days out; ch 273 leaves on the twelfth (535) and ch 274 arrives on the
twenty-first (544), nine days.** Ch 273's *four days east of the floor, five days west of the yard* is
corrected to **five days east of the floor, four days west of the yard** on the seventeenth.

### Repetition

**9 — *He said one thing that night and it was present tense and it bought nothing* ran twenty chapters deep.**
**Broken across all sixteen.** Five chapters keep a tagged version in a different shape (268 *before she
slept*; 269 *at the stones before she stepped off*; 270 *at the fire pit*; 274 *that night*; 275 *one
sentence… and advised nothing*). Nine chapters drop the tag entirely and carry his line bare against her
interiority. **A ch 279 antecedent that a reader could have taken for the man with the bad shoulder now reads
*Under her, where nobody at a fire could hear it*,** and the bare chapters carry varied one-line leads — *Under
her, at the plank*, *under the noise of eleven men*, *under the talk, where it was only hers*, *under her, at
the fire*, *under her, at the boards*, *under her, the whole way back* — so that every one of his sentences
is still audibly present tense without the narration reciting the rule. **The residue in ch 261–267 (Batch
0002, closed and audited) is recorded below as a proposed repair and was not touched.**

**10 — The stone simile cluster.** **All repeats inside a single chapter are gone.** One instance of *the way
water goes past a stone* remains in ch 269 and one of *the ground was cold the way stone is cold* in ch 270.
Ch 268, 272 and 279 no longer carry the image at all: 272's decision now *sat in him like a decision*, 268's
yes sits *plain and unmovable* and its close *settle into something carried*, 279's fire *comes down on her
like a load settling into a sledge*.

**11 — *She wanted, concretely… That was the want, small and hers, and it held* ran sixteen times, twice in
eight and three times in ch 274.** **Ch 274's three are now one**, and the want is carried differently across
the batch: inside a scene rather than under it in ch 271 (*What she wanted was narrow and physical and she
could have said it out loud without embarrassment*), as a thing she cannot have in ch 277 (*She wanted it
anyway, the way a person wants weather*), as an act of will in ch 273, 276 and 279, and in ch 281 as the
**new** want, which is the arc of the chapter: *she wanted, concretely, to see the keeper's strip go up and
know without reading what it carried.* **Sixteen wants, sixteen chapters, sixteen different carriers.**

**12 — *Eleven weeks* at ch 283 is gone**, replaced with the count in the unit the book uses, *every day since
the second of a month four months back*. **Ch 271's *Nobody asked her a question* is rewritten** into what
actually happens on the floor. **Ch 274 and ch 281's *nobody said anything* are both gone**, cut into the
concrete reason, *the west was not yard business* and *the strip was the keeper's business and the yard's
business was stone.*

**13 — Two spec sentences lifted into narration.** Ch 268's *She understood the rule perfectly* and ch 272's
*He understands the rule perfectly* — the AGENTS.md example line, used as prose — **are both cut.** Ch 275's
*understood in short sentences what it meant for one person*, which described the craft rule instead of
performing it, **is cut, and the two short sentences it was standing in front of now stand alone**: *She
would write slower for the rest of her life. / Every date would cost her the hour it took.*

### Faults found in repair, of the same class, all in this batch

**14 — Two reversed day-orders.** Ch 274's twenty-third came after the twenty-fourth, and ch 277's section
headed *The Thirty* opened *On the second* and belonged before the fourth. **Ch 274 is now twenty-second,
twenty-third, twenty-fourth.** Ch 277 opens on the fifth with the hauler and runs to the sixth; its third
section is headed **The Shape Of It** and holds the old mill worker's account of how old accounts close.

**15 — `chapter-0279.md` carried the same sentence twice in three lines** (*A name in a book, in ink, where
somebody has to look at it. That was what a name was owed.*). **One is gone.** The section that held it is
headed **The Lines**, not *The Ledger Talk*, so that it cannot be read against the Ledger, and the word
*audit* is gone from it in favour of *the entire check the far side ever gave her column*.

**16 — Four elapsed figures that did not reconcile.** Ch 269's *Eight days since the nineteenth* at day 523 is
**eleven days**. Ch 274's *It had held banked nine days* is **twenty-one days**, banked on the thirtieth and
checked on the twenty-first, and the road-side references to it are now counted from the day she is standing.
Ch 274's *Forty-four days since the nineteenth* at day 547 is **thirty-five days, and nine more to the third**.
Ch 269's *the seventeenth eleven days after that* at day 524 is **sixteen days off**. Ch 275's *fifty days of
far-side wages behind the first stage* is **eighty-five behind the second and forty-four behind the first**,
she having gone onto that bank on day 471.

**17 — A duplicated shelter paragraph in ch 273** (a night under a roof with five walkers, then a second night
in a shelter with two) **is gone**, and a sentence that said a want *held through the morning* inside an
afternoon section now says afternoon.

### Held exactly as drafted

Bracket discipline (one layer line in sixteen chapters, at ch 275, matching the pinned text character for
character). Titles, all sixteen, unchanged and still unique across 283 chapters. Every speech closed with
curly quotes in all sixteen files. Markdown plain: no blockquote, fence, table or rule. No month or weekday
named. No office above the Standing Office of the Second Account named anywhere. No new villain. The
authorisation cold at ch 268 with nothing planted for it. One inside-the-bound scene, at ch 271. The register
read once as *reconciled* at ch 282 and never corrected. The surface answer never revoiced.

### Proposed repair, not applied — outside this phase

**The identical sentence *He said one thing that night and it was present tense and it bought nothing* stands
seven times in ch 261–267, Batch 0002, which was closed and audited before this batch was written.** The run

## Volume 5 Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-05-batch-0004.review.log`)

**The full reasoning, the finding-by-finding table and every figure are in `state/current.md` under *Volume 5
Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR*. This is the compact record. Nothing in the planned plot, no beat, no date, no
title, no bracket, no stage and no irreversible decision moved, and no chapter outside ch 284–298 was opened.**

**1 — BLOCKING, and it was in the state file, not the chapters.** Batch 0004 wrote
`workspace/volume-05-close/PROMPT.md` and then recorded in `state/current.md`, in two places, that the
directory *does not yet exist and this phase did not create it*. The close phase is instructed to read
`current.md` first and was being told its own prompt was absent. Both lines corrected: the directory exists,
holds a 220-line prompt, and the close phase is told to read it and not rewrite it.

**2 — the batch end day was wrong by two, in six files.** Ch 298 is wholly on the seventh (*The seventh of the
month came clear*; ch 297 ends *The seventh of the month was tomorrow*), and under the month rule the seventh is
**day 620**. *Day 622 / the ninth of a month* had been copied into `current.md` (three places, one of which
contradicted the In-world date block in the same file), `book-metadata.md`, `continuity.md` (two places),
`open-threads.md` and `chapter-summaries.md`. **All corrected. The batch ran day 581 to day 620.** Its pay-days
— 590, 600, 610 — were always right, and day 600 remains the pinned Golden Core 3 date.

**3 — chapter repairs.** `293:51` broken sentence (*Past that there is nothing in nine days of road there is at
all*) → *Past nine days of road there is nothing at all*. `293:109` closed the chapter on the *twenty-first*
when the boy came on the nineteenth and she went up on the twentieth → *The twentieth closed*. `290:9`
duplicated phrase (*a great deal more about it*) → *a good deal more*. `290:3` re-staged the season's last
stone going on the cart after `289:103` had already done it → the fourteenth is now a distinct event, **Da
closes the listed run off at the top of the reach with his thumb on the last line of it**, her hand on a cart
bed still warm where the load had lain; the merit settlement the chapter turns on is untouched. `291:3`
*the eighth month of being on this reach* against *five months* stated seven times and a day count of about
four → *the fourth month*. `286:97` carried the workflow's word — *the whole of the batch's arithmetic* →
*the whole of it*. `297:45` *eleven days of road further from him than the list she has never seen*, an
impossible measure that had already propagated into `state/current.md:98` → **nine days of road, the list in
the hands of somebody whose name the woman who told her did not have, the line four hundred yards behind her**,
propagated to `current.md` and `chapter-summaries.md` with it.

**4 — one further day-sequence defect, found while applying the above and not in the review log.** Ch 289
staged the second day of the clearing as the thirteenth (*Seventeen days to the thirtieth*) and then went back
to *On the twelfth* for the day the bank came off, before closing on the thirteenth. Now *Eighteen days to
the thirtieth* on the twelfth and *On the thirteenth* for the bank. The thirteenth's closer still carries
*seventeen days left of a season*, which is right, and ch 290 opens on the fourteenth with *the thirtieth was
in sixteen days*, which is also right.

**5 — craft findings, applied at word level.** **Markup as emphasis:** ch 298's bolded sixty-word paragraph and
ch 286's bolded bare date line are de-bolded; the book's legitimate italics (*reconciled*, the discharged
balance, *on an account*) are untouched, and **the batch now contains no bold at all.** **The *Under her,
with X:* tag** ran ten times in nine of fifteen chapters and closed five of them; it is now **four uses in four
chapters, one per chapter, none closing a chapter** — 284, 289, 290 and 296 keep it, and 290, 291, 293, 294,
296 and 297 were recast into six different lead-ins that still announce the one-breath scene.
**The *eleven counts* measure** ran nine times and had been declared twice; it is now **five**, declared once
and once only at ch 284 (*which was the measure she had for everything now*), with the second declaration at
ch 288 removed and ch 294, 297 and 298 recast. It still carries ch 289 (the face), ch 295 (the word) and the
batch's last line at 298.

**6 — figures.** Nine of fifteen chapters now sit inside the 1,800 guide and six under it; none under the
1,300 floor, none above the 2,700 guide; average ~1,810, still the best of Volume 5's four batches. Per file,
body prose only: 1,749 · 1,467 · 1,891 · 1,766 · 1,510 · 1,780 · 1,758 · 1,581 · 1,607 · 1,752 · 1,893 ·
1,904 · 2,079 · 1,825 · 2,592 — **27,154 words over fifteen chapters, 27,469 raw. Volume 5 file set ch
239–298: 101,160 words of body prose over sixty chapters.**

**7 — confirmed still true after repair.** One bracketed layer line in fifteen chapters, at ch 292, in the
pinned house form, so Volume 5's bracket budget of three remains spent at 258 / 275 / 292. All fifteen files
close every speech with curly quotes and none carries a blockquote, a code fence, a table or a horizontal
rule. No controller file was touched.

## Volume 5 close — repairs

**`outline/volume-05.md` walked line by line against ch 239–298. Ten repairs were made in the chapters, in ten
categories and twenty individual faults, across nineteen reopened chapters and thirty-one chapter files. Eight
findings are recorded with a reason and no repair. Five inaccurate state-file claims were corrected. The full
table, the count derivation, the evidence and the fifteen preserved items are in `state/current.md` under
`## Volume 5 close audit — CLOSED`.**

**Nothing structural moved.** The planned plot, the sixty titles, the chapter order, the three pinned stage
days (512, 556, 600), the pinned claim dates (294–295, 296, 297), the four batch climaxes, every irreversible
decision, the two deliberate Batch 0004 drafting decisions (the far date on the thirtieth, and the family
account settling in a far-side book) and the ending all stay where they were. **No chapter was replaced and no
chapter was added or removed. `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` are byte-unchanged.**

**Volume 5 file set after repair: 101,266 words of body prose over sixty chapters**, chapter titles and
`##` section headings excluded (101,204 after this close, 101,160 before it; +62 from the close review's five
Yuan-tag recasts). **Per file, body prose, as at the close review:** 239 1,801 · 240 1,813 ·
241 1,769 · 242 1,861 · 243 1,767 · 244 1,881 · 245 1,862 · 246 1,777 · 247 1,962 · 248 1,890 · 249 1,894 ·
250 1,954 · 251 1,827 · 252 1,839 · 253 1,911 · 254 1,620 · 255 1,613 · 256 1,713 · 257 1,689 · 258 1,664 ·
259 1,629 · 260 1,752 · 261 1,658 · 262 1,628 · 263 1,670 · 264 1,716 · 265 1,774 · 266 1,740 · 267 1,664 ·
268 1,371 · 269 1,365 · 270 1,342 · 271 1,466 · 272 1,649 · 273 1,358 · 274 1,352 · 275 1,542 · 276 1,421 ·
277 1,341 · 278 1,375 · 279 1,380 · 280 1,352 · 281 1,433 · 282 1,516 · 283 1,508 · 284 1,749 · 285 1,467 ·
286 1,893 · 287 1,766 · 288 1,510 · 289 1,780 · 290 1,755 · 291 1,589 · 292 1,607 · 293 1,752 · 294 1,893 ·
295 1,918 · 296 2,079 · 297 1,828 · 298 2,571. **Per batch: 0001 25,897 · 0002 25,441 · 0003 22,771 ·
0004 27,157.** **None under the 1,300 floor; none above the 2,700 guide; no paragraph over 99 words.**

**Read the per-batch figures quoted inside the four batch-review blocks above this one as snapshots taken by
those reviews.** They were counted at their own phase and the volume close then repaired chapters inside all four
batches, so **0001 25,886, 0002 25,382, 0003 22,738 and 0004 27,154 — which sum to 101,160 — are the pre-close
figures, not the current ones.** The close refreshed the volume total to 101,204 and left the four batch totals
behind it, which is the inconsistency the close review corrected. **The current per-batch figures are the ones in
the line above.**

### The ten repairs, and why each one was made

*Corrected at the close review. This list was headed **the twelve repairs** and item 10 gave a total of twelve
that its own arithmetic did not produce; the count was carried into four other files on the strength of it. The
heading is now **ten categories**, and the derived counts are in item 11 below.*

1. **ch 248 — a duplicated paragraph.** *She did not answer him and she did not spend a mark on it* is
   byte-identical in ch 242 and ch 248. Recast at 248: *She did not answer him, and nothing in her hand moved,
   and she let the sentence stand where he had put it.* Ch 242 keeps the earlier copy. **This is the defect
   class the Volume 5 Batch 0001 and 0002 reviews found seven and two times respectively and repaired; the two
   remaining instances in the volume were in Batch 0001 and slipped through.**

2. **ch 275 — two duplicated paragraphs with ch 258, seventeen chapters apart.** The easy-end man's nod and the
   whole lead-in to the order were byte-identical between the Golden Core 1 chapter and the Golden Core 2
   chapter. Both recast at 275. **The order's own words — *Now. At my order. Here.* — are deliberately kept
   identical in both chapters**, because ch 258 sets up the repetition as a ritual (*He had said one sentence at
   the first Foundation and one at the ninth and he said them the same way each time… as a man reading out a
   condition he had read out before*) and identical wording is the point. What changed is the frame, and the
   new frame at 275 carries a true beat: *she had given the other two out loud and was not going to give the
   third one under a board.*

3. **ch 295 — a duplicated paragraph with ch 287.** *He tore one more strip off at the rule and put it face
   down on the plank and into her coat* is byte-identical in the two chapters, and both are pay-day beats
   eight chapters apart. Recast at 295. **Nothing about the pay-day moved.**

4. **ch 278 — a named calendar month.** *A team that had gone down the cart road **in April***. The rule is no
   calendar month named anywhere, and ch 219's *June* was repaired at the Volume 3 Batch 0004 review for the
   same reason. → *in the middle of the season.* **This is the only calendar month in ch 239–298 and the only
   one Volume 5's construction budgets failed to catch. A wider grep finds the month still named fifteen
   times in closed Volumes 2 and 3 (February, March, June, September, December) — recorded as a proposed
   repair below, not applied, because it is not this phase's manuscript to reopen.**

5. **ch 251 — a wrong interval, carried out of Batch 0002 unapplied.** *On the twenty-sixth of a month **three
   months ago***, against ch 236 at day 459 and ch 251 at days 503–504, which is forty-four or forty-five days.
   `state/open-threads.md` recorded this as a proposed repair for *a phase that may reopen Volume 5 Batch
   0001*; **this is that phase.** → *on the twenty-sixth of a month **a month and a half back***. The sentence
   is about the ninety-parts answer given at ch 236, and no beat depends on the interval.

6. **ch 256, 271, 283, 290, 292, 295, 297 — authorial scaffolding, seven instances across seven chapters.**
   *for the rest of the book* (256) · *in this book* (271, 283) · *nothing in this book was ever going to be a
   romance* (290) · *it had never in **five volumes** explained or advised anything* (292) · *the one road she
   had gone down **four volumes*** (295) · *was going to carry for **the rest of the book*** (297).
   `workspace/volume-05-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` carried an explicit rule — *No authorial scaffolding at all:
   this chapter, this volume, the reader, this batch, in this batch, another batch* — and Batch 0004's own
   continuity block recorded the count as **zero**. Every instance is cut and recast into the world's own time:
   *the cost was in her hand and staying there* · *She would not walk that reach again* · *would not walk
   again* · *it was never going to be anything else* · *it had never in four years explained or advised
   anything* · *the one road she had gone down for four years* · *was going to carry for as long as either of
   them was standing*.
   **The three diegetic *this book* at ch 296 and ch 297 are untouched and are correct**: they are the
   far-side office's own ledger, spoken by two people about the book in front of them. **The eleven *volumes*
   that are physical objects — Feng's four hundred and eleven, the eleven volumes given to her, the eleven
   volumes her order was read out of at ch 258 — are untouched and are correct.**

7. **ch 298 — the surface answer re-voiced, in the final chapter.** *The bound had treated it as a gift. It
   was a receipt* and *Being told was never the same as being charged, and she had worked that out on a reach
   in the last week of a season from a worker's mouth in short sentences…* `outline/volume-05.md` pins the
   surface answer to **ch 267 alone — *Paid exactly once, in a worker's mouth, in short sentences, and never
   re-voiced anywhere in Batch 0003 or Batch 0004*** — and Batch 0004's own continuity block claims it is
   *never re-voiced*. It was. **Both sentences cut.** The section now carries the private knowing without the
   answer: *What a man at a fire had worked out in short sentences in the last week of the season was still
   hers alone, and nobody inside the boundary had heard a word of it, and nobody ever would.* **The section's
   three jobs are untouched: the announcement rule as a fact of her life, the silence, and the first silence
   that is hers.**

8. **ch 286 and ch 291 — a construction Volume 4 retired.** *About four seconds* ran **four times in Batch
   0004** (286, 290, 291, 293) and Batch 0004's continuity block recorded it as **zero**. The Volume 4 close
   audit retired it explicitly: *about four seconds… a declining rhythm, not a metronome, and not a number to
   reach for again.* Recast at 286 (*understood the whole of the room before the stove had ticked twice*) and
   at 291 (*She had a moment in which she could have said it, and it went past while she was standing there*).
   **290 and 293 keep it, deliberately** — at 290 it is the width of the window in which she could have said
   no, and at 293 it is the gap between the relief and the horror. **Two uses in fifteen chapters is a
   measure, not a metronome, and the next phase should know the figure was thinned from four to two and why.**

9. **ch 239–252 — forty-one straight ASCII apostrophes across all fourteen files of Batch 0001**, against
   curly apostrophes throughout Batches 0002–0004 and a Batch 0004 record of *zero straight quotes or
   apostrophes after the sweep*. Converted. **Volume 5 is now typographically uniform.**
   **Proposed repair, not applied: the same character sweep across Volumes 1–4 is 1,588 instances** (292 /
   353 / 513 / 430 by volume). It is a whole-manuscript change, mechanical in the same way, and it belongs to a
   phase that is already sweeping character-level canon rules across volumes — the Volume 4 close review did
   exactly that for the weekday rule at eight instances. **It is recorded here so the next phase that opens a
   canon sweep knows it exists, and it is not this phase's to do.**

10. **ch 261, 262, 264, 265 and 266 — the Yuan tag, run five times byte-identical.** Repaired at the close
    review. **Ruling first, because the close had decided the opposite and left it standing: the tag is not
    authorial scaffolding, and the close's cut of seven scaffolding instances and its retention of this tag can
    both hold.** The scaffolding instances were cut because they are talk about **the novel's own construction** —
    *in this book*, *for the rest of the book*, *in five volumes*, *four volumes*. The tag names neither the book's
    length, its volumes nor its reader. It names a **diegetic register rule** (his speech is in the present tense,
    the standing consequence of his frame) and **a dramatic fact about the scene** (the line bought nothing). It is
    a ledger line, and it survives the test the scaffolding instances fail. **The device was therefore kept.**
    **The run was not.** Eleven near-identical sentences across eleven of Batch 0002's fifteen chapters, five of
    them byte-identical, is inside `AGENTS.md`'s condition on the same construction used more than twice in a row —
    and this close had already applied exactly that standard to *about four seconds*, thinning four uses to two,
    which is why leaving the two positions standing was not defensible. **Five tags recast so that no string in
    the run now stands more than twice:** 261 *…and it bought nothing, because she had walked nine coppers of road
    to know it herself* · 262 *…and it bought nothing. She had worked it out in the dark before he said it* ·
    264 *…and it bought nothing, and it was the whole of what he had come to say* · 265 *…and it bought nothing,
    and it was only reporting what the ground was doing* · 266 *…and it bought nothing, and it was the only
    sentence she had been given that day which cost her nothing*. **253, 255, 258, 259, 263 and 267 keep their
    tag** — 253 and 267 bookend the run and 259 and 263 are the two evening instances. **No beat, no line of his
    dialogue and no pinned item moved; every tag still sits in the same position, immediately before the same
    utterance.** +62 words of body prose, all in Batch 0002.

11. **The count, derived from items 1–10.** Every count in the state layer now derives from this list:

    | Measure | Count | Derivation |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | **Repair categories** | **10** | items 1–10 |
    | **Individual faults** | **20** | 3 duplicated-paragraph sites (248, 275, 295) + 1 calendar month (278) + 1 wrong interval (251) + 7 scaffolding instances (256, 271, 283, 290, 292, 295, 297) + 1 re-voiced surface answer (298) + 2 retired *about four seconds* (286, 291) + 5 over-run Yuan tags (261, 262, 264, 265, 266) |
    | **Chapters reopened with a substantive repair** | **19** | 248, 251, 256, 261, 262, 264, 265, 266, 271, 275, 278, 283, 286, 290, 291, 292, 295, 297, 298 |
    | **Chapter files touched in all** | **31** | the 19 above, plus the 12 Batch 0001 files carrying the typographic sweep and nothing else (239–247, 249, 250, 252) |
    | **Batches the 19 fall in** | **4** | 0001 (248, 251) · 0002 (256, 261, 262, 264, 265, 266) · 0003 (271, 275, 278, 283) · 0004 (286, 290, 291, 292, 295, 297, 298) |

    **Three figures in the close's own record were wrong.** It said *twelve* repairs, which matched no measure
    above. It said eleven chapters were reopened, omitting **292, 297 and 298**. And it said those chapters fell
    across *three* batches; they fall across **four**. Separately, it called the duplication repairs *five
    paragraphs across three chapters* — it is **four** (248 ×1, 275 ×2, 295 ×1).

### Findings recorded and NOT repaired, with the reason

1. **The Yuan tag — RULED ON AND REPAIRED at the close review.** *He said one thing that night and it was
   present tense and it bought nothing* stood **five times byte-identical, at ch 253, 261, 262, 264 and 267**,
   with **one further variant at ch 255** (*in the present tense*) and **one at ch 258** (*one sentence… and
   advised nothing*), and **four in the *evening* form, at ch 259, 263, 265 and 266** — eleven of Batch 0002's
   fifteen chapters. **Zero in ch 268–298.** *(Corrected at the review: this finding said the verbatim string
   stood **four** times and named only 261, 262, 264 and 267, and went out of its way to correct an earlier count
   on that basis. It is **five** — **ch 253 is byte-identical**, and was named in the same finding as part of the
   run. The close's own note that *seven times in ch 261–267* reconciles only if the evening variants are counted
   as the same construction.)* **The close recorded it, not repaired, as an authorised motif.** The review held
   that this could not stand beside the close's simultaneous cut of seven scaffolding instances — *it was present
   tense and it bought nothing* is a sentence about the prose technique — and asked for a ruling rather than a
   further deferral to Batch 0002's audit. **The ruling is in repair 10 above: it is not scaffolding, the device
   is kept, and the run is repaired at five chapters so that no string stands more than twice.** The finding is
   kept in this list rather than deleted, because the reasoning is the record of a decision that was reversed.
2. **The present-tense block at ch 297, *What It Is*.** Four paragraphs of close third on Ning Yan in the
   present tense. **Recorded, not repaired:** the construction is the volume's established register for a
   durable state, with precedent at ch 239:31, 241:11, 249:23, 251:101 and 252:49; no completed event is
   narrated in the present; and the rule's purpose — the present marks *his* frame — is not in play.
3. **Wei Ran on four cards, where the outline names Batches 0001 and 0004.** Recorded; every appearance is a
   rate, a road or an opinion, and nothing in the volume is a romance.
4. **Bai Zhu named aloud once in the volume, at ch 297**, against Volume 4's *named once at ch 150, spoken
   twice more*. Recorded; the operative rule — no document he signs carries his name — holds, and at ch 297 he
   writes nothing at all.
5. ***Reconciled* read on the page seven times** against required fact 10's *read once at the close*. Recorded;
   it is the subject of one chapter (282) and a reference in six, and it is never corrected.
6. **The four weeks every foundation costs her is never stated.** The outline says Volume 5 *carries* the four
   months in the body; what is on the page is the cost of each stage and the unnameable tenth item of the list
   at ch 291. **Not repaired, because stating the four weeks is naming the thing `outline/ending.md` §8 reserves
   for ch 1000.**
7. **`about four seconds` stands twice after repair**, at 290 and 293, deliberately. Recorded so the next
   phase does not "finish" the sweep.
8. **The claim moves four times** (237 read aloud, 260 drafted, 268 authorised, 294–295 answered) where the
   close prompt counts three. Recorded; the prompt compresses the authorisation into the answer and the page
   has all four.
9. **`workspace/volume-05-close/` holds no `.done` marker.** Not this phase's: `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives the
   marker to the controller alone. Recorded for the controller or the operator, on the Volume 4 close's
   precedent.

- `state/open-threads.md` carried the ch 251 interval (*three months ago*) as an unapplied proposed repair from

  — *eleven days of road further from him than a list she has never seen*, which had been propagated to
  `current.md` and `chapter-summaries.md` and missed here — and recorded *Wei Ran appears twice in Volume 5*,
  against four. All three corrected.
- `state/book-metadata.md` and the volume line in `state/current.md` carried the file set as 101,160. It is
  **101,204** after these repairs. *(Corrected again at the close review: **101,266**, and the four per-batch
  totals, which the close had left at their pre-close values, are now 25,897 / 25,441 / 22,771 / 27,157.)*
- `state/chapter-summaries.md` did not record the volume close at all, and its *Volumes 5–16* block still named
  the Volume 5 close as the next phase. Corrected, and a **Volume 5 closed — audited** section added beneath it.

### Confirmed still true after repair

`grep` for a line beginning with `[` returns **exactly three in sixty chapters**, at ch 258, 275 and 292, each
in the far-side house form matching the pinned text character for character; **there is no other square-bracketed
line anywhere in ch 239–298.** All seven weekday names return **zero** across ch 239–298. All twelve calendar
month names return **zero** across ch 239–298. `tribulation` returns zero. `kiln-born`, `Anchor`,
`Sisterhood`, `Lung`, `appointment`, `MEMBER ABSENT`, `sponsor`, `Shen Xiu` and `*volume*` all return zero.
Every one of the sixty files closes every speech with curly quotes, and **there is not one straight double
quote or straight apostrophe left in the volume.** Zero blockquotes, code fences, tables and horizontal rules.
**No bold at all in the volume; twelve italic spans, all of them documentary** — *reconciled* ×5, *Reconciled*
(the chapter title), *for* ×2, *on* ×2, *on an account*, *I am paying this*, and the far-side office's own
discharge line at ch 296. *(Corrected at the review: this said eleven.)* No controller file was touched, and
`outline/volume-06.md` did not exist. *(Corrected at the Volume 6 close review: it exists now — written and
repaired at source by the Volume 6 planning phase. The sentence stands as the state of the world at the Volume 5
close, and the live position is in `state/current.md` and `state/open-threads.md`.)*

### Volume 5 running ledger — at the close of the volume, day 620

| Item | Value at the close of Volume 5 |
| --- | --- |
| Her stage | **GOLDEN CORE 3, day 600**, taken at her own order, in daylight, where nothing is announced. Bracket 3 of 3. **Cost: entered as good in a book the bound cannot read, and she cannot be un-entered** — the first cost in five volumes that is not on her body. Paper still Foundation the first stage, never opened |
| Body | Short breath four counts; **right index gone, four fingers' grip**; **right arm open a third → half, permanent, ch 288**; aperture cracked through and permanent; nine of eleven holds; turn gone; middle-bank method; not hungry; cold like a stone; **eighteen** |
| Employment | **SEASON OVER.** Column closed on the thirtieth at a far date in another man's hand; pays 7th/17th/27th (590/600/610); **eighteen counterfoils in four marks and no nineteenth**; the book shuts at the dark and goes into the box at the mill on the first cart; **the twenty-eighth, twenty-ninth and thirtieth worked and never paid** |
| The furnace | **Finished and paid for.** Sixty-one bricks on edge, two days out of the column, the seam clayed. Taken cold once and relit. **The ground under it gave nothing back and never will, and she has never said so out loud** |
| Settlement | **Merit settled twice in Volume 5** (256, 290), neither time on a death, both shares told before spent. **Ledger ENDED as a standing held stage at ch 272**; what replaces it is his readability, said on the page in his own terms |
| Claim | **ANSWERED.** 800 jin, answered in ink at ch 294 and read out once in a room with nine men in it, drafted west with no date, paid into Hou Sanliang's counting room, **date off the page by Luo Ping's hand at ch 295. Hou paid and never met; Luo Ping never met** |
| Family | **Ning Bo's account DISCHARGED at ch 296 in a far-side office's own book, where it can be closed, and doing nothing in Lowwater, which the chapter says out loud. Ning Wu's decade ENTERED at ch 297 as a line with no mark on it. He does not appear and is not met. Register still *reconciled*, read and never corrected** |
| Yuan | **Readable from outside the bound without her, and therefore findable.** Unentered state and the date of the morning he died, gone, named on the page before the line went in. **No figure of what is left of him anywhere in ch 239–298** |
| Inside | **One report, once, ch 295.** Floor, nine men, the same nine; wash house; tower light; counting room; the date off the page |
| Still open | The Hunter's request pending and the line's own people still not knowing she exists · everything above the Standing Office of the Second Account · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · what became of the eleven · why the register reads *reconciled* · the year still carrying · **and the country past the last working point, which she stood half a mile short of and did not go past** |

## Volume 5 close — REVIEW REPAIR

The close review audited the close and returned four findings. **Three were count errors in the state layer and
one was an editorial judgement that had been deferred twice. All four are now resolved.** The chapters were
re-opened once, at five sentences. **No chapter was replaced, no beat moved, no title changed, and
`outline/series.md`, `outline/ending.md` and `outline/volume-05.md` are untouched.**

### 1 — The reopened-chapter list was short and mis-batched. REPAIRED.

`state/book-metadata.md` recorded *eleven chapters across three batches*, and named ch 248, 251, 256, 271, 275,
278, 283, 286, 290, 291, 295. **Against the commit it is wrong twice over.** The list falls across **four**
batches, not three — 248 and 251 in 0001, 256 in 0002, 271/275/278/283 in 0003, 286/290/291/295 in 0004. And it
**omits ch 292, ch 297 and ch 298**, each of which carries a real repair in that commit: 292 *five volumes* →
*four years*, 297 *for the rest of the book* → *for as long as either of them was standing*, and 298's two
re-voicings of the surface answer cut. **The close reopened fourteen chapters with a substantive repair, and the
close review re-opened five more (261, 262, 264, 265, 266). The current list is nineteen chapters across four
batches.** Thirty-one chapter files have been touched in all, counting the twelve that carry only the
typographic sweep.

### 2 — "Twelve repairs" had no traceable source. REPAIRED.

*Twelve repairs* was carried in `current.md`, `continuity.md`, `open-threads.md`, `chapter-summaries.md` and
the close summary. **The repairs table has ten rows.** The count was corrected to a derived vocabulary —
**ten categories, twenty individual faults, nineteen chapters, thirty-one files, four batches** — and the
derivation table is now written into both `current.md` and `continuity.md` so the figures can be re-derived
rather than trusted. **A fourth stale figure was found while doing this and is also corrected:** the close
refreshed the volume word total to 101,204 but left all four per-batch totals at their pre-close values, so they
summed to 101,160 against a volume figure of 101,204. Per-batch and per-file counts are now re-derived from the
chapters in every state file, and the batch-review blocks that quote the old figures carry a note saying they
are snapshots.

### 3 — The audit's own precision claim was off by one. REPAIRED.

Finding 1 stated the verbatim tag *stands four times verbatim (ch 261, 262, 264, 267)* and corrected an earlier
count on the strength of it. **It is five — ch 253 is byte-identical**, and was named in the same finding as
part of the run. The close's companion claim that the duplication repairs were *five paragraphs across three
chapters* is also wrong; it is **four** (248 ×1, 275 ×2, 295 ×1). Both corrected in place, with the correction
recorded rather than the error deleted.

### 4 — The judgement deferred twice. RULED ON AND REPAIRED.

The close cut seven instances of authorial scaffolding — *in this book*, *for the rest of the book*, *in five
volumes*, *four volumes* — on the ground that they are talk about the book's own construction. In the same
document it recorded eleven instances of *it was present tense and it bought nothing* as an authorised motif.
The review held that both positions could not stand and asked for an explicit ruling.

**RULING: the tag is not scaffolding.** The scaffolding instances are about the novel — its length, its
volumes, its reader. The tag is about **the world**: a diegetic register rule (his speech is in the present
tense, the standing consequence of his frame) and a dramatic fact about the scene (the line bought nothing).
It is a ledger line, and it passes the test the scaffolding instances fail. **The device was kept.**

**But the run was a fault, and it is repaired.** Eleven near-identical sentences across eleven of fifteen
consecutive chapters, five of them byte-identical and four more byte-identical in a second variant, is inside
`AGENTS.md`'s condition on a construction used more than twice in a row — and this same close applied that
standard to *about four seconds*, thinning four uses to two. **Five tags recast (261, 262, 264, 265, 266) so
that no string in the run now stands more than twice.** 253, 255, 258, 259, 263 and 267 keep their tag; 253 and
267 bookend the run. **No beat, no line of his dialogue and no pinned item moved; every tag still sits
immediately before the same utterance.** **+62 words of body prose, all in Batch 0002.**

### Re-verified after the review repair

`grep` for a line beginning with `[` still returns **exactly three in sixty chapters**, at 258, 275 and 292.
Weekday names, calendar month names, `tribulation`, `Anchor`, `Sisterhood`, `Scribe`, `kiln-born`, `Lung`,
`appointment`, `MEMBER ABSENT`, `sponsor` and `Shen Xiu` all return **zero**. Zero straight apostrophes, zero
bold, zero blockquotes, code fences, tables or horizontal rules. **The longest paragraph in the volume is still
exactly 99 words.** The three named duplicate paragraphs (248/242, 275/258, 295/287) remain gone, and a
paragraph-level duplicate scan returns **only the two intentional bookend pairs of the Yuan tag, at 253/267 and
259/263.**

# Volume 6 — The Real Realm (ch 299–360), Batch 0001 (ch 299–313)

## Established facts, Volume 6 Batch 0001 (ch 299–313) — READING AND BEING READ

**Fifteen chapters, 24,531 words of body prose, average ~1,635, day 621 to day 645. ONE bracketed layer line, at
ch 310. Chapter titles and section headings excluded. Drafted against `outline/volume-06.md` and against the
fifteen cards at `outline/batches/volume-06-batch-0001.md`. Every card beat is on the page.** *(24,514 before
the review repair; the seventeen words are the repair itself and no beat, day, title or stage moved.)*

### The batch in one sentence

She sent her column up in a box, found out that being entered as good had meant it would be read by a floor
that never needed a bound, worked a season that is not a season because there is no book to put it in, and
answered the question the reading produces — in daylight, out loud, one stair above a counter she had never
been above.

### The new machinery, established once at ch 300 and used after that

- **A book that closes clean on the thirtieth is a finished book. It goes in a tray. The tray goes up to the
  mill on the first cart. Every reach on that ground sends one a month.** Up the road past the store there is a
  stair in the counting house and everything that goes up it is read against everything else that went up it.
  **Nothing comes back down.** Five sentences from a man with a rule and a paste-pot, between two trays, at the
  second hour of the eleventh, after one question. **No chapter after ch 300 teaches it and no mouth in the
  batch says a box could stay.** The break is ch 344, Batch 0003's, and nothing here foreshadows it.
- **The mill** is a working building with a wheel that has not turned since before she came. The wheel is not
  what the place is for. It has a shed, a loading door, a bench with a folded blanket, a lean-to, a paste-pot,
  and a yard with a stack of trays under boards and sacking. **The mill is where boxes pass through; it is not
  the archive.**
- **The sorter** — a man past forty with the shoulders of a man who has carried trays for thirty years. Not a
  character, not a confidant, **asked one question and given five sentences and nothing since.** He is right,
  stays right, never explains why the rule is the rule, and lets her sleep on his floor with the sentence *you
  can have the floor till the cart wants turning back. Not after.*
- **The counter** — the man behind it is the same clerk as ch 297 and ch 294, the same counter, stove, spike
  and six men. He appears at **301, 305, 306 and 308 only.** He is not an antagonist and does not know more
  than a counter knows.
- **The copying floor** — one flight above the counter, entered once in ch 299–313. Tables, four people, a
  stack of sheets about as high as a man's chest with a string round it, a paste-pot on each table. **The
  stair above it goes up out of the light, is looked at for nine seconds, and is never entered, described or
  named.**
- **A strip of cloth is the only channel between floors.** It is the same cloth the keeper's board is made of,
  cut to the same shape, with three columns ruled on it and a line across near the bottom. It comes down off
  the round of carts and it carries **no name, no heading she can read, no seal and no signature.** The answer
  does not go back down into the counter's book. **It goes up, or it does not go.**
- **Pei** — a farmer of about fifty with a boy of nine, half a mile east of the mill. Right throughout. Says
  the rate out loud once and pays what he is told because he has been told.

### The clock

Ch 298 closed on **day 620, the seventh of a month.** **Batch 0001 ran day 621 to day 645** — the eighth of a
month to the second of the next. Months are thirty days, month-day `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`; the month this
batch opens in began on day 614 and the next begins on **day 644**. Every day 621–645 is covered once.

| Ch | Day / days | Month-day | Title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 299 | 621 | the 8th | The First Cart |
| 300 | 622–624 | the 9th–11th | The Closed Season |
| 301 | 625–626 | the 12th–13th | What The Counter Said |
| 302 | 627–628 | the 14th–15th | Past The Last Farm |
| 303 | 629 | the 16th | Seven Coppers At The Gate |
| 304 | 630 | the 17th | **MIDPOINT** — What The Box Was For |
| 305 | 631–632 | the 18th–19th | The Strip Off The Cart |
| 306 | 633–634 | the 20th–21st | Is There More |
| 307 | 635 | the 22nd | The Cut She Went Down |
| 308 | 636 | the 23rd | **IRREVERSIBLE** — One Stair Up |
| 309 | 637 | the 24th | **CLIMAX** — Priced From Above |
| 310 | 638 | the 25th | **GOLDEN CORE 4** — Half A Reach |
| 311 | 639–641 | the 26th–28th | The Brick Money |
| 312 | 642–643 | the 29th–30th | The Bundle Going Up |
| 313 | 644–645 | the 1st–2nd | The Ground Past The Mill |

- **The far-side pay-cycle is dead and was not revived.** Pays fell on the seventh, the seventeenth and the
  twenty-seventh at the trestle. **There is no trestle, no plank, no book and no pay-cycle anywhere in ch
  299–313.** **Day 630, the seventeenth, came and nothing happened on it — one sentence at ch 304, and it is
  not a scene.** Ch 298 already spent a pay-day producing nothing and this batch did not spend it twice.
- **The season is not named and no date is put on any road or turn.** What is used is *what is lit and what is
  not*, a thirtieth, a pay-cycle that is gone, and the fact that the ground past the mill is flat and farmed.
  **Rain is referred to once, at ch 307, as having fallen two days back, which is not a season.**
- **No named weekday, no named calendar month, no named week.**

### The money — five figures, four chapters, and the rate twice

| | Day | What she opens | Rate a reach | Coppers in her hand |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **ch 303** | the 16th | a reach and a little over | **seven** | **eight** |
| **ch 309** | the 24th | a reach and a little over | **five** | **six** |
| **ch 310** | the 25th | half a reach | **five** | **two and a half** |
| **ch 311** | the 26th–28th | half a reach a day | **five** | **two and a half a day, seven and a half across three days** |
| **ch 311** | — | — | — | **nine**, for a full charge |

- **These five are the whole of it.** The days the table does not name — the 14th, 15th, 18th, 19th, 20th,
  21st, 22nd, 23rd, 26th's own second half — are worked and paid for and **get no figure on the page.** The
  fourteenth, fifteenth, twentieth and twenty-first were worked and paid at the gate with the count done by
  Pei out loud and **never written down anywhere in the batch.**
- **The rate is said out loud twice in the batch and no more:** seven at ch 302, five at ch 309. Five a reach
  is also referred to at 310, 311 and 313 as the standing rate, which is use and not a third quotation.
- **The historic figures are named as history and never as a current rate:** five coppers a day at the yard
  (302, 306, 308, 310) and twenty-nine coppers on a promise (303, 306).
- **Nothing else has a price in this batch.** Bread, the shop at a gate two hundred yards back toward the mill,
  and the material shed past the mill are all real and none of them is given a figure. **The eleventh figure is
  not available and a drafter who needs one has run out of table.**
- **She is not hired anywhere in this batch.** No booking, no term, no stub, no name on anything, paid in her
  own hand at a gate at the end of the day for what was done, and **that is not employment and she does not
  want it to be** (ch 302).

### Body at the last page (ch 313)

**GOLDEN CORE 4**, taken day 638, the twenty-fifth, at her own order, in the open, in a drain bank on Pei's
field at the fourth hour, past the last working point, **where nothing is announced.**

- **Right index finger gone, four fingers' worth of grip.** Not improved anywhere in the batch and nothing
  offers it back.
- **Right arm open half the time, permanent, since ch 288.** Used at 302, 307, 309, 310, 311 and named at 310
  as the reason she cannot carry a full charge. **Not made worse and not made better by ch 307.**
- **Nine holds out of eleven in the left hand.** No hold spent anywhere in the batch.
- **Four counts of breath everywhere.** Unchanged through ch 307 and ch 310.
- **Cold the way a stone is cold.** Unchanged.
- **Not hungry since the first Foundation** — and at ch 311 she names a different thing and says in her own head
  that she is not going to call it hunger.
- **The turn at the end of a bar stroke: gone.** The middle-bank method: in use.
- **Aperture cracked through and permanent.**
- **Eighteen years old.**
- **The relicensed licence still says Foundation the first stage on the front of it and she has never touched
  it.** `licence` and `license` both return **zero** across ch 299–313. **No clearance, seal or countersignature
  is invented for anything and no chapter says one was or was not required.**
- **The Clay Plate is worn against her ribs and is not described.** `plate` returns **zero** across the batch.

### The stage, at ch 310

**Golden Core 4, day 638, the twenty-fifth — thirty-eight days after Golden Core 3 at day 600.** Taken at her
own order, in the open, in the middle of a reach, bar in her left hand, **nobody within two hundred yards.**

Bracket, on its own line, matching the pinned text character for character and the only square-bracketed line
in the batch:

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage four, is held.]

- **The layer said it once and stopped.** It did not explain and it did not advise and it did not begin.
- **Nothing was announced.** There is nobody inside to be told and no county book on that ground to take
  anything down; the sound went into the ground under Pei's field and about a quarter of a mile into the air,
  and nothing came back. **What the offices above took was the reading, not the sound.**
- **The cost, permanent and named once, in three parts:** the flat of a second finger **from longer to
  longest**, so that **every line she writes anywhere now costs her the hour it is written in twice over**;
  **the yard work she can still price one-handed narrows to half a reach**; and nothing else. She works out the
  third part on the page in nine words about a sheet of paper and a room she cannot get to.
- **There was no part of her that was slower**, and the chapter says in plain words that that is the part that
  is frightening.
- **No tribulation. No suppression and no absence arithmetic.** `tribulation` returns zero.

### The irreversible decision of this batch — ch 308, *One Stair Up*

**At about the second hour of the twenty-third she said *there is more*, out loud, in daylight, on a copying
floor one stair above the counter, to a clerk with a brush, with four other people in the room copying.**

- **She did not give her name. The room found it** — read off a column and matched to the woman standing
  there. That is the batch's whole idea in one gesture.
- **She confirmed the gap** — the twenty-second, and the twenty-third to the fourth hour — in the ordinary
  voice, because the gap is true and hers and she has never once put a true day in as a false one. **She said
  *I was under a bank* and he wrote it down.**
- **The price, in one plain sentence about one person:** *Her column was the only thing in the world she could
  prove about herself, and from the fourth hour of the twenty-third it is a thing somebody else can read.*
- **Nobody persuaded her.** The refusal not to answer was available to her at 306 and she took two days over it
  and went down a drain both days instead, and then a bank came in on her.
- **She cannot be un-answered. The answer went up the stair with the answer.**
- **This is NOT the second vouching.** It is **the same office on the same floor** that entered her as good at
  ch 292, and a running column is not a second office vouching. **A drafter may use *written down* and *in the
  book*; the word *vouched* is not used anywhere in ch 299–313, and `vouch` returns zero across the batch.**
- **The words *sending* and *sent for* are used nowhere in ch 299–313.** `grep` returns zero. Ch 358 and
  Batch 0003 own them.
- **The finding that being read means being sent for is not softened anywhere in the batch.**

### The physical risk — ch 307, *The Cut She Went Down*, day 635, the twenty-second

A silted stone drain on the low side of Pei's field, to the chest, cold, a man has to go in on his back, loose
standing clay at the shoulder from rain two days back. **The bank came in on her while she was on her back.**
Four minutes under it. **There was nobody within a mile who would have come and not one of them would have
come and there was no version of that afternoon in which a man stood in that cut.**

**She got out with the bar.** Nothing broke in her that she has not had back once; the four fingers, the arm,
the nine holds and the count of breath were all exactly what they had been at the fourth hour. **Nobody died.
Nobody was hurt except her. Nobody was saved.** She told Pei the low side was under and would want a spade and
two men on a rope, **and she did not tell him about the shoulder, and there was nothing in that for her.**

**The cost is in days and not on her body: the twenty-second, and the morning of the twenty-third.**
**This is the batch's one use of the eleven-counts measure and it is not a declaration** — *in about as long as
it takes to count eleven*. Volume 5 declared it once at ch 284; the batch does not repeat that.

### The deadline — the ring

**Open and cold since the seventh of the month; taken cold and unlit since the twenty-first.** It was carried
out of the field behind her own boards at the yard on the **thirteenth**, on her back under the bar, and it
stands at the back of the mill shed in a corner.

- **Relit at the sixteenth** (ch 303) out of what was left in her hand after bread and material. Banked low.
  Broken down to a carrying size on the flat stone by the door at the dark, **eleven minutes on a poor piece
  with the flat of a second finger**, and the flat has been longer than eleven minutes since ch 275.
- **Banked lower every night from the nineteenth.** A banked fire is not a fire. The twentieth and the
  twenty-first are the two nights of ch 306.
- **A third of a charge bought on the twenty-sixth and carried back in two loads**, and the ring lit low again
  on the twenty-eighth. It has not been hot since the twenty-first and will not be again for four days at two
  and a half a day.
- **A full charge is nine coppers and does not come in a size one arm can carry.** Half a reach a day at five
  is two and a half a day, so: **four days of clearing for one charge, and then three trips for the same one.**
  That is the whole of ch 311 and it is the pull into Batch 0002.
- **The ground under the ring gives nothing back.** Carried in the body and in narration at ch 306 and never
  said out loud by her, and the only sentence she says about it on the boards is *It is not holding*, which is
  about the clay.

### The angle of what was read — ch 308

**The word is *good*. It is a certificate. A certificate says the person does not need the whole of a day's
rate.** Put in the book at the second hour of the seventeenth of a month, in the open, at the last working
point, in front of eleven men, by her own mouth, when she was seventeen. **She wanted to be checked by
somebody, once, and the only instrument on that ground that could check a woman was a stage eleven men could
see her take. It checked her. It is now costing her two coppers a reach, permanently, and the rate does not
come back up.**

**The batch's anger is in the arithmetic and does not come out of her mouth.** It is the first real anger since
the crossing and it is aimed at an institution and cannot be spent on anybody: **everything that has ever been
done to her in this country was done to her by somebody who was right, and being right is the part that costs
her.**

### Wei Ran

**One card, ch 302, on the fifteenth.** She does not stop the cart; she slows it. She gives a rate (*seven a
yard on that gate, it is what the other gate pays and it is not worth the walk*), a road (*the ground turns to
soup about two hundred yards past that hedge and it will hold a wheel about half a season, every year, not one
year*; *a cut in it about a mile on*) and an opinion, in about as many sentences as it takes to say them.
**She offers no team, no cart, no favour, no word to anybody and no face to anybody.** She is twenty and Ning
Yan is eighteen and **the book does not romanticise that and nothing in this batch is a romance.**
**She appears in five cards of Volume 5 and this is the sixth and the last before Batch 0004.**

### Feng, by report, once

**Ch 312, in a carter's mouth, to a woman at a gate, and never asked for.** *There is a stair about nine days
west of here. Men go up it carrying bundles and they come down with nothing in their hands, and they have been
doing that since before I was driving.* He has never met Feng Lushi, does not know his name, and never thinks
about it again. **No tower, no light and no floor of nine men — those were ch 244 and ch 295 and are not
repeated. The tenth part is not given. Nothing here proves the crews wrong, and nothing here says that what she
is doing past the mill is the same as what is done nine days west.** **No second Lung and no second appointment;
both unspent and both still unspent.**

### Yuan in this batch

**Five sentences in fifteen chapters, one each at ch 302, 304, 306, 311 and 313. Every one in the present
tense. None buys anything, advises anything, explains anything or advances anything.**

- **No figure of what is left of him is on any page.** No count, no fraction, no percentage, no decimal, no
  elapsed span that measures him. The ordinary numbers of the world — coppers, reaches, holds, hours — are in
  full use throughout and none of them is about him.
- **His ch 272 losses are not given back by one word:** the unentered state and the date of the morning he died
  are still gone, and nothing in the batch softens the finding that being read means being sent for.
- **The Ledger is never ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about.** `grep` for *Ledger* returns nothing
  in ch 299–313 outside this record.
- **Merit: zero.** Nothing killed, nothing listed cleared, the channel not restored, not spent and not
  measured. **The Hand is not spent and not refused in this batch** — it is spent at ch 325 and refused at
  ch 355, both Batch 0002's and Batch 0004's.
- **The sustained scene in which he is on the page in his own right is ch 338, *The Room With Doors*, in Batch
  0003. Nothing in these fifteen chapters moves, duplicates, pre-spends or describes it.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 313

**The register still reads *reconciled* — `grep` for *reconciled* returns zero across the batch, because no
card in this batch reads the register and the allowance for it was zero.** MEMBER ABSENT stands three times and
was not raised · the ch 150 refusal unsoftened · the ch 128 silence unbroken and nothing about the sponsors'
second book lodged, copied or spoken · **the year of non-forgiveness still carrying and not mentioned once in
these fifteen chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven and unthanked · *kiln-born* zero on the page and still in two
heads · what became of the eleven not raised · **no office above the Standing Office of the Second Account
named, and no office named at all** · the surface answer not re-voiced, hinted at or paraphrased · the seventh
condition never listed, counted or referred to · **the four months erased in Chapter 3 carried in the body and
never named** — `grep` for *erased*, *base went out*, *four weeks* and *could not account* returns **zero** ·
the claim not reopened and the word *claim* not used of a figure · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the
page · Luo Ping never met · Ning Wu does not appear and the list is not asked after · **Bai Zhu is off the
page in this batch entirely** — his request is still pending and the line's own people still do not know she
exists · no harem and nothing a romance · she never says her mother's name · no new enemy and no named villain.

### Live at the end of this batch, going into Batch 0002

- **She is a running column.** A person who is good, who works, in a book one flight above a counter, in a hand
  she has never seen, copied upward. **The twenty-second and the morning of the twenty-third are missing out of
  it and are recorded as missing**, in the words *I was under a bank*.
- **The rate for a reach is five coppers and she can open half of one.** Two and a half a day.
- **A charge of the ring's material is nine, at a size one arm cannot carry in one trip.** The ring is lit low
  and not full. **Four days of clearing for one charge and then three trips for the same one.**
- **The only thing on that ground that can pay for brick in a size one arm can carry is not coppers** — and she
  named the three candidates standing in a yard on the twenty-sixth and took none of them.
- **Past the mill the ground is flat, farmed and lit and there is nothing on it, and she is standing on it**,
  about a mile and a half east of a wheel that has not turned since before she came, sleeping on the ground.
- **Eighteen counterfoils in four marks and no nineteenth, for ever, because there is no plank.**
- **About weeks at the open and no figure after it.**
- **The three words *is there more* are on a stack against a wall one flight above a counter, answered.**

### Open after the batch, going into Batch 0002

- **What does an office above a copying floor do with a person it has written down as good?** Answered in part,
  in money: it sets a rate. **One office has, the same one, at ch 292 and again at ch 308.** Open: whether
  anything is sent for, and that is Batch 0003's.
- **What is above the copying floor?** Never entered, never described, never named. Looked at for nine seconds.
- **Where does a tray go when it has gone up?** It is not the mill's to keep and it is not the counter's.
- **How does a woman with four fingers feed a furnace that needs nine coppers a charge?** **This is Batch
  0002's whole pressure and nothing in these fifteen chapters answers it.**

## Construction budget — Volume 6 Batch 0001 (ch 299–313), measured by grep, not promised

**Every figure below was run against the fifteen files after they were written, not asserted in advance. The
card file's page said zero by absence of text; this is the measurement of text.**

| Construction | Result across ch 299–313 |
| --- | --- |
| **Authorial scaffolding** — *this chapter, this volume, in this book, for the rest of the book, the reader, in this batch, another batch, N volumes* | **0 as scaffolding.** **One instance of the string was found and cut at review:** *the second thing she found out is the whole chapter*, at ch 311, which is scaffolding and not a thing a person in a shed thinks. The one surviving hit is **ch 305, *the answer does not go back down into this book*** — that is the physical ledger in the counter's room, not this manuscript, and it is left standing. Grepping the literal string *in this book* therefore returns one hit that is correct prose |
| **Bracketed layer lines** | **1, at ch 310**, matching the pinned text character for character. Zero in the other fourteen chapters |
| ***About four seconds*** (retired out of Volume 4, two uses left standing in Volume 5) | **0.** Two instances were written into ch 304 and cut before the batch was saved |

because a budget that only ever reports clean is not a budget:** *about four seconds* written twice at ch 304,
and the retired thanks fact written at ch 300 as well as at its one licensed home at ch 309. Both were cut and
