no chapter in `chapters/volume-01/` … `chapters/volume-05/` was opened.**

### Volume 6 Batch 0001 (ch 299–313) — REVIEW REPAIR

**Thirteen prose repairs across seven chapters — ch 304, 306, 307, 308, 310, 311 and 313 — and one arithmetic
fault that had propagated into this ledger and into `state/chapter-summaries.md`. No beat, day, title, turn,
midpoint, climax, irreversible decision, bracket or stage moved, and nothing in `chapters/volume-01/` …
`chapters/volume-05/` was opened. The batch was not restarted and no chapter was rewritten.**

**The fault that mattered — ch 311's arithmetic contradicted ch 310 and its own card.** The chapter opened
*Half a reach was what she could open in a day* and then said *The twenty-seventh was a reach and a half* and
*The twenty-eighth was a reach and a half*, at five coppers a reach, which is fifteen coppers across two days
against the **seven and a half** the same chapter states at the bottom and against **two and a half a day** in
the money table above. Golden Core 4 at day 638 had already halved her to a half reach a day, and the twenty-
seventh and twenty-eighth are two days after it. **Three lines now read half a reach**, and the four-line
arithmetic is **half a reach a day at five = two and a half, three days = seven and a half, a charge = nine,
so four days of clearing and then three trips** — which reconciles with ch 310, with the card at
`outline/batches/volume-06-batch-0001.md`, and with ch 313. **`state/chapter-summaries.md` carried the same
error in the ch 311 entry and was corrected with it.** Nothing else in the batch touched the money: the five
figures, the four chapters and the rate are exactly as they were.

**Authorial scaffolding, one instance, cut.** ch 311 opened the size-of-a-charge section with *The second thing
she found out is the whole chapter.* It now reads *The second thing she found out took her about four minutes
and she had it before she was off the step.* **The row above now stands at zero and says so honestly.**

**Six verbatim repeats broken, at 304, 306, 307, 308, 310 and 313.** The gate payment was described in almost
the same words three times, at 303, 309 and 310; **310** now reads *The same fold in the inside of the door
frame, the count done by him out loud, into her own hand.* The counter hand — *flat on the counter on the side
she could reach* — stood at 305, 306 and 308; **306** and **308** were recast and 305 keeps it. The refusal of
the channel, *She did not answer him and she did not tell him to stop*, stood whole at 304 and whole again at
313; **313** now reads *She did not answer him out loud and she did not tell him to stop, and she sat with her
back against the boards and let the cold come up through her*, so the batch does not close on a line it has
already used. The strongest-body sentence was one sentence three times; **307** now opens *For four years*, and
the three instances no longer share a frame. *worked until the light was off the field* stood at 302 and 304;
**304** now reads *did not come out of it until*. *About as long as it takes to look at a door* at 308 echoed
312; **308** now reads *the length of one look at a door*. **About four minutes** ran twice inside 310; the
second now reads *the length of a breath she could not feel the end of*.

**Deliberate, and left standing.** The *In the shed at the mill, on the boards* channel header is a ritual and
runs at 302, 304, 306 and 311. *That was the whole of the hiring and it took about as long as the tray took to
lift* answers *…as long as the gate took to open* at 302, and the pair marks the two thresholds. At 309 she
does the sum and at 310 she does not, and the shared sentence is the point of the pair. The short line at 300,
*She had not been in use since the seventh of a month*, distils the inventory above it and the next paragraph
names it as the sentence the whole chapter was sitting on. **None of these was touched.**

**One hedge added and no distance moved.** ch 310 puts the material shed *about a quarter of a mile* east of
the gate and ch 311 said *five hundred yards* flat; the gate-to-shed figure at 311 now reads *about five
hundred yards*, so both statements are hedged and neither asserts a number the other denies. **No map
changed.**

**Re-measured after the repair:** 0 exact duplicate paragraphs; 6 near-duplicate pairs, all motifs; 0 bold;
4 italic spans, all documentary, unchanged; 1 bracketed line, at ch 310, matching the pinned text; 0 blockquotes,
code fences, tables or horizontal rules; every speech closed, 0 straight quotes; longest paragraph still 100
words at ch 309; **24,531 words, average ~1,635, shortest ch 313 at 1,421, longest ch 299 at 1,927.**

## Established facts, Volume 6 Batch 0002 (ch 314–329) — WORK WITHOUT HIRE

**Sixteen chapters, 23,931 words of body prose, average ~1,496, day 646 to day 699. ONE bracketed layer line, at
ch 321. Chapter titles and section headings excluded. Drafted against `outline/volume-06.md` and against
`workspace/volume-06-batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, which is this batch's card. Every pinned item in it is on the page.
**The batch has been through review and repaired; the full record is at the end of this section under *Volume 6
Batch 0002 (ch 314–329) — REVIEW REPAIR*, and the repair did not move a beat, a day, a stage, a bracket or a
planned event.**

### The batch in one sentence

She went and found work that is not wages, cleared two listed jobs that settled merit and each cost her the
furnace, took a stage that took the feeling out of her hands, watched a cart go out of a yard on a day it was
not going to go, and then stood at a gate in daylight and said her own name and asked to be put in a second
office's materials book.

### The new machinery — the stone-and-brick works, three miles east of the mill

Established once, in ch 314, from the board on the wall, and used after that. **No chapter after ch 314 teaches
it and no mouth in the batch explains why the rule is the rule beyond what the board itself says.**

- **The yard.** A mortared wall about as high as her chest, running about four hundred yards each way from a
  gate made of two piers and a beam. Inside: a yard, a long low building with an open end, **eight or nine men**
  working at the open end in the ordinary way, a bench, a lean-to, boxes against the long wall in two rows, and a
  **cart with its shafts down, a board across the shafts and a stone under each wheel.**
- **The board**, made of the same stuff as the keeper's board at the post two miles west. It reads, verbatim:

  LISTED WORK. PAID IN WHAT THE WORK WAS MADE OF. NO HIRE. NO SEASON. NO NAME ON THE LINE. TAKE IT DOWN AND IT IS YOURS TO FINISH. PUT IT BACK AND IT WAITS FOR THE NEXT ONE.

  and under that, one line per job: a job, a number of days, and a number of boxes.
- **A listing** is work set by an office and put on the board. **When a listed thing is finished something settles
  out of the ground it was done on** — the rule is established, not re-explained, and she knew it from ch 290.
  **A gate does not settle anything.** That is the batch's live rule and it is what ch 318 is built on.
- **The men.** One behind the stack past forty, who sorts brick and clay and does not come out for a woman, and
  who says *I am not going to tell a woman she cannot do a thing she has not tried*. One who keeps the board —
  thinner, ink on the side of his right hand, eleven years on that board — who tells her the truth twice, is
  right both times, and gives her the whole price of a thing at one sitting because she asked. **Neither is a
  character, neither becomes a confidant, neither explains why the rule is the rule, and neither is made wrong.**
- **The yard's rule:** material leaves the yard against a name in a book, so that if a load does not arrive the
  yard knows whose it was. **There is no account on that ground with her name on it** until ch 329.

### The second office — entered at ch 329, never named, in no mouth, in no chapter

- It **sets the lines the board carries.** The listing man does not write the lines; he puts up what comes down.
- It keeps **a materials book of what the ground owes and what has been paid on it.** Everything that goes out of
  that yard against a name goes into it and it does not stop at that yard.
- **It is copied.** *Material is copied further than days are, because days stay on the ground they happened on
  and material goes to every yard between here and wherever it stops.*
- It has **a list of its own** which **is not put up on a wall where a woman can take it off. It is offered.**
  It comes down about four times a year.
- **It is not the counter and not the floor above the counter and not the man with the brush.** Nothing above the
  copying floor is entered, described or named in this batch either.
- The entry was made at about the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth of a month on **a strip of the same cloth as
  the board, about as wide as a hand, with a line ruled down it and a line across near the bottom, cut at the
  bottom edge with a knife, which means there had been more of it at one time.** It was turned face down on the
  bench and it was not read out and it was not held up. **It goes up.**

### The clock

Ch 313 closed on **day 645, the second of a month.** **Batch 0002 ran day 646 to day 699** — the third of a
month to the twenty-sixth of the next. Months are thirty days, month-day `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`; the month
this batch opens in began on day 644 and the next began on **day 674, which is the pinned Golden Core 5 date**.
Every day 646–700 is covered once, none unassigned, and **day 699 is shared by design** — ch 328 closes on her
walking east and coming up to the gate and ch 329 opens in the same gateway with the words already said.

| Ch | Day / days | Month-day | Title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 314 | 646 | the 3rd | Four Nails |
| 315 | 647–650 | the 4th–7th | The Lane Crossing |
| 316 | 651–654 | the 8th–11th | It Came Twice |
| 317 | 655–657 | the 12th–14th | The Ring Went Out |
| 318 | 658–661 | the 15th–18th | Six Miles To The Fire |
| 319 | 662–665 | the 19th–22nd | The Block In The Ditch |
| 320 | 666–672 | the 23rd–29th | A Thing That Is Not Hers |
| 321 | 673–674 | the 30th, the 1st | **GOLDEN CORE 5** — Warm Hands |
| 322 | 675–678 | the 2nd–5th | What Her Hand Could Not Say |
| 323 | 679–681 | the 6th–8th | The Thing That Answers |
| 324 | 682–685 | the 9th–12th | The Man Who Keeps The List |
| 325 | 686–688 | the 13th–15th | **THE HAND SPENT** — The Cart Turned Round |
| 326 | 689–691 | the 16th–18th | A Hand In The Book |
| 327 | 692–695 | the 19th–22nd | The Fire She Did Not Need |
| 328 | 696–699 | the 23rd–26th | Three Days On It |
| 329 | 699–700 | the 26th–27th | **IRREVERSIBLE** — She Asked At The Gate |

- **Day 700, the twenty-seventh, is ch 329's closing walk west.** She does not date it on the page and the only
  other twenty-seventh in the batch is retrospective at ch 320.
- **The lane crossing was cleared in three days, the fourth to the sixth.** The board said four. The retaining
  wall took the full four. **Seven days of her own wall in all, which is the figure every later chapter gives as
  *a week of her wall*.**
- **No pay-cycle.** There is no trestle, no plank, no book and no pay-cycle anywhere in ch 314–329 and nothing
  falls on a date. The seventeenth was spent at ch 304 in Batch 0001 and was not spent twice.
- **No named weekday, no named calendar month, no named week.** *Three weeks*, *about eleven days*, *four days*,
  *about a month* are ordinary relative stretches and are in ordinary use.

### Geography, as established in ch 314 and used after

Mill at 0. **Pei’s gate half a mile east of it.** Her camp after ch 313, about a mile and a half east, past the
last hedge. **The stone works three miles east of the mill**, on a made road with a hedge on the north and a line
of elms about half a mile on it. **The counter with the stove two miles and four hundred yards west of the mill.**
**The material shed past the mill** — brick and clay, three coppers for a third of a sack, nine for a full charge
— **is a different building from the works** and the two are never confused. **The ring is at the mill shed**, in
the corner behind a third of a sack, on a flat stone by the door. **Six miles to the fire and back.**

### The money — this batch's own table

| | Day | What | Figure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **the gate, every day** | — | half a reach at five coppers a reach | **two and a half** |
| **the material shed** | — | a third of a sack, which is a third of a charge and is about **a day's** ring | **three** |
| **the material shed** | — | a full charge, which is **three days** of the ring and does not come in a size one arm can carry | **nine** (carried from ch 311) |
| **a listing** | — | what the work was made of, into a box, at the works; **one box for the lane crossing, two for the retaining wall** | **one box = four days of the ring = twelve coppers; two boxes = eight days = twenty-four coppers** |
| **the retaining wall** | the 22nd | what she was owed out of two boxes, after a block worth about a fifth of a box went into a ditch | **twenty-three, not twenty-five** |
| **the tray of twenty-three** | the 30th | how long it lasts a ring that is burning and answering nothing | **eight days** |
| **carrying one tray** | — | three miles each way, about three pieces a load, with a gate to work every morning | **eight loads, a little better than two days a load, sixteen days** |
| **a ring, kept going** | — | three coppers every fourth day, six miles, and eleven minutes a lump | **one third of a charge** |

- **A box is four days of the ring.** That is the batch's working unit and it reconciles: **a box is four thirds
  of a sack; a third of a sack is three coppers and about a day's ring; so a box is twelve coppers and four days
  of the ring, two boxes are twenty-four coppers and eight days, and a charge at nine coppers is three days.** The
  tray she was handed holds twenty-three pieces — two boxes by the yard's figure of twenty-five, less the block —
  and it lasts **eight** days.
- **A listing settles twice and costs what it pays.** The settlement takes the fire that is in the ring that day,
  which is between nothing and four days of it. **The rest of the box is not a trade with her; it is the money,
  and it is the part of the payment that does not come out of her foundation.**
- **No figure of what is left of him is on any page.** `tribulation` returns zero.

### Body at the last page (ch 329)

**GOLDEN CORE 5**, taken day 674, the first of a month, at about the fourth hour, in the mill yard, in the open,
with the ring out on the flat stone and a stack of trays under boards and sacking against the wall behind her,
**where nothing is announced.**

- **Right index finger gone, four fingers' worth of grip.** Not improved anywhere in the batch.
- **Right arm open half the time, permanent, since ch 288.** Used at 314, 315, 318, 319, 322, 325.
- **Nine holds out of eleven in the left hand.** No hold spent anywhere in the batch.
- **Four counts of breath everywhere.** Unchanged through 319 and 322.
- **THE COST OF GOLDEN CORE 5, named once, permanent:** the cold leaves her hands first. She stops being cold the
  way a stone is cold and starts being warm the way a furnace is warm, **and she can no longer tell by feel
  whether a night will hurt her.** Shown on the page in ch 322: a palm on frost that tells her nothing, a block
  levered up on no information at all, a knee and a hip on flat stone and a bar catching it for about a quarter
  of a second longer than she needed. **Nothing gives it back and nothing offers to.**
- **Not hungry.** **Cold** — superseded at ch 321 by the warm hands.
- **The turn at the end of a bar stroke: gone.** The middle-bank method: in use.
- **Aperture cracked through and permanent.** The settlements come up through the base and go out through it,
  and **the ring is what is under her base** — that is the batch's finding, and it is what makes a settlement
  cost her the fire.
- **Eighteen years old.**
- **The relicensed licence still says Foundation the first stage on the front of it and she has never touched
  it.** `licence` and `license` both return **zero** across ch 314–329. No clearance, seal or countersignature is
  invented for anything and no chapter says one was or was not required.
- **The Clay Plate is worn against her ribs and is not described.** `plate` returns **zero** across the batch.

### The stage, at ch 321

**Golden Core 5, day 674, the first of a month — thirty-six days after Golden Core 4 at day 638.** Taken at her
own order, standing, in the open, bar laid down on the stone, nobody in the yard but a man with a rule and a
paste-pot who does not come out.

Bracket, on its own line, matching the pinned text character for character and the only square-bracketed line
in the batch:

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage five, is held.]

- **The layer said it once and stopped.** It did not explain and it did not advise and it did not begin.
- **Nothing was announced.** No one inside to be told and no county book on that ground. The sound went into
  the ground under that yard and about a quarter of a mile into the air, and a dog on a farm a mile off did not
  bark, and nothing came back.
- **One bracket in sixteen chapters.** Volume 6's budget is six in sixty-two; this batch spends one and Batch
  0001 spent one.

### The irreversible decision of this batch — ch 329, *She Asked At The Gate*

**At about the eighth hour on the twenty-sixth of a month, in daylight, at a gate, about nine feet off a bench, she
said her name out loud — *Ning Yan, of Lowwater, I am asking to be put in the materials account of the office
that sets this board* — and asked nothing.**

- **She is not hiring.** He says the first thing and says it looking at her and not at the board: there is no
  day-book, no rate, no day, no stub, nothing torn off anything. **If the office lists a thing and she clears it,
  what the thing was made of comes to her account.**
- **It is entered against material, not against a wage.** She says it as a materials and not as a wage on the
  first go and he tells her that most of them say it as a wage and have to be taken back and made into a
  materials, and that it takes four days longer.
- **The word, said out loud once, in daylight, by the man who is right: it is a vouching.** *It is the same act
  twice and it is not the same thing twice. That one was about your days. This one is about material.*
- **The price, in four short sentences in the ordinary voice, with nothing added to them:** you cannot take it
  back — not by walking a season's road the other way, not by leaving, not by telling a man at a gate that you
  did not mean it — it is in a book and the book is copied and the copies go to a floor she has never been in,
  in a hand she has never seen, and nobody in nine days of road can take her out of it.
- **What it closes:** being a woman who works off a wall with no name on anything anywhere. It cannot be opened
  again.
- **What it puts in motion, on the page, in four things she did standing up in about a minute:** a yard that had
  been giving her work with nothing on any board about who she was is finished at the eighth hour of the
  twenty-sixth; there is now one other way of being paid on nine days of road and it is not wages and it follows
  her; **there are two books on nine days of road with a person in them that is the same person and they do not
  see each other and she sees neither; and leaving the reach no longer takes her out of the books.**
- **Nobody persuaded her.** She was asked to be sure, because after this there is not one hour she gets to keep,
  and she had been sure for three days.
- **The contrast with ch 324 is the chapter's other half and it is a yes and not a fight.** On the ninth of a
  month she asked *whether* a woman not on the books was allowed to ask. On the twenty-sixth she asked *for* a
  thing, in her own name, out loud. Nobody made a speech about it. Nobody argued her out of it and nobody
  argued her into it and nobody in that gateway had any interest in the outcome except that the thing in front
  of them was the thing they were built to do.

### The Hand, spent at ch 325 — the first time in this book

**On the thirteenth of a month, at about the ninth hour, a cart went out of the works on a day the works had not
put it out, with twenty-three pieces of dressed stone on it for a woman on no book anywhere.**

- **Told before spent.** The reach was bought on the **twentieth-fourth of the last month** at the seventh hour
  at the top of a ditch between two farms, out loud, in the ordinary voice, with the price named in four sentences
  before she said the one word. The cart went nine days later. **That is the arrangement and it has never once
  been broken in four years.**
- **It came out of her foundation.** Not out of her hand and not out of her pocket and not out of any book.
- **Whose hand moved:** hers does not. The chapter names it without his having to say it, and she works it out
  alone on the road between the elms in about nine minutes. **A round is a line.** Her column is a live line
  since the fourth hour of the twenty-third of the last month. **A hand in her line put his hand in a line about a
  cart.**
- **What it moved him toward, ch 326.** He has proved to himself in about nine seconds that he can get into any
  line on that ground, and there is no place on it where he cannot, and there is more of that ground than there
  is road behind her. The finding that being read means being findable is **not softened** and **the words
  *sending* and *sent for* are used nowhere in the batch** — `grep` returns zero; ch 338 and Batch 0003 own them.
- **What it cost somebody else, and this is the batch's worst thing happening at the hands of a right man:** the
  listing man wrote a line on his own board with **nothing at the other end of it**, did not ask her one
  question, did not stop halfway through, and told her plainly at ch 328 that it is a hole, the first in eleven
  years, that he will have to answer for it in a month with his mark on the end of it, and that a man with a yard
  will then do the ordinary thing and **take the cart off his own round until somebody puts something on the
  line.** She says *do not lose it on account of me* and she is right.
- **Nothing in Volume 6 kills anybody for it.** It is not spent again in this batch and it is not refused.
  Ch 338 and ch 355 are Batch 0003's and Batch 0004's.

### Merit, settling twice, both on listed work cleared

- **First, ch 316 (days 651–654).** The cold came up through the base **twice** off the lane crossing. **What it
  was for, told before spent:** one line, in a record an office reads, that the work at the lane crossing was
  cleared by the person whose days are in the book on the floor above a counter. Nothing else on it. No figure,
  no rate, no date. **It cannot be un-put.** She said *take it* and said it out loud on the flat in the ordinary
  voice, which is the arrangement and has been for four years. **What it cost on the page:** the ring went out
  cold on the eleventh of a month and had not been hot since the seventh of the last month. **The settlement comes
  up through the base and goes out through the same base, and the ring is what is under the base.** A line in a
  book has no thirtieth in it.
- **Second, ch 320 (days 666–672).** **Twice as big**, off twenty feet of retaining wall. **What it was for,
  told before spent:** the reach to put a thing on that ground that is not hers and not his and does not stop
  because of either of us. **What it cost on the page:** the answering. **The ring stopped answering her and has
  not answered since the twenty-fourth of the last month**, and she found that out by going to it and waiting
  about nine minutes, and she did not go on trying.
- **The batch's rule, which is the thing a reader can use:** a settlement settles twice and once as much as the
  thing listed is worth, so a listing that pays one box costs her at worst four days of fire and gives her four
  days of fire, **and the rest of the box is the money.** A gate settles nothing at all.
- **No figure of what is left of him is on any page**, no count, no fraction, no percentage, no decimal, and no
  elapsed span that measures him. **The channel is not counted back into a rate.** `tribulation` returns zero and
  nothing is killed in either clearing.

### Yuan in this batch

**Five sentences in sixteen chapters. One utterance each at ch 316, 320, 326 and 329. Every one in the present
tense. None buys anything, advises anything, explains anything or advances anything.**

- 316 — *It comes off a listed thing when the listed thing is finished, and I want a line, and I tell you what it
  is for before I spend it.*
- 320 — *It is twice the last one, and it comes off twenty feet of wall and not off a head and an apron.* /
  *I want a cart — not one with my hands, I have had no hands since before you crossed, but the reach to put a
  thing on that ground that is not yours and not mine and does not stop because of either of us.*
- 326 — *There is a line for the yard now, and I am in it.*
- 329 — *You said it out loud.*

- **The *Under her, …* lead-in family: zero.** No lead-in of any shape stands before any of the four.

- **The Ledger is never ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about.** `Ledger` returns **one hit across

- **The sustained scene in which he is on the page in his own right is ch 338, *The Room With Doors*, in Batch
  0003. Nothing in these sixteen chapters moves, duplicates, pre-spends or describes it.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 329

**The register still reads *reconciled* — `grep` returns zero across the batch, because no card read it and the
allowance was zero.** MEMBER ABSENT stands three times and was not raised · the ch 150 refusal unsoftened · the
ch 128 silence unbroken · **the year of non-forgiveness still carrying and not mentioned once in these sixteen
chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero on the page and still in two heads · what became of the
eleven not raised · **no office named at all, and nothing above the copying floor entered, described or named** ·
the surface answer not re-voiced, hinted at or paraphrased · the seventh condition never listed, counted or
referred to · **the four months erased in Chapter 3 carried in the body and never named** · the claim not
reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page · Luo Ping never met · Ning Wu does not appear · **Bai
Zhu is off the page in this batch entirely** and his request is still pending and the line's own people still do
not know she exists · **Wei Ran does not appear in this batch at all** — she is off the page between Batch 0001
and Batch 0004, as `outline/volume-06.md` has her · **the copying floor is not entered again** · no harem and
nothing a romance · she never says her mother's name · no new enemy and no named villain.

### Live at the end of this batch, going into Batch 0003

- **She is in two books.** A running column of days in a hand one flight above a counter, and **a materials
  account in a second office nine miles east, entered against material, copied, and entered on the twenty-sixth of
  a month.** She can see neither and neither can see the other, and **from that hour leaving the reach no longer
  takes her out of the books.**
- **The second office's list comes down about four times a year and is offered, not put up on a wall.** The next
  thing that comes down that is hers will be priced by the job and not by her arm.
- **The rate for a reach is five coppers and she can open half of one**, and **every stage she takes from here
  takes it down again.** The works pays by the job.
- **A charge is nine coppers and does not come in a size one arm can carry.** A box is four days of the ring.
  She is six miles from the fire and eleven minutes a lump away from any clay at all.
- **The ring is lit and answers nothing**, and what went out of her on the twenty-fourth of the last month was
  the answering and there is no material in the world that puts it back.
- **Her hands are warm and her hand is no longer an instrument**, and she is out on flat ground every night.
- **He has proved he can get into any line on that ground**, and a yard nine miles east has a hole on its board
  that a man with ink on the side of his hand will have to answer for in about four days' time.

### Open after the batch, going into Batch 0003

- **What does a second office do with a materials account?** **Answered in part, in material: a cart went.** Open:
  whether anything is made to be presented, and that is Batch 0003's.
- **What is above the copying floor, and what is above the second office?** Never entered, never described, never
  named. Looked at for nine seconds at ch 308.
- **Where does the strip go?** It goes up. It is not the mill's to keep and it is not the counter's.
- **What was he before he died?** Open, Volume 11. **Was she shown a readout?** Open, Volume 11.
- **The surface answer beneath the drift, the tenth part, the eleven, *kiln-born*, the register, the Hunter's
  request.** All open. All somebody else's volume.

Bracket 2 of Volume 6's six. **Cost: the cold leaves her hands first — she stops being cold the way a stone is cold and starts being warm the way a furnace is warm, and she can no longer tell by feel whether a night will hurt her.** Paper still Foundation the first stage, never opened |
| Body | Short breath four; right index gone, four fingers' grip; right arm open half, permanent, since ch 288; aperture cracked permanent; nine of eleven holds; turn gone; middle-bank method; not hungry; **hands warm**; **eighteen** |
| Employment | **NOTHING.** No wages, no season, no booking, no stub. **A gate at five coppers a reach, half a reach, two and a half a day — paid at a gate in her own hand, and every stage she takes takes it down again.** Plus **listed work off a board at the works, paid in what the job was made of, taken off the wall by her own hand, with no name on the line.** **And since the twenty-sixth of a month, a materials account in a second office, entered out loud at a gate.** Eighteen counterfoils in four marks and no nineteenth, for ever |
| The furnace | **Open and lit full since the nineteenth of a month, in a shed at the mill, out of twenty-three pieces of dressed stone a cart carried off a yard on a day it was not going to go.** **It answers nothing.** The ground under it gives nothing back and never has and she has never said so out loud |

of a month.** The geography was reconciled at the same time: **the mill is 0, Pei's gate half a mile east, the
material shed about three-quarters of a mile east, the works three miles east, and the ring is in a shed at the
mill** — so a sack off the shed is two miles there and back and a box off a listing is six.

## Volume 6 Batch 0002 (ch 314–329) — REVIEW REPAIR

**Reviewed and repaired. Thirteen findings, all closed. No beat, day, stage, bracket, turn, midpoint, climax,
irreversible decision or planned event moved, and no chapter outside ch 314–329 was opened.** The batch was not
restarted. The prose was kept and corrected in place; **the only chapter touched that had no arithmetic or
continuity fault is ch 328, and it was touched three times for elapsed time, not for its scenes.**

### The five faults that were on the page

| # | Fault | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **The money rate contradicted itself between ch 314 and ch 323.** Ch 314's sum read as *nine coppers = four days of ring*; ch 323 read *a third of a sack, three coppers, = four days of the ring*. | **The batch's own anchor, six texts deep, is that a third of a sack is a **day's** ring and she buys one every fourth day.** Ch 314 never actually said otherwise — *four days* there is the gate-work cost of a charge — but it read ambiguously, so `Four days.` became `Four days of work for it.` and *four days to a charge* became *four days of work to a charge*. Ch 323:27 now reads *about a day's ring*. The rule reconciles end to end: a box is four thirds of a sack = twelve coppers = four days; two boxes = twenty-four coppers = eight days; a charge at nine = three days |
| 2 | **A box was worth three different amounts** — four days at ch 315, nine at ch 321, four and a half at ch 323 — and ch 323's *nine days of the ring is thirty coppers* implied 3.33 coppers a day against the batch's own three coppers for a *third of a sack* | **Six texts said four days and won: ch 315:71, ch 317:81, ch 318:65, ch 318:87, ch 318:95, ch 318:97.** Ch 321's standalone **Nine days** became **Eight days** — the tray is two boxes — and ch 323:27 became *eight days of the ring, and eight days of the ring is twenty-four coppers, and twenty-four coppers at two and a half a day is ten days at a gate*. Ch 323:29's *Thirty coppers is five months* went with it. **Ch 323's *out of two boxes* was correct** — ch 319 has the retaining wall's pay as two boxes and the yard's figure at twenty-five — and was left alone. Also corrected: ch 323:5 *a fifth of the box* for nine pieces out of twenty-three → **two fifths**; ch 323:7's *eight hours a box and eight boxes a round* → **eight loads and a little better than two days a load**, which is the figure ch 320:93 already gave. Ch 326 and ch 327's *nine days of a fire* became **eight** |
| 3 | **The elapsed time to the cart was wrong twice.** The reach went at the seventh hour on the twenty-fourth, day 667; the cart left at the ninth hour of the thirteenth of the next month, day 686. That is **nineteen days**, and ch 325 said nine and twenty-two | **Both now read nineteen** (ch 325:59, ch 325:65). The continuity record at *the twenty-fourth of the last month* + *the cart went nine days later* matched neither and was rewritten |
| 4 | **ch 314 collided with ch 315's day.** It opened on the third and then dated its eastern trip to the fourth three times, which is the same hour, the same day, that ch 315 has her down in the ditch | **All three now read the third** (ch 314:67, 89, 111). The ch 314:111 clause *worth more than everything she had earned since the seventh of a month* also went: the seventh is three days in the future, and a box at twelve coppers is not more than a fortnight's gate work. **It is replaced with the true version** — four days of work, earned on a board with no name on it, for an afternoon and four nails |
| 5 | **ch 315's day count did not match its span.** It went down on the fourth, came up on the sixth — three days — and reported five, under a heading that read The Fifth Day | **It is three days and the board said four, and the chapter now says so three times.** The beat was not thrown away: the man's line is now that the day she did not use goes back on the board and is not his to hand out, and her interior beat is that **for the first time in five months a man on that ground being right had cost her nothing at all.** The ripple was carried: ch 327:39's *four days and a fifth* → **seven days, three on a head and an apron and four on a footing**, which is the *week of her own wall* every later chapter names |

### The elapsed-time drift the review sampled and did not finish

ch 328 claimed **seven weeks** at a yard she first walked into on the third of a month, three instances, and ch 324
and ch 325 called a six-to-ten-day acquaintance *a month*. All now measured against the day table: ch 324 *six days*
and *a week* ×2, ch 325 *ten days*, ch 328 *three weeks* ×3, ch 326's *three days before the cart question came
round again* → **six** and *in about six days* → **about eight days** for the twenty-sixth. **ch 329's *a yard that
had been giving her work for a month* was left: the third to the twenty-sixth is twenty-three days and the figure
stands.**

### The batch in one sentence

Her column got read against a second book by a man with a brush, a paper came down a road with two columns on it
and a holding under her days, she spent the last of what he had that an office could not write so that a fire in
a shed would hold without her, took two more stages, and found out at the end of it that a book on that ground
had stopped shutting clean because she had cleared some work.

### The clock and the day table — and the one place this batch reaches past its own week

this batch opens in began on day 674 and the next began on day 704 and the one after that on day 734.**

  minutes a piece*, *three weeks*, *five weeks*, *close on eight weeks*, *fourteen days* are ordinary relative
  stretches.
- **No pay-cycle.** Nothing falls on a date on that ground and no card revived one.

### New machinery, established once and used after

- **The name column on the works' board (ch 330).** Since the twenty-sixth the listing man cannot write a line
  that leaves the yard against nothing; there is a ruled space about two fingers wide at the left of the board
  and every line runs into it. **A materials account is paid to a place, not to a person**, and she gives the
  mill. He tells her, unprompted, that the mill has a book that shuts on the thirtieth and goes up the mill road
  to the counting house. **Nothing else about the mill's book is known at ch 330 and nothing else is said.**
- **The cross-reference (ch 331).** On the copying floor, a man with a brush: **every month he copies her column
  out against another book by hand, because the two of them were put across from each other on the twenty-third
  of a month back.** She asks what a cross-reference is for and is told, in one sentence: *so that a thing about
  you is also a thing about what you have done.* **She cannot see either book and neither book can see the other.**
- **The two books' different ends (ch 340).** A day ends, so the counter's book shuts on the thirtieth. **Material
  does not end**, so the second office's book has been open since before the man behind the counter came there and
  will be open when he is gone. **Everything the second office holds about her is copied across into the one that
  shuts, by hand, at the end of every month.** This is the machine the break at ch 344 turns on and it is
  established here, in the correct mouth, before it is used.
- **The sending (ch 336), written in this batch and not refused in it.** A strip about as wide as a hand comes down
  off the bundle at the works on the seventeenth of a month. **Two columns.** The left is five months of her days
  in the counter's hand, with *good* above the hours, and **two ruled lines under it that have nothing at the
  other end of the rule** — a materials account against the mill, and below it the holder line, in a fine hand.
  The right column runs the length of the strip: a mark that is not a name, four figures, the word HELD, a rule,
  and **two words under the rule in the same fine hand that she cannot read at all — the room.** Four lines at the
  foot, verbatim:

  WHAT IS COPIED AT THIS REACH IS COPIED TWICE.
  THE COLUMN OF NING YAN, OF LOWWATER, IS KEPT AND IS COPIED AND IS NOT ON THIS GROUND.
  THE HOLDER IS TO BE PRESENTED IN PERSON IN THE ROOM NAMED UNDER THE HOLDER.
  THE DOORS OF THAT ROOM SHUT ON THE TWENTY-EIGHTH OF A MONTH.

  **The room is not named on the strip. The room is on the copy, and the copy is above the counter. Nothing above
  the copying floor is entered, described or named anywhere in this batch.** The doors line is a standing fact
  about a room, not a deadline on her, and the request is **open and unanswered at the last page of this batch.**
- **The four words that came back (ch 344), verbatim:** *It goes as it is.*

### The irreversible steps, three, and none of them is deferred into Batch 0004

1. **Ch 336.** The readable line has been looked at and a sending has been written against her, with her column
   on it and his holding under it. **She cannot be un-read and the paper cannot go back down the road.**
2. **Ch 337–338.** Yuan is addressed as a holder. **At the price named in advance on the page before it was paid:
   his unnamed state, and with it the ability ever again to be nowhere an office can address.** The Hand is
   spent out of her foundation, told before spent, and it does not come back.
3. **Ch 344.** **The box circuit is broken.** Not by a person refusing and not by a person breaking anything. A
   book will not close clean because two lines in it have no end on them, a strip of cloth with four words on it
   comes back down the stair, and a keeper pastes the strip in anyway because that is what the board is for.

### Golden Core 6 — ch 332, day 712, the ninth of a month

Taken standing, in the open, out past the last hedge about a mile and a quarter east of the mill, with the bar
laid down on the grass and nothing within about half a mile. **Thirty-eight days after Golden Core 5 at day 674.**
Licensed by no county paper of any kind.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage six, is held.]

**Cost, named once, permanent: one hold out of the eleven goes — nine to eight.** Told before spent, in the
ordinary voice, at the top of the low drain on the far side of the elms on the seventh of a month, and spent into
the listed clearing she cleared by hand past the last working point — a head at the low drain, two days and one
box, cleared in two days. **A hold is not a finger and it is not the arm. It is the difference between a hand
closing on a thing and a hand closing on nothing while the weight of it goes somewhere else.** Eight do not come
back.

### Golden Core 7 — ch 342, day 749, the sixteenth of a month

Taken standing, in the open, on the same ground past the last hedge, at about the fourth hour. **Thirty-seven days
after Golden Core 6. Thirty days of the interval were spent behind ch 341's fourteen-day section and the stage
day itself was pinned thirty-seven days back from day 712.**

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage seven, is held.]

**Cost, named once, permanent: the short breath goes past four counts where work is heaviest — five counts on a
loaded reach — and never comes back to four there.** Four everywhere else, always. On a bank of four feet at the
second gully, at the third count, going up with four dressed pieces under one arm and the bar in her left hand,
it is five. **Put the load down and it is four.** The place where it goes to five did not move across eleven
climbs in an hour and it will not move again.

**And the money moves with it, on the page, the same evening.** A drain at four feet deep is a loaded reach.
Half a reach was two and a half coppers; **a third of a reach is one and a half**, taken out of the fold in the
inside of the door frame at Pei’s gate on the eighteenth of a month. The gate has never once in five months not
been exactly her.

### The Hand, spent a second time, at ch 338

- **Told before spent, in her own words and then his, at the second hour of the twenty-second of a month in the
  shed at the mill**, on the floor, with a piece of cloth folded in three under her hand. That is the arrangement
  and it has never once been broken in four years.
- **What he wants, in his own words:** the fire to hold without her, so that she never has to be at a shed at the
  fourth hour again. **He says on the page that this is the only thing he has ever wanted that was for her and
  not for himself.** It is the first such want in four years.
- **Out of her foundation**, not her hand and not a book. What leaves her is *the thing under her base* — the
  part of a body that keeps a fire lit in a room she has to walk to. He is not there and has not been; the reach
  is hers and she gives it.
- **What it costs him, named on the page before it is paid:** the unnamed state. Five months of standing behind
  the last thing in him that an office cannot write on, given up so that a holding can have a name on it.
  **After it he is not nowhere.** A holding has to be held by name, and a name is a word an office can put on a
  strip and carry up a road, and there is no such thing as a road that does not go where that word goes.
- **It is not a re-run of ch 272 and the two chapters do not share a setting, a cost, a want or a verb.** Ch 272
  is a field of lines with a keeper's paste pot; ch 338 is **a room, with a floor, a table, a chair pushed back
  at an angle, two shut doors and one with a handle on it**, and a hand with a brush that is slow and is making a
  thing that will afterwards be correct. Ch 272 cost the unentered state and the date of the morning he died.
  Ch 338 costs the unnamed state. **The date of the morning he died is not given back and ch 272's finding is
  not softened — he reaches for the morning once, it is not there, and he does not look again.**
- **The frame.** Ch 338 is the sustained scene in which he is on the page in his own right, away from her hearing,
  and **the six-sentence ration does not apply to it**. It has **six utterances, every sentence in the present
  tense**. It follows ch 272's own handling of a frame in which the room is described in the present, which is
  the precedent the volume outline set for this scene.
- **Yuan outside ch 338: two utterances in sixteen chapters, at ch 332 and at ch 337.** Nothing at ch 330–331,
  333–336, 339–345. *(Corrected at review. The first record said one, at ch 337, and missed ch 332's* All right.
  *at the top of the low drain on the seventh of a month.)*
- **Nothing in Volume 6 kills anybody for it.**

### Merit

**Does not settle in this batch.** Two settlements were Batch 0002's and Batch 0004's halves. `tribulation`
returns **zero** across ch 330–345, nothing is cleared and nothing is killed, and **no figure of what is left of
him is on any page.** The cold has not come up through the base since the twenty-fourth of the last month and
ch 333 says so once, plainly, and does not make a mystery of it.

### The Hunter — on the page, ch 335 only

**Bai Zhu, at nine feet off a cart track on the fifteenth of a month at about the fourth hour, notebook shut under
his arm, no other paper of any kind.** He attends, certifies, writes four lines a day and **buys nothing**. He
does not ask her a question and has never asked her one. He says one true thing about the shape of the new paper
— *a column and a holding under it; the last shape was a line and a line is a thing that gets found, this has a
holding under it* — and then, once, in the ordinary voice, without weight and without a threat:

**I do not want it answered either.**

He explains it in one sentence — an answered thing goes up and somebody above him has to reckon with the answer and
he has never believed the reckoning would be the right one — and does not say it again. **He also says his paper
has been in with the district for two years and a writ is not issued to a field agent, and that he is still here
and will not come inside nine feet again unless she puts him there. His request is still pending. The line's own
people still do not know she exists.** He says *your brother is on it* once, flatly, and nothing else about it.

### The structural answer — begun at ch 331–345, in four mouths, in short sentences, never as one speech

**The line past the last working point is walked, kept, copied and vouched by offices that never needed a bound —
kept up by hand, by people paid by the day and by the clearing, with families on it.** The tenth part is **not**
given, nothing proves the crews wrong, and no card claims otherwise.

- **Ch 331, the man with the brush:** *there is a line out west and it is kept and there are men on it and they
  are paid by the day and their families are on it and nobody east of here has ever needed one thing out of it,
  including me, and I have been copying books for eleven years.*
- **Ch 335, Bai Zhu:** *it is kept. It has been kept for longer than I have been alive and it is kept by men, not
  by an office. Nobody out here has ever needed one thing out of that.* And then, flatly: *I cannot tell you why
  it is there. I have not been told why it is there. I have met four men in nineteen years who worked it and not
  one of them could tell me what was at the other side of it.*
- **Ch 337, the listing man:** *there is a road west of this gate about nine miles and there is a line on it. Men
  go out on it and come back. They are paid by the day. I had an uncle on it.* Then: *I have never needed one
  thing out of it. Nobody on this ground has.* And: *and now there is.*
- **Ch 345, the keeper of the board:** *that is not because anybody is hiding anything. That is because there has
  never been anything on one that needed answering.* Nine years, about three hundred strips, about four hundred
  books up the road and never one back down.

### What is broken, and how

**The closed-season box circuit, and nothing else was touched.**

A book that shuts on the thirtieth has a beginning and an end on everything in it. Two lines under her column —
**a materials account against the mill, and a holding with nothing at the other end of the rule** — have no end,
so the book does not shut. **The man with the brush cannot take them out** (a man who copies does not decide what
is in a book, and taking them out would be a false book), **cannot rule across them**, writes the day in the
margin against both, says *I am going to have to ask*, goes up the stair, and gets four words back on a strip in
about four hours: **It goes as it is.**

The tray is corded with a page fanned open in it and goes up the mill road on the first cart at about the sixth
hour. **A strip comes off it.** The cart stops at the post and the keeper — about fifty-five, nine years on that
board — reads it, works the paste round the pot with the toe of his boot, presses it down with the flat of his
hand, and pastes it in, **because that is what the board is for.**

**From that hour no book on that reach holds only hours and no tray on that reach closes clean** — and the rule
that has run for nine years, *nothing comes back down*, is broken too, because an open line has to be answered by
somebody. **At ch 345 a strip comes down that road that is a question and not a reading.** It is the first thing
in nine years to come down it. It goes back up on the next bundle. **She can read her own name on it and cannot
read the fine hand, and the fine hand has to be read in a building nine days of road east of that post.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 345

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** across ch 330–345, because no card read it
and the allowance was zero · the Clay Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) ·
the relicensed licence **never opened** (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and
never raised · **the year of non-forgiveness carrying and not mentioned once in these sixteen chapters** ·
Shen Xiu unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero on the page and still in two heads · what became of the eleven not raised
· **no office named at all, and nothing above the copying floor entered, described or named** · the surface answer
not re-voiced · the seventh condition never listed, counted or referred to · the four months erased in Chapter 3
carried and never named · the claim not reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page · Luo Ping never
met · Ning Wu does not appear · **Wei Ran does not appear in this batch** · no harem and nothing a romance ·
she never says her mother's name · no new enemy and no named villain · the Ledger never ordered, held, priced,
refused or thought about (`Ledger` returns **zero** across the batch) · **Feng does not appear and no second Lung
and no second appointment** · the fourth erased months carried in the body.

### Live at the end of this batch, going into Batch 0004

- **She is in three books she cannot see.** A running column one flight above a counter; a materials account in a
  second office nine miles east; **and now both of them read together, by hand, once a month, by a man with a
  brush, against each other.**
- **A sending is written and is unanswered.** She is carrying it folded in three against her ribs. **The holder
  under her column is to be presented in person in a room that is not named on the strip** and she is the only
  person on nine days of road who can carry it there. The request is standing. **It is not refused and no card in
  this batch may refuse it — ch 358 is Batch 0004's.**
- **He is addressed as a holder and is therefore sendable**, at a price he named himself in advance, and the two
  ch 272 losses are still both gone.
- **Her furnace holds without her** and she has not walked to the mill at night in four days and is not going to.
- **The circuit is broken** and a question is on the board, and the fine hand has to be read by somebody who has
  never been able to read it.
- **Her body is smaller than it was at the ninth of a month.** Eight holds of eleven. Five counts of breath on a
  loaded reach and never four there. A third of a reach at a gate and one and a half coppers a day. **Four listed
  things cleared in seven weeks, four lines left on the board, and five more coming down over two months.**
- **The Hunter's request is pending. The line's own people still do not know she exists. Nothing above the
  copying floor has been entered. The register still reads *reconciled* and nothing has read it in a month.**

paragraph at 0.63 and the *nobody inside to be told* paragraph at 0.45 between ch 332 and ch 342. Both are the
same act staged twice, ten stages apart, in the same order, on open ground, with nothing announced, and the
bracket that follows each is a pinned string that cannot vary. Batch 0002 recorded the same kind of thing for the
*told before spent* ritual at ch 316 and ch 320. **The two utterances are not the same sentence.**

**One discrepancy between the batch prompt and the page, resolved in favour of the page, as the prompt itself
directs:** the prompt says Batch 0004 opens at day 736. It does not. **Ch 342 is pinned to day 749, and ch 343, 344
and 345 are dated on the page and follow it to day 773, so the batch closes after day 773 and Batch 0004 opens
there.** The prompt's own instruction — hold day 712 inside days 701–735, take GC7 on day 749, and leave the days
between to be carried as elapsed rather than walked — was followed, and ch 341 carries the fourteen days in a
section of its own. The Batch 0004 prompt states the corrected handoff at its head, with the derived table.

## Volume 6 Batch 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-06-batch-0003.review.log`)

**No beat, day-table position, title, turn, midpoint, climax, irreversible decision, bracket, stage or planned
event was moved, and no chapter before `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0330.md` was opened. Every repair below is a
date, a count, a name's geography or a duplicated string, and all sixteen chapters were re-read for prose after
the last one.**

### The eleven manuscript repairs, in the order the review gave them

1. **Ch 342 — duplicated sentence cut.** `It was over at about four minutes.` stood as a whole paragraph
   immediately after the paragraph that already ended with it. One of the two is gone. **The batch's bracket count,
   the stage and the day are untouched.**
2. **Ch 333 — the eighth hold is found on the ninth, not the seventh.** Golden Core 6 is pinned to day 712, the
   ninth of a month, and ch 332 stages it at the fourth hour of the ninth; ch 333 had put the discovery at the
   seventh and then contradicted itself four paragraphs later with *Eight of eleven at the ninth*. **Four
   references corrected** — the two-day clearing now ends on the seventh with the box waiting and she carries nine
   holds into the yard on the morning of the ninth and eight out of it; the box off the wall, the check at the
   yard gate and the last time she was glad of it are all on the ninth. **The arithmetic section, the six lines on
   the board and the whole argument of the chapter are untouched.**
3. **Ch 341 — the closer is the sixteenth, not the fifteenth.** GC7 is pinned to day 749, the sixteenth, and ch
   341's last sentence was walking her out to the hedge on the fifteenth, which is ch 342's opening scene on the
   wrong day. **The day is corrected and the sentence was also recast** so that it no longer re-narrates ch 342's
   opening sentence in the same words: she carries the bar out past the last hedge before the light and lays it on
   the grass, and that is where the chapter ends. The fourteen-day section and the pinned GC7 day are untouched.
4. **Ch 345 — `the cart this morning` corrected to `the cart on the first`.** The chapter opens on the tenth day,
   the keeper says twice that the cart came down on the first, and the closing paragraph says the strip had been on
   the board ten days. One phrase contradicted all three.
5. **Ch 340 — the trays now count.** The chapter enumerated three trays and then said the *fourth* one would be
   corded that night. **It now enumerates four**, the first went up in the month after she came past the mill, the
   second on the thirtieth of the month before that one, the third on the thirtieth of the month before this one,
   and the fourth is the one on the bench with the knot in the man's teeth; *she had been in four trays* follows,
   and the closing paragraph refers to this one by name instead of by number.
6. **Ch 340 — the step scene is the thirtieth of the month before, not the seventh.** It is ch 331, dated the
   thirtieth, and ch 340 already calls it *a month before* two sections further on. **The other loose date in the
   same exchange was corrected with it** — the question she had wanted to ask had been ready since *the
   twenty-eighth*, which is the twenty-eighth of the month before this one, not the twenty-eighth two days ago.
7. **Ch 341 — nine holds corrected to eleven.** She has eleven holds and has had them since she was nine; the
   count in the sentence about nine hundred carries is now *eleven holds in that hand*, and the five-month clause
   reads *with one of them gone* rather than *with a hold gone*, so the two halves of the sentence agree.
8. **Ch 338 — the geography slip.** *There is a woman three miles east of it* put her three miles east of a room
   whose location is never established. **She is now placed from a known point instead** — *Two miles west of a
   yard, at the second hour of the twenty-second of a month* — which is where ch 337 and ch 338 already put her,
   and the rest of the paragraph is untouched.
9. **Nine days corrected to five months, four places.** ch 337 named the same interval as *nine days* in one
   sentence and *five months back* in the next, and ch 338 called the unnamed state *nine days of standing behind*
   twice while the chapter's own clock said five months in three other places. **ch 337 line 69, ch 337 line 75,
   ch 338's *What Is Left Of Him* and ch 338's *What Is Taken Of Him* all now read five months.** The ch 272
   finding, the apology, the date of the morning he died and the price itself are untouched.
10. **The false `no date on the page` premise corrected in six files.** See the next block.
11. **Yuan's utterance count corrected.** Eight utterances in sixteen chapters, not seven; two outside ch 338,
   not one; and the ch 337 block is **four** sentences, not nine, which is what ch 339 already said in her own
   mouth.

### The seven minor prose repairs

- **Ch 331** — *a thing being done to a column of* broke on the preposition and the sentence ended. Cut.
- **Ch 339** — *since the eighth of the first month of the last month* is not a date any reader can hold. Now
  *since the eighth of a month*, the convention used everywhere else in the book.
- **Ch 339** — the hand that stopped telling her warm from cold had been gone *for over three weeks*. It goes at
  Golden Core 5, day 674, and ch 339 is day 729: **close on eight weeks.**
- **Ch 340** — the five sentences about the tray road were heard *on the eleventh of a month*. Ch 334 puts the
  tray man back out of the shed on the thirteenth, *for the first time in five weeks*, which is the eighth.
- **Ch 342** — *it took her until about the eighth hour to get as far as she used to get by the eighth* was
  circular and read as losing no time. **Now `by the seventh`**, which is the hour she goes in at and makes the
  lost hour legible without touching the pay, the bar or Pei.
- **Ch 343** — the opening said the seventeenth was *the first time in two months* she had been paid for less than
  half a reach, and left ch 342's sixteenth unaccounted for. **It now says the sixteenth and the seventeenth were
  two mornings running, and that less than half had not happened in two months.**
- **Ch 343** — *the second day* and *the fifth* sat in the same chapter reading as month-days. **Both now carry
  `day`**, which is what they are: days of a listing that began on the eighteenth and ran five against the board's
  four.

## Established facts, Volume 6 Batch 0004 (ch 346–360) — STANDING AND REFUSING TO STAND

**Fifteen chapters, 21,479 words of body prose, average ~1,432, day 774 to day 848, and the volume closes with it.**
**TWO bracketed layer lines, at ch 352 and at ch 357 — brackets 5 and 6 of Volume 6's six, and the budget is spent.
Ch 360 carries none and that silence is the point.** Chapter titles and section headings excluded. Drafted against
`outline/volume-06.md` and against `workspace/volume-06-batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, which is this batch's card. **The
prompt's corrected handoff was right and the page followed it: the batch opens after day 773, not at day 736 and not
at day 749.** Every pinned item in the prompt is on the page. **REVIEWED AND REPAIRED — eighteen findings applied,
no beat moved; see *Volume 6 Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR* at the foot of this file.**

Per file, body prose: 346 1,361 · 347 1,326 · 348 1,403 · 349 1,354 · 350 1,501 · 351 1,322 · 352 1,326 · 353 1,507 ·
354 1,385 · 355 1,393 · 356 1,455 · 357 1,696 · 358 1,533 · 359 1,391 · 360 1,526. **None under the 1,300 floor and
none above the 2,700 guide.** Volume 6 file set after this batch: **94,946 words of body prose over sixty-two
chapters.**

### The batch in one sentence

She worked the last of the lines on a board that was going bare, asked a stage on open ground for the thing she wanted
a board to do and got it, lost the heavy end for good and a third of her wages with it, wrote a refusal out on cloth
with the flat of her own finger, learned that her second vouching had been carried further up than she asked it to
go, refused a sending out loud at a counter in front of nine men, found her own book standing on a stone under a
keeper's ladder, and moved one stone she had never touched.

### The clock and the day table — and the two dates this batch is built on

Months are thirty days and month-day is `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`. **The month this batch opens in began on day 764
and runs to day 793; the next begins on day 794 and runs to day 823; the one after that begins on day 824.**

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 346 | 774–775 | the 11th–12th | The Lines On The Wall |
| 347 | 776–777 | the 13th–14th | Nine Feet Off The Road |
| 348 | 778–780 | the 15th–17th | The Room With A Date In It |
| 349 | 781–783 | the 18th–20th | Lift, Set Early, Move By Hand |
| 350 | 784 | the 21st | What The Quiet Is For |
| 351 | 785 | the 22nd | The Last Heavy Thing |
| 352 | **786 (pinned)** | the 23rd | **GOLDEN CORE 8** — It Takes Two Hands Now |
| 353 | 787–790 | the 24th–27th | The Weight Goes To The Bar |
| 354 | 791–795 | the 28th–2nd | Nothing Came Down The Road |
| 355 | 796–801 | the 3rd–8th | **THE HAND REFUSED ONCE** — He Will Not Give One |
| 356 | 802–817 | the 9th–24th | The Room With Two Things In It |
