- **Every pinned day verified against `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`. Day 786 is the 23rd of the month beginning on day
  764. Day 818 is the 25th of the month beginning on day 794. Day 845 is the 22nd of the month beginning on day 824.**
  Day 773, the tenth of the month beginning on 764, is the last page of Batch 0003 and ch 346 opens on the eleventh
  of the same month, one day after it. **Ch 346 and ch 349 carried no date on the page at review and now do: ch 346
  opens on the eleventh and ch 349 carries the eighteenth in its opening and in its first-day beat.**
- **The batch's own structure is two twenty-eighths.** The sending's fourth line says *the doors of that room shut on
  the twenty-eighth of a month*. Those are **day 791 (ch 354)** and **day 821 (the twenty-eighth of month B, inside
  ch 358–359)**. The first one passes while she is on flat ground with a bar that needs two hands and nothing comes
  down any road to tell her it happened. The second one passes two days after she refuses the sending at a counter.
  **Ch 358's refusal is therefore a refusal made against a date, and that is the batch's real deadline.**
- **No named weekday, no named calendar month, no named week.** *Three weeks*, *four weeks*, *six weeks*, *two
  months*, *about a fortnight*, *twice a week*, *four days*, *nine or ten days* are ordinary relative stretches.
- **No pay-cycle.** Nothing falls on a date on that ground and no card revived one.

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

| | Entering the batch (day 774) | At the last page (day 848) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gate at Pei's | five coppers a reach, **a third of a reach, one and a half a day** | five coppers a reach, **a fifth of a reach, one copper a day**, down at ch 353 |
| Listed work | paid in what the job was made of; a box is four days of the ring; a third of a sack of clay is three coppers | **the board is bare.** Three lines taken in this batch — a footing (two days, one box), a ditch (two), a head (three) — and nothing until the season turns |
| Bread | two a day at a store five miles off, and it had been seven days' bread for five months | **six days' bread, then five from the twenty-eighth** |
| Furnace | lit by itself, about a box and a half of clay left | **about half a box, and the ring is hers** |

- **The gate rate falls at ch 353 because Pei is right.** A reach is priced by what comes **over the bank**, and
  after Golden Core 8 she can dig a reach and cannot get what she digs up four feet of bank with one hand. *Five
  coppers a reach; a fifth of a reach is one; a quarter of one is not a thing any gate on nine days of road pays.*
- **The board goes bare at ch 356, on the fifteenth.** Four lines on the wall on the eleventh of the month last, four
  more coming down over two months, and by the fifteenth there is nothing on it. The listing man is right:
  the board is bare until the season turns, because a board is a board and it carries what is put on it.
- **Clothing and food are not re-derived here and no figure in this table is new except the five-days' bread.**

### The construction the batch is built on, and how it moves

| | Entering (day 774) | At the last page (day 848) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The board | four lines | **bare** |
| The books | two she cannot see, read against each other by hand once a month | **three, and the third is a copy of the second made upward out of its own office in a hand she has never seen** |
| The book that shuts | will not shut, page fanned open, corded twice, sent up four times as it is | **three things in it with nothing at the other end of the rule, one of them her refusal, and she put it there herself** |
| The book | in a tray going up the mill road | **standing on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post, open in the middle, unreadable to the only man holding it** |
| The sending | written, unanswered, folded in three against her ribs | **refused, entered in the office's own book with the twenty-sixth against it, and standing. It is in a building she cannot reach** |
| The reach | — | **begins, once, by accident, on the twenty-second** |

### Golden Core 8 — ch 352, day 786, the twenty-third of a month

Taken standing, in the open, on the same ground past the last hedge about a mile and a quarter east of the mill, at
about the fourth hour, with the bar laid down on the grass. **Thirty-seven days after Golden Core 7 at day 749.**

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage eight, is held.]

**Cost, named once, permanent: the middle-bank method stops paying — the bar she learned to carry one-handed now
needs two hands she does not have, and the heavy end closes to her for good.**

The method is stated once on the page at ch 349 and used after that — *lift with the furnace, set early, move by
hand*, learned at a bank nine days west from a woman with a scar across the back of both hands. It was never
strength; it was never waiting. **A bar has to be under the stone before the stone comes, and the piece has to go
where the piece is going, and the last foot of that is done by hand. That is two hands.** She has eight holds in the
left and four fingers and a thumb on the right, and the right arm open half the time and had been for four years.
**She can carry a bar. She cannot carry a stone and a bar.** That is said out loud on open ground where there is not
a living thing within half a mile, in two sentences, and it is the only time in seven stages she has said anything out loud on that ground
besides the stage itself.

**What closes:** not the ramp — the ramp was never hers and she asked about it in a gateway a day before and a correct
man said no. Everything else. Any work measured by what comes out of the ground in pieces rather than in length is
measured by weight, and weight is carried with a bar and a hand. **Nothing on that board pays her for it, and nothing
on that board could be put her on, and there is not going to be one.** *(The chapter's own line, recast at the
review — it had stood twice in a row as *there is no listing on that board …*.)** She wanted a line on the board her
hand could not do, for eleven days, out loud, at a post and a gate and a bench. **She got one and it was not on the board.**

### Golden Core 9 — ch 357, day 818, the twenty-fifth of a month

Taken standing, in the open, on the same ground, at about the fourth hour. **Thirty-two days after Golden Core 8, and
not thirty-seven. She counts it back on the way out, gets thirty-two twice, and does not know what to make of it.**
The stage is short of its interval because she is no longer carrying it on wages alone: six days of the last interval
were days she worked a board line instead of a gate reach, and there is nothing in her that can read that.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Golden Core, stage nine, is held.]

**It is the last bracket in Volume 6.**

**Cost, named once, permanent: the finding, not the body.** Nothing came out of her hands and nothing came out of her
arm and no hold and no count of breath, and she stood on the grass for about two minutes waiting for the bill. The
bill came at the seventh hour and it came in the shed. **The furnace of her own, finished:** three handfuls of ash on
the ring did not bring it up and did not put it out, and a lump out of a box against the wall went in at the low end
and it came up against the brick and stood there about nine feet high and it was hers.

**And then the finding, in the ordinary course, from a correct man with a brush, on the twenty-fifth, at the end of
the month, at about the second hour, on the copying floor.** Two sheets are on the top step and neither is his. One
has her column on it in a hand she can read from four feet. **The other has two columns on it, and under the days at
the foot of the left one are two lines with nothing at the other end of the rule — the materials account against the
mill, and the holding — and both of them are in the hand she has never been able to read.**

He does the copy at the end of every month. He has been copying books in that building for eleven years. **He copies
down. Everything comes up that stair and it gets read against everything else that went up it, and he has never in
eleven years copied a thing upward out of the room, and he is not the man who could.** The second sheet came down on
the bundle on the twenty-second, **already written**, and it goes back up on the first cart whatever he does to it.

**She had asked a man with a bench at a gate in daylight, on the twenty-sixth of a month four months back, to be put
in a materials account against the mill.** She had done it because a furnace eats three coppers a third of a sack and
she had a third of a reach a day at a gate, and she had known at the time that it could not be taken back.

**It had gone four hundred yards further than she had asked for it to.** She is a person two offices will vouch for
and she found out on the last step of six stages she had taken in seven months that the second of those two vouchings
was carried up out of the office that keeps it, written again in a hand she has never seen, by somebody she will never
meet, and sent back down her own road so that a man with a brush would set her against herself.

**She cannot be un-entered twice over.** There is no going back to being a woman who works off a wall with no name
on anything and there is no going back to being entered once. The whole of what asking in daylight bought her is that
she is the one standing at the bottom of the stair finding out, instead of a person who finds out from a strip about
three weeks after it happens. The chapter says what that costs one person in one plain sentence and does not say it
twice.

### The Hand, refused once, at ch 355 — refused and not spent

- **The third spending does not happen in this volume.** Hand was spent at ch 325 and at ch 338 and is refused here,
  out loud, and that is the whole of it.
- **The asking.** The third strip off the works bundle in six weeks with words at the bottom of it, on the third of a
  month at about the second hour, and under the four lines there is a fifth: *the holder named is the only holder
  named, a second holder is to be named under the first, and the naming is to be done by the first holder.*
  **That fifth line is in the ordinary hand, and she can read every word of it.** **This record said *same fine hand,
  still unreadable*, which was wrong, and the page was ambiguous rather than clear — the fine hand was named in the
  next paragraph about the strip as a whole and never about that line.** **Corrected here and made explicit on the
  page at the review**, because the reading is the chapter: an office above has written her something she can
  understand, and what she cannot read is the holding under her column, which is the thing she came to ask about.
- **What a second holder is**, said plainly on the page: a second mark under the first, with a second set of figures
  under that, so that if one name cannot be produced another can. Every book on nine days of road has one for every
  column in it and every one of them is one name and not two, and no office on this ground has ever needed a second,
  because they have never needed one.
- **His refusal**, at the second hour of the fourth of a month, in the shed, in his own words, every sentence present
  tense, and as long as the refusal needs. *No.* He will not put a second name under that line, and he had decided it
  weeks before she asked. **Because a second holder is a second person an office can send for, and it would be on that
  line for as long as the line is there, and so a man with a cart that goes twice a week would be on it at the far end
  of his life without knowing what he was standing under.**
- **The price of refusing, in his own words and not as a stage and not as a settlement: she says it, and he agrees.**
  *You are refusing a thing that would make me safer.* **Yes.**
- **He does not buy anything, advise anything or advance anything, and he does not say one word about her hours, her
  column, the four fingers, the eight holds or the four minutes of breath.** He said four sentences about a man at the
  works and then he said no.
- **What she did with it: she wrote the refusal herself**, at the fourth hour on the ninth, three lines, eleven
  minutes a line with the flat of her second finger, every line costing its hour twice over, and it went back up the
  road on the next bundle. **Nothing came out of the ground she was standing on.** Her hands are her hands.
- **Yuan's sentences outside ch 355: three utterances in fourteen chapters — ch 348 (two sentences), ch 351 (two
  sentences), ch 356 (two sentences). Nothing at 346, 347, 349, 350, 352, 353, 354, 357, 358, 359 or 360.** Six
  sentences in sixteen chapters against the card's eight.

### The refusal at ch 358 — out loud, in daylight, with her name said, entered and standing

- **The office's own book is the seasonal book on the counter**, because it is the only book on nine days of road that
  shuts, and he says so at ch 340 and again here. The man behind the counter has a column with her name at the top of
  it that will not shut, and a line in it that has to be produced in a room on the twenty-eighth, and the room is not
  named on the strip and is not in that building.
- **The question is asked in the room, in the ordinary voice, in front of about nine men, with six of the counter's
  own at their cups.** *Are you going to carry the holder in.* He says before he asks it that he will write down what
  she says either way and the word he hears, and he does.
- **What she said, verbatim in the chapter:** *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. No.* And then: *The holder is not presented. I am
  not carrying him to that room. And I will not stand in one and say that I have.*
- **What he wrote, verbatim in the chapter:** *“Ning Yan, of Lowwater. The holder is not presented. Refused at the
  counter. The twenty-sixth.”* Fifteen words, read back to himself once without being said out loud. *(Set in
  sentence case inside quotation marks at the review; it stood as a bare all-caps line, which read as a panel.)* He does not write why, and says out loud that a man behind a counter does not write down
  what a person says about a thing he cannot see, and that a reason written in a margin is a thing that gets read out
  to a room.
- **What she said for, and why she had gone up there.** She had wanted somebody to come and tell her. She went up
  because there was a date on a strip and it was two days off and there was no one else on nine days of road who could
  say anything about it. **Ten weeks at her ribs for that fourth line, and six weeks ago eleven feet off a man with a
  notebook in ten inches of wet grass, told of four rooms and two that shut, and not told what one looked like and not
  able to ask.** *(She had never been in that room. The sentence that said she had, at the review, was false on the
  page and false against ch 347, and it is corrected.)* **She had not gone up there to protect the line. She had gone up there because she was alone
  with a date.** The chapter says so on the walk back, after the room.
- **This is a different kind of act from the vouching she agreed to at ch 329** — a yes given in daylight for
  materials, and a no given in daylight for herself. **Neither chapter states the other.**
- **What it costs the book:** it now holds three things with nothing at the other end of the rule — the materials
  account, the holding, and a refusal with a day against it and a name at the left of it. **It was already going up
  four times as it is, and it goes up on the first cart that night with a refusal in it that was not in it on the first
  of the month, and she put it there herself, and there is no hand in that building that can take it out.**
- **It cannot be undone.** From that hour the country past the line has to deal with her where she stands or not at
  all. **The strip went into the book with the book. She had nothing folded in three against her ribs after the
  twenty-sixth.**

### What breaking cost — ch 359, and it is the bill and not the breaking

**The break itself is ch 344's and is Batch 0003's. Ch 344 broke the circuit. Ch 359 is what the break costs, and the
two are not the same act and neither chapter says the other.** He held it to nine days and it was read and answered.

- The twenty-eighth passes. Nothing comes down the mill road, and she goes and looks at it anyway, and there is
  nothing on it, and the doors are nine days of road east of a post and she is not going to know for about six weeks
  if she ever knows.
- The first of the month: the tray goes up the mill road at the sixth hour with the cord over the top of a book with a
  page fanned open, and she watches it get past the elms. It is the seventh tray.
- **On the ninth, about the eleventh hour, a tray comes back down the mill road on the cart that takes the empty trays
  up.** A cord is still round it. The book will not shut. **The man behind the counter could not keep it on a counter
  and could not send it up a fifth time, so he put it back on the cart that came down with the empties, and that is
  correct, and that is the bill.**
- **The keeper** — about fifty-five, nine years on the board, a paste pot and a rag — has never in nine years had a
  book come down the road. He cannot open it, read it, shut it or send it anywhere else. He says *What do I do with a
  book* and is told.
- **The bill is said in a room with a person.** There is a low building at the back of that board with a door standing
  open and a bench inside it. She puts the tray on the bench and tells him what is in it: **a furnace no office on
  nine days of road built, a holder not one person in the world can produce, and a refusal with her name at the left
  of it.**
- **He pastes the strip in anyway, because that is what the board is for.** And then he says the other half of it,
  which is new and which is the whole of the chapter: *The board is for the strips.* The book goes under the ladder on
  a stone, because the stone is the driest thing he has and the roof does not come in on it, and it stands there.
- **The plain part about one person.** There is a woman in a shed a mile up that road who cleared some work off a
  board because she wanted clay, and then asked a man with a bench to put her in an account against material in
  daylight in her own name, and he was right to do it. And her own book is standing on a stone at a post in the open
  nine feet off a road, and **no office that reads that fine hand is ever going to know where it is**, and the only man
  holding it cannot read it and will not tell anybody, because there is nobody to tell.

### Nascent Soul 1 — ch 360, day 845, the twenty-second of a month. No bracket.

**The silence is the point and the chapter does not break it once.**

- A clearing past the mill, on the twenty-second, in the morning, **with no bar** — the bar takes two hands and it
  has stood against the fence of the shed since the twenty-third of the month before last. A gang of four men about
  two hundred yards off on the next bank. There is nothing on nine days of road for her to do and she has been pulling
  stones out of a fallen hedge for three days.
- **The reach begins, once, by accident.** She puts her hand flat on a stone she has not touched and has not been near.
  **It comes.** Not up. Along, at about the pace her hand goes, about the length of her own arm, and it stops with her
  hand on it and she puts it down on the grass where it stopped, and it is down. **The hole it was in is the shape the
  stone was in. There is no dust around the far side of it.**
- **Nobody hears it.** The four men are about nine feet off and not one of them stops, and that is correct of them: a
  stone that goes four feet and stops is a thing a man with a bar can do.
- **Nothing is said.** No bracket, no announcement, no voice, nothing out of the layer, and nothing out of him.
- **The cost in one plain sentence: she is surprised by nobody in the room where it happens, and the surprise does not
  come back.** Four men nine feet off do not look up. Where he is — in the room with the floor and the table and the
  two shut doors — **nothing at all happens, because she had not put this there**, and she had been able to put
  everything else there for four years. She goes looking for the feeling and it is not there, and **it will not come
  back, and it is not a thing anybody gives her twice.**
- **It does not repeat.** She goes back on the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth and takes four stones out the
  ordinary way and lifts the fourth about two inches and nothing comes, and the not being disappointed was the other
  half of the cost.
- **An office writes it down afterward, in a hand she has never seen.** A carter brings her a strip off the bundle at
  the gate post on the twenty-fifth, because her name is at the top of it. She can read the ordinary half. About a
  third of the way down it, in the fine hand she has never once been able to read, are four or five lines with a rule
  under the last of them. **She knows what is in them, and she does not need to read a word of them**, because there
  is a date on that strip and she knows the date. **The hand can read what happened. She cannot read the hand.**
- **Nine days of road behind her, and a stair, and a floor, and nine men on it who have not gone up that road in five
  months because she is not carrying their hours. And a tower with a light in it.** Everything that ever happened to
  her inside that boundary was announced, out loud, in a room, to nine people who had to write it down before she
  finished saying it. **She is standing on a bank in somebody's ordinary country with a furnace behind her that no
  office built and a column ahead of her that every office above the mill can read, and she has done one thing on
  that bank that not one person within nine days of road could have priced and not one of them heard.**
- **She will not walk it again.** Said on the page, in her own act, without a farewell to anybody.

### The structural answer — continued at ch 346–359, in four mouths, in short sentences, never as one speech

**The line past the last working point is walked, kept, copied and vouched by offices that never needed a bound, by
people paid by the day and by the clearing, with families on it.** The tenth part is **not** given, nothing proves the
crews wrong, and no chapter claims otherwise. **Four mouths, four chapters, four or five sentences each**, and not one
of them is the same mouth as the four Batch 0003 used (the man with the brush at 331, Bai Zhu at 335, the listing man
at 337, the keeper at 345 — **the brush man's mouth is used once in this batch and at the finding, at ch 357, and
says something different**).

- **Ch 346, the man behind the stack at the works:** *There is a line out west of this yard. About nine miles. It is
  walked and it is kept and there are men on it and they are paid by the day and their families are on it. Nobody east
  of here has ever needed one thing out of it.* Then: *That is all I have got about it and I have never had reason to
  look at it.*
- **Ch 350, the man who keeps the list:** *There is a line out west of this gate about nine miles. It is kept. There are
  men on it and they are paid by the day and their families are on it, and it has been there longer than my board.*
  *Nobody on this ground has ever needed one thing out of it.*
- **Ch 353, the carter, who has driven past the end of it four hundred times:** *I have driven past the end of it four
  hundred times and I have never once been let onto it. Men go out on it and come back. They are paid by the day.
  Their families are on it. Nobody out here has ever needed one thing off it.* Then: *I do not know what is at the top
  of it and I am not the man who would find out.*
- **Ch 357, the man with the brush, at the finding, and it is four sentences and he does not know he is saying it:**
  *There is a line out west of this building about nine miles. It is kept and there are men on it and they are paid by
  the day and their families are on it. Nobody out here has ever needed one thing out of it. You are not the first
  person on this floor to be a person a road went past, and you will not be the last.*

### The Hunter — on the page, ch 347 only

**Nine feet off a cart track on the thirteenth of a month at about the fourth hour, notebook shut under his arm, no
other paper of any kind.** He buys nothing and he asks nothing and she asks **him**, for the first time in five months,
one question: *What is a room with a date on it.* He answers in the ordinary voice — **yes**; there are four rooms on
the ground he has been in, two of them shut, both on the last day of a month, and neither shut because of anything he
wrote. Then, unasked, four lines a day and nine hundred pages nobody reads and a bundle that goes in with the district
at the end of a month, and **his paper is still in with the district and a writ is not issued to a field agent, and he
is still here and will not come inside nine feet again unless she puts him there.**

**She wanted him to be angry with her and he was not, and there was no handle on him anywhere.** **His request is
still pending. A Wall writ is not issued to a field agent. The line's own people still do not know she exists.**
Nothing about his paper, his order or his brother is raised, and **being sent for — his condition since ch 338 — is
not resolved, is not spoken of, and does not happen off-page.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 360 — the last page of Volume 6

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** across ch 346–360, because no card read it and
the allowance was zero · the Clay Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the
relicensed licence **never opened** (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never
raised · **the year of non-forgiveness carrying and not mentioned once in these fifteen chapters** · Shen Xiu
unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero on the page and still in two heads · what became of the eleven not raised · **no office
named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the copying floor entered, described or named** · the surface answer not
re-voiced · the seventh condition never listed, counted or referred to · the four months erased in Chapter 3 carried
and never named · the claim not reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page · Luo Ping never met · Ning Wu
does not appear · **Wei Ran does not appear** · Feng does not appear and **no second Lung and no second appointment** ·
no harem and nothing a romance · she never says her mother's name · **no new enemy and no named villain** · the Ledger
never ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about (`Ledger` returns **zero** across the batch) · **merit does not
settle in this batch and nothing is killed and no figure of what is left of him is on any page** · Yuan addressed as a
holder at ch 338 **stands and is not undone**, and the date of the morning he died is not given back and ch 272's
finding is not softened · **the sending is refused and not the act of ch 329, and neither chapter states the other** ·
**no warrant, no demand, no confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending,
no crossing, no audit, no examination, no signature** — the pressure of this batch is standing and refusing to stand
and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Volume 6, going into the volume close and into Volume 7

- **She is in three books she cannot see and has just learned that the third is a copy of the second made upward out
  of its own office by somebody she will never meet.**
- **She has refused, out loud, in daylight, with her name said, and the refusal is entered in the office's own book
  with a day against it, and it is in a building she cannot reach.**
- **Her own book is standing on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post on the mill road, and no office that can
  read it will ever know where it is.**
- **The circuit is broken and the road now runs both ways**: strips down that do not fold, and now a book.
- **He is addressed and therefore sendable**, at a price he named in advance, and **being sent for is a thing that
  can happen to him in principle and the volume does not resolve it and does not have it happen off-page.**
- **The reach has begun, once, and does not come back, and an office has written it down in a hand she cannot read.**
- **The Hunter's request is pending. The line's own people still do not know she exists. Nothing above the copying
  floor has been entered, and nothing above the second office, and nothing above the Standing Office of the Second
  Account.** The register still reads *reconciled* and nothing has read it in two months.

was opened.**

**One discrepancy between the batch prompt and the page, resolved in favour of the prompt, as the prompt directs:**
the prompt carried the corrected handoff and the page followed it. **The batch opens after day 773 and not at day 736
or day 749**, ch 346 is the eleventh of the month that ch 345's tenth belongs to, and every pinned day in the prompt —
786, 818 and 845 — went on the page at the month-day the prompt's own rule gives it. **One thing the prompt did not
have and the page built: the two twenty-eighths.** The sending's fourth line carries a date and the batch is a
countdown to it, with the first twenty-eighth (day 791) passing in ch 354 with nothing on any road and the second
(day 821) passing two days after the refusal at ch 358.

**One thing recorded rather than repaired.** Ch 349 says the middle-bank method had been unused for four months because
nothing on nine days of road weighed anything. The first draft was corrected to *there had been nothing on nine days
of road that weighed anything until a footing came off a board about eight days before*, because ch 341 and ch 342
both have her carrying four dressed pieces under one arm up a bank of four feet five hundred times, and the longer
sentence would have contradicted them. **The underlying fact stands and is not repaired: the last time she worked a
middle bank proper was a bank nine days west in Volume 5, and nothing on this ground has weighed anything since.**

## Volume 6 Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-06-batch-0004.review.log`)

**Eighteen findings, eight of them must-fix. Every one is applied. No beat, day-table position, title, turn,
midpoint, climax, irreversible decision, bracket, stage or planned event moved, and no chapter before
`chapters/volume-06/chapter-0345.md` was opened. The review's own *what passes* list was not re-litigated: two
brackets, closed speech in all fifteen files, zero markdown violations, zero bold or italic, 360 unique titles and
all fifteen original word counts were confirmed and re-measured after the repair.**

### The eight must-fix findings

1. **Ch 348 narrated one day twice.** *The First Day On The Footing* ran the seventh hour, five feet in, the lost
   hour of thinking and eleven trips up the low side; *What She Did With The Rest Of The Day* then ran the same
   hours again about 150 words later. **The exact-duplicate scan could not see it because the two passes are not
   identical** — which is why the batch's recorded *0 substantive duplicates* was true and useless. **The second
   section is now the seventeenth and is a different day:** the face off and piled at the bottom for two days, and
   what was left being the other half of the job, which is putting the stone back. **The heading changed with it** to
   *The Seventeenth, And The Low Side*. The first pass, the hour she lost, and the eleven trips are untouched.
2. **Ch 348 gave away Golden Core 8.** *A bar that took two hands* appeared on the fifteenth, eight chapters before
   the stage that costs it and in the one chapter that says out loud she would not get there. **Now *a bar in one
   hand*,** which is what a body at Golden Core 7 has and what ch 349 and ch 352 both rely on.
3. **Ch 358's deadline was the wrong day.** The opening said *the thirtieth of a month was two days off* on the
   twenty-sixth; the chapter then says the twenty-eighth three more times, and the twenty-eighth is the batch's
   pinned deadline and the date on the strip. **Now the twenty-eighth.**
4. **Ch 358 put her in a room she has never been in.** *She had been in a room with two shut doors about three weeks
   ago and had been told what the room looked like by the only person who has ever been in one* — she has never been
   in that room, the refusal exists **because** she cannot produce him, ch 347 has her standing eleven feet off a man
   in a wet field, and the Hunter describes four rooms on ground he has been in, so *the only person who has ever been
   in one* is false. **Now:** ten weeks at her ribs for the fourth line, and six weeks ago eleven feet off a man
   with a notebook in ten inches of wet grass, told of four rooms and two that shut, and not told what one looked
   like and **not able to ask**. Day 776 to day 819 is six weeks and not three.
5. **Ch 358's duration was ch 360's figure.** *Thirteen weeks* for carrying the strip is right at day 845 and wrong
   at day 819, where the strip came down on day 750. **Now ten weeks.** ch 355 and ch 356 keep their six weeks.
6. **Ch 354's last line was wrong under every reading.** *Twenty-nine days to the next one* — ch 354 ends on day 795
   and the next stage is day 818. **Now twenty-three**, and it no longer contradicts ch 357's own *Thirty-two days.
   Not thirty-seven* sitting under the twenty-eighth paragraph.
7. **Ch 349 broke the point of view and pre-announced ch 352.** *She did not know that yet and would find it out on
   the twenty-third of a month on open ground with the bar lying on the grass* is close third narrating what the POV
   character does not know, three chapters early, in the one chapter whose subject is not knowing. **Now:** the
   footing is two days on a board going bare inside two months, and what she knew on the crest was only the pleasure
   and a bad feeling about the pleasure. **The cost of the bar is no longer foreshadowed anywhere.**
8. **Five all-caps standalone lines read as System panels**, and **two of them were the book's ordinary hand, not
   the fine hand** — ch 356's was her own refusal written out with the flat of her own finger, and ch 358's was the
   clerk's margin entry in the ordinary hand. This volume spends six brackets in sixty-two chapters on stages only.
   **All five are sentence case inside curly quotation marks** — ch 348's fourth line, ch 355's fifth line, ch 356's
   three written lines and the head of the sheet on the top step, and ch 358's margin entry — and each is now
   carried by the hand it is in, named in the sentence around it. **Two bracketed layer lines remain and no more.**

### The ten should-fix findings

9. **The structural answer arrived as four near-identical speeches** (ch 346, 350, 353, 357; fuzzy overlap 0.43–0.68).
   Four mouths is right and four copies of one paragraph is not. **All four now say something different.** ch 346
   keeps the fullest version and the *nobody east of here has ever needed one thing out of it*, as the first statement
   in a worker's mouth. **ch 350's listing man** talks about his board and his gateway — *it was there before this
   yard had a board · men go out on it in the morning and their families eat off it · not one line of work has ever
   come in off it to this yard*. **ch 353's carter** keeps the paid-by-the-day line and replaces the closer with *in
   nineteen years I have watched that road from this side and not one of them has ever come off it with anything in
   his hands*. **ch 357's man with the brush** replaces the traffic-of-the-line clause with the thing only he knows —
   what is on it is paid for by the day out of a place he has never seen, and **every month-end the ordinary traffic
   of it comes past his floor on a cart and is read against other things**, which sets up his own finding two
   sections later. **Worst surviving cross-chapter overlap in the batch is 0.50**, between two flat-stone sentences
   that are the same beat in the same book.
10. **The middle-bank method was explained twice**, ch 349 in full and again at ch 352, and once more inside ch 349
    itself. **ch 349's explanation now states the rule and its first-day scene shows it** — the two passages that
    repeated each other word for word are split, and a short physical beat was added (the gully coming out flat four
    feet down, three pieces lifted at the wrong angle in two days, three quarters of an hour to take the third out a
    foot further along). **ch 352 now carries only the deduction it needs**: four words she cannot get past, one hand
    on the bar and one hand on the stone, and she does not have two hands.
11. **Ch 352 and ch 357 framed their stages in near-identical sentences** (overlap 0.70). **ch 352 keeps the full
    frame** — *the seven before it*, the sound going into the ground, the dog a mile off. **ch 357 is recast**: it
    came the way it always came, out of the ground she was standing on and not out of her, and it said it once and
    gave her nothing to read and nothing to do about it.
12. **Ch 357 named the wrong anchor for its own arithmetic.** It opened *Golden Core seven had been on the sixteenth
    of a month two months back · Thirty-two days* — thirty-two is Golden Core 8 to Golden Core 9, and Golden Core 7 to
    Golden Core 9 is sixty-nine, so a reader doing the chapter's own sum got a contradiction. **Now *Golden Core eight
    had been on the twenty-third of a month a month back*,** which is day 786, and 818 − 786 is thirty-two, and
    *Not thirty-seven* is the Golden Core 7 to Golden Core 8 interval it is contrasting against.
13. **Three stage-counting bases in three consecutive chapters.** ch 353's absolute run — five stages, six stages, the
    seventh took the bar — was right and ch 351's list of six stages named only five things. **ch 351's speech now
    carries six items**, ending *and then the heavy end*, which is what ch 353 attributes to the sixth. All four
    chapters now run the same frame: ch 352's *the seven before it*, ch 357's *the way it always came*, ch 353's
    five/six/seventh, ch 351's the last six. **No stage count moved.**
14. **A narrator absolute eight chapters before the reach.** ch 352's *It will be like that for the rest of her life*
    hands ch 360 its ending. **Now past tense and unarguable:** *She could not argue with the shape of it. The bar
    she had learned to carry one-handed … had asked her for two hands on the fourth hour of a morning on open
    ground, and she did not have two hands, and the heavy end was not coming back.* ch 352's own next line,
    *and it is closed for good*, was already the safer register and is untouched.
15. **Nascent Soul never appeared on the page**, and ch 357's cost being *nothing went* meant two consecutive stages
    landed with no name and no visible cost. **ch 360 now carries a section called *The Word At The Head Of It*:** the
    fine hand put a word at the head of those four or five lines and she cannot read a letter of it, and she knows
    the word — *the one after Golden Core, Nascent Soul, the fifth*, a name she has known since before she could count
    to nine — and she went into it three days ago on a bank behind a mill with four men two hundred yards off and
    nothing said out loud. **It is a name on a page, not a panel. The bracket count is unchanged at two and ch 360
    still carries none, and the silence when it happened is unbroken.**
16. **Two relative-date expressions were mangled.** ch 350's *since the eighth of the ninth of the month before last*
    reads as two days and named none; **now *for about a month*.** ch 348's *since the first of the month a fortnight
    before the twenty-second of the month before* named three dates and meant one; **now *the first of the month
    before last*.**
17. **Nobody had become the batch's default intensifier** — 27 instances, while all seven exact banned phrases stayed
    at zero, so the letter of the budget held and the effect did not. **Down to 6**, twenty-one recast across ten
    chapters: *by itself* where a furnace lights itself, *not one person*, *no one*, *not a living soul*, *not a living
    thing within half a mile*. **The six that remain are each load-bearing** — ch 346's is the structural answer in
    its first and fullest form, ch 351's two are a woman alone in the dark and a warning there is nobody to give, and
    ch 358, 359 and 360's are about there being nobody to tell.
18. **Minor date slips, all in the class the review named.** ch 359's *nine days after the first cart had gone up* is
    **eight days** — day 824 to day 832. ch 346 carried no date on the page and now opens on **the eleventh**;
    ch 349 carried none and now carries **the eighteenth** in its opening and its first-day beat. **Four more slips of
    the same class were found while fixing these and are fixed with them:** ch 354's *eleven days ago* on a bank is
    **eight**; ch 354's *wrong about the sixth stage* is the **seventh**, which is the stage that took the bar;
    ch 356's *the eleventh of the month before last* is **the month last**; and ch 355's *about four weeks* before the
    twenty-eighth is **about three weeks**. Two adjacent intervals were tightened with them: ch 355's
    *three weeks before that a man had refused* became *in the three weeks before it*, which is the same twenty-two
    days and not a second twenty-one; and ch 359's *a refusal she gave at a counter nine days ago* is **a fortnight
    ago**.

### One false figure the review could not reach

**The batch's construction budget recorded `sending: 7` and it is 0.** The row counted the substring inside *send*,
*sent* and *sends*. The same row recorded `sent for: 2` where the page has **send for: 1 and sent for: 0**. Both rows
are corrected in the table above. **This is the failure the card forbids in the direction nobody watches for**: the
card says no budget may record zero while instances stand on the page, and a budget that records seven where there
are none is the same lie with the sign flipped.

### Re-measured after the repairs

**21,479 words of body prose over fifteen chapters, average ~1,432**, none under the 1,300 floor and none above the
2,700 guide. **Volume 6 file set: 94,946 words of body prose over sixty-two chapters.** **Exactly two bracketed
layer lines**, at ch 352 and ch 357, character for character as pinned; zero in the other thirteen. **Every speech
closed in all fifteen files, zero straight apostrophes and zero straight double quotes** (one straight apostrophe
was introduced in a ch 356 repair sentence and was swept before the batch was saved again). **Zero** blockquotes,
code fences, tables, horizontal rules, bold and italic spans. **Zero** all-caps standalone lines in the batch. **Zero** hits on
authorial scaffolding, *about four seconds*, *eleven weeks*, the seven banned phrases, generic web prose, the
retired zero-list terms, and the batch's zero-list vocabulary. **`nobody`: 6**, down from 27, each load-bearing.
**Longest paragraph in the batch is 89 words**, none over ninety. **No chapter-to-itself duplicate pair** under the
fuzzy scan; worst surviving cross-chapter overlap 0.50, which is two sentences that are supposed to rhyme.
**360 `#` headings across the manuscript, zero collisions, and no chapter title changed.** Month-day arithmetic
re-derived for every pinned day under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`: **786 is the 23rd, 818 the 25th, 845 the 22nd**,
unchanged, and every repaired relative interval in this batch now agrees with the day table.

### Recorded and not repaired

- **`She took her hand off.`** stands as a five-word paragraph at ch 359 and again at ch 360, and the exact-match scan
  at eight words could not see it. **It was left alone.** One is her hand coming off the cover of her own book in a
  building a mile up a road and the other is her hand coming off a stone on a bank in a field, and the echo is
  deliberate: the same gesture, the same woman, nine days of road from anything that would have written either down.
- **ch 346's opening still carries six lines and five more to come**, while the same chapter's board shows four, and
  ch 350 later reads four. **Left alone.** It was true when written and it is a *yard's* arithmetic given by a
  narrator-adjacent register in the first two hundred words of the batch; correcting it means choosing which of three
  numbers the writer meant, and no downstream beat, date or payment turns on it. **Written down so the next reader
  does not have to find it again.**

and findings are under `## Volume 6 close audit — CLOSED`.

### Two claims in the close card that were wrong against the page

**The page wins in both cases, as the card directs. Neither is repaired; both are recorded.**

1. **The card says Batch 0003 used four structural-answer mouths, at ch 331, 335, 337 and 345. It used three.**
   `grep` for *west*, *paid by the day*, *families* and *kept by men* across ch 345 returns nothing of the kind:
   ch 345's mouth is the keeper with the paste pot and his whole card is about strips and boards. **The fourth
   Batch 0003 mouth recorded in the *Established facts, Volume 6 Batch 0003* block above and in
   `workspace/volume-06-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` does not exist on the page.** The volume has **seven** structural
   answer mouths, not eight: ch 331, 335, 337, 346, 350, 353 and 357.
2. **The card says none of Batch 0004's four is the same mouth as Batch 0003's four. One of them is** — ch 357's
   is the man with the brush, who is Batch 0003's mouth at ch 331, in the same room, twenty-six chapters earlier.
   **The chapter was repaired** (repair 5 above) so that the two no longer open or close on the same words, and
   **both still say what the volume needs them to say**, because the mouth being the same man at the finding is the
   finding's set-up: he tells her that the ordinary traffic of that line goes past his floor on a cart at the end
   of every month and is read against other things. **The card's claim is wrong and is recorded as wrong.**

### Recorded at the close and not repaired

Seven findings, with reasons, are in `state/current.md` under `### Seven findings recorded and not repaired`. The
three that a Volume 7 drafter is most likely to trip over:

- **The only exact duplicate paragraph standing in the volume** is *A line is a thing that gets found / A holding
  is a thing that can be asked for* at ch 335 and ch 338. **It is planted in her head at ch 335 and paid in his room
  at ch 338**, where the chapter adds the third sentence that is the whole of ch 338. A refrain that is planted and
  then answered is this book's method, not its repetition.
- **The keeper's figure at ch 345 and ch 359 is near-verbatim in the same mouth.** The first time it is a man's
  record; the second time it is his record with one thing standing on a stone under his ladder. The echo is the
  chapter's effect.
- **`nobody` stands 88 times across sixty-two chapters, and the wider family with *no one* and *not one* at 142** —
  *no one* 4, *not one* 50, of which *not one person* is 15. About 2.3 a chapter, maximum seven in one chapter (ch
  307 and ch 329), both of them chapters where the structural answer or the vouching is being stated in full.
  **This is the one measured number in the volume above where the book's own guidance would put it**, it is
  distributed rather than concentrated, and cutting sixty instances across four closed batches at a volume close is a
  rewrite rather than an audit. *(Corrected at the close's review: this gave 191 and no reading of the strings
  reaches it.)*

## Volume 6 close — review repair

**The Volume 6 close was reviewed and came back with six findings: three prose faults and three bad measurements,
plus a file-count contradiction and a stale figure carried inside one state file. Five are repaired and one is
recorded. Full reasoning is in `state/current.md` under `## Volume 6 close review — REPAIR`.**

**Three chapters were opened and no other. No beat, day-table position, title, turn, midpoint, climax, irreversible
decision, bracket or Golden Core / Nascent Soul stage moved, and no chapter before `chapter-0299.md` was opened.**

| File | Fault | Repair | Words |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **ch 338** | **The close's own repair 12 introduced a fresh verbatim duplication.** After the all-caps name was put into sentence case, the standalone *He has never seen that name written.* sat three lines above a paragraph opening *He has never seen that name written. He has read it four thousand times…* | The paragraph now opens *He has read it four thousand times in a layer, and he has never once seen a person put it down on anything **with a brush**.* The brush is already on the page twice in this chapter, so the added clause closes onto the hand at the table instead of repeating the line above it | −4 |
| **ch 332** | **A layer-silence line still byte-identical in construction to ch 342**, and its opening clause *The layer said it once in the far-side house form and stopped* is verbatim the opening of ch 310's line | *The sentence came out in the far-side house form, the same length as the five that had come before it, and then there was nothing after it. She had stopped listening to it by the third one. All she did now was wait it out.* | +19 |
| **ch 342** | **The other half of that twin pair.** Same clause sequence, same three negatives, four chapters from ch 332 | *Seven.* / *That was the count now, and the layer gave her the same sentence in the far-side house form it had given her at the first of them, and she was already walking toward the bar before the last of it was out.* | +14 |

**Both halves of the twin pair were recast, not one.** Recasting only ch 342 would have left ch 332 echoing ch 310
instead, and the close's claim that the six post-bracket lines all differ would still have been false. **All six now
differ**: ch 310 keeps the full statement of the rule, ch 321, 332, 342, 352 and 357 each say it in their own shape.
**The rule is not weakened** — ch 310 is the first of the six and is where the reader needs the whole of it, which
is also what `AGENTS.md` asks for: state a rule once, then dramatize it.

**The one-word paragraph *Seven.* at ch 342 is the chapter's own idiom**, not an invention: the chapter is *Five
Counts*, it already stands on *Four. Four. Four.* at the top and *Four.* in the middle, and it is the seventh stage.
The line ends on her walking toward the bar, which is the first line of the section that follows.

### Two state-file faults repaired, and one condition carried

1. **`state/current.md` contradicted itself on the file count.** One cold-reader block said the close made its
   repairs across *eleven* chapter files. Seven other locations in four files, and `git`, say **seventeen**.
   Corrected to seventeen.
2. **A stale figure inside one file.** `state/open-threads.md` gave Batch 0001 as 24,531 four lines below its own
   header stating 24,529 post-close. Corrected to 24,529, and the two sibling headers at `state/current.md` still
   carrying pre-close Batch 0001 (24,531) and Batch 0002 (23,931, with its old average of ~1,496) were corrected in
   the same pass. **Historical per-batch records elsewhere in the state files are left at their batch-time figures on
   purpose** — that is what a batch record is — and are not rewritten here.
3. **Recorded, not repaired: all 31 files ch 330–360 have no trailing newline.** Confirmed present in `HEAD~1`, so
   it was inherited from Batch 0003 and is not a fault the close introduced. A trailing newline changes no word and
   no line; touching 31 chapter files at a volume close to fix a whitespace artefact produces a diff nobody can read.
   **Carried forward as a known condition.** Separately, `state/phase-ledger.json` is referenced by both prompts but
   is neither committed nor gitignored — it is created by the controller at runtime, and controller-owned files are
   not edited here.

## Volume 7 planned — what `outline/volume-07.md` fixed, and what Batch 0001 inherits

**Recorded by the Volume 7 planning phase. No chapter prose was written in it. `outline/series.md` and
`outline/ending.md` were not opened and are unchanged. `outline/volume-07.md` exists and is 786 lines;
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md` is its first drafting card and is the only prompt that phase created.**

**Nothing in this block is measured against ch 361–374, because no Volume 7 chapter exists. Everything below is
the plan, and the first drafting phase's construction budget is measured against the plan and against the chapters
of Volume 6, not against a promise.**

### The fixed items

- **Title, range and goal are fixed by `outline/series.md` §9 and were not re-planned:** *Stronger Things*,
  ch 361–420, sixty chapters.
- **The volume runs day 849 to day 1,090 — 242 days over sixty chapters**, against Volume 6's 228 days over
  sixty-two. **Month-day is `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`, day 134 was the first of a month, and months are thirty
  days.** Months in Volume 7 begin on days 854 · 884 · 914 · 944 · 974 · 1,004 · 1,034 · 1,064.
- **Four batches, fourteen / fifteen / fifteen / sixteen chapters**, pressure: rooms bigger than she is · what she
  is worth in a room · the reach on purpose · what it costs to be right. **No two consecutive, and none of them a
  tending, a ration, a hiring, a crossing, an audit, an examination, a hunt, a signature, a keeping, a debt, a
  family paper, an ash or a confinement.**

### The day table, every pinned day re-derived under the month rule

| Stage | Ch | Day | Month-day | Interval | Cost, named once |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Nascent Soul 1 (already on the page) | 360 | 845 | the twenty-second of the month beginning 824 | — | spent at ch 360: the surprise does not come back |
| **Nascent Soul 2** | **371** | **886** | the **third** of the month beginning 884 | **41** | **the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone** |
| **Nascent Soul 3** | **383** | **924** | the **eleventh** of the month beginning 914 | **38** | **her hand stops being able to lie** |
| **Nascent Soul 4** | **394** | **960** | the **seventeenth** of the month beginning 944 | **36** | **a thing she reaches cannot be put back where it was** |
| **Nascent Soul 5** | **404** | **999** | the **twenty-sixth** of the month beginning 974 | **39** | **she cannot be given anything** |
| **Nascent Soul 6** | **412** | **1,037** | the **fourth** of the month beginning 1,034 | **38** | **she cannot be paid** |
| **Nascent Soul 7** | **420** | **1,076** | the **thirteenth** of the month beginning 1,064 | **39** | **the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into** |

- **Six stages in about eight months, real intervals of 41, 38, 36, 39, 38 and 39 days, no skipped breakthrough,
  no tribulation.** Volume 6 ran seven stages in about seven months at 38, 36, 38, 37, 37, 32 and 27. **The 41 is
  the longest interval in either volume and the ch 371 chapter is required to say why it is that long** — the board
  was bare, the season had not turned, and a stage needs material she had no way to pay for.
- **None of the six costs is a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a missing finger**, so none collides with the
  body's standing injuries. **Three of the six are not losses at all** — the hand that cannot lie, the gift that
  cannot be taken and the pay that cannot be entered are three doors closing rather than three things taken away,
  and **two of those three do not touch her body in any way. None undoes a Volume 6 closure:** the furnace is not
  un-finished, it simply stops lighting itself, which is a different fact and a worse one; the heavy end stays
  closed; the bar stays a two-handed tool; the refusal at ch 358 stands in its book and nothing takes it out.
- **The series-table cost of Nascent Soul — *she can no longer be surprised by a person* — is already spent at
  ch 360 and is not re-spent, re-explained or softened anywhere in Volume 7.**

### Bracket budget for Volume 7, honest and checkable by grep

**Six in sixty. One at each Nascent Soul stage, at ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420, and nowhere else.** The far-side
house form is Volume 6's, character for character, with only the realm and the number changed:

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage two, is held.]

**Volume 6 spent six in sixty-two and none at Nascent Soul 1, because the handover was entered by hand and that
silence was the point. The form does not change at any of Volume 7's six.** Ch 394 is the one chapter that hears
the last four words of it and finds they have stopped being true — there is no county within nine days of road —
and she thinks it, and the layer does not change.

### The announcement rule, re-derived for Volume 7

**The rule did not change. The listeners did.** The layer still announces; the sound still goes into the ground,
because there is nobody inside a bound to be told and no county book to take it. **What changed is that the rooms
past the mill have learned to look.** A stage used to reach a strip about three days later, in the fine hand, at a
gate post. **From ch 371 it happens in daylight in front of two or three people on that ground**, and after that it
is a thing discussed in rooms she is not in, at figures she cannot check.

**The consequence that drives the volume: from ch 371 she is entered as a body and not only as a column, and that
is the reason she is the weakest thing in every room she walks into.** Nobody announces it. A man at a bar looks at
her and works out where she goes in the order.

### The protagonist, and the second rung of *Breaking*

- **`outline/series.md` §4 gives him Hand across Volumes 6–8.** Volume 6 took the first volume of it. Volume 7
  takes the second half and the second rung.
- **The second rung is the direction of a sending.** At ch 402 the holding is used, by him, on purpose, for the
  first time, to move an office. **What breaks is the assumption that a line on that ground can only be read
  downward.** The Wall is not opened, the Order is not on the page, and no office above the copying floor or the
  second office is named or entered.
- **The third frame scene is pinned at ch 401**, away from her hearing, in his own frame. **He has been on the
  page that way exactly twice in six volumes, at ch 272 and ch 338.** The third makes him **answerable**, which is
  a third thing and worse than being sendable, because an answer can be quoted.
- **Hand is spent once (ch 402) and refused once, out loud (ch 419).** Each spending is told before spent, out of
  her foundation, and does not come back. **Nothing in Volume 7 kills anybody.**
- **Merit settles three times** — once in Batch 0001, once in Batch 0003, once in Batch 0004 — each on a listed
  piece of work cleared past the mill, each share told before spent. **No figure of what is left of him is stated
  on any page**, and the last figure in the manuscript is still *about six weeks* at ch 236.
- **Ledger is never ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about**, and `grep -i Ledger` returns **0** across
  all sixty-two files of Volume 6 and returns 0 on Volume 7's page. It took two close repairs at ch 306 and ch 319
  to make Volume 6's zero true rather than approximately true, so this is measured and not asserted.

### Two irreversible decisions

1. **Ch 388, hers.** She says a person's name out loud in a room and asks for something — the first time in ten
   months she has asked anybody for anything. It is entered in a room that keeps things and it cannot be unsaid.
2. **Ch 417, hers, and it is the volume's own.** She declines to know what the fine hand says about her. A denial
   is an entry, so she goes on not knowing, forever, on purpose. **Nothing is written anywhere, no book fails to
   shut and no circuit breaks — the bill is entirely hers.**

**Volume 6's break was a circuit broken (ch 344), Volume 6's refusal was a permanent entry in a building she
cannot reach (ch 358), and Volume 7's refusal is an answer withheld (ch 417): three acts in three consecutive
volumes, three different shapes, none a restatement of another.**

### The structural answer, continued and not paid

**Six mouths, six new people, none of them one of Volume 6's seven, four or five sentences each, never a speech:**
a carter (ch 365) · a gate man (ch 379) · **Rao**, the column-keeper (ch 391) · a woman who drives clay (ch 398) ·
a copying man one floor below Rao (ch 409) · **Geng**, who runs the lifting floor (ch 415). **The tenth part is not
given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** The origin answer (Volume 11) and the moral answer (Volumes 15–16) are
not previewed, and the surface answer is not re-voiced.

### Three new named people, chosen so none collides with an existing one

**Rao** (keeps the columns on the third floor, sixteen years — the midpoint's reversal) · **Dai** (whose job it is
to read the fine hand — offers once, refused once, right both times) · **Geng** (runs the lifting floor — strong,
correct, and a block short for a week). **`Mei`, `Xie` and `Du` were rejected as bare surnames because the
manuscript already has Mei Jiu, Lu Mei, Xie Shuo, Xie Qiao and Du Hong inside the bound.** **The keeper with the
paste pot stays unnamed**, as he was in Volume 6, and is given the heaviest scene in Volume 7.

### Construction budget carried into Volume 7 — measured against Volume 6, not promised

Every figure in the right-hand column of `outline/volume-07.md` § *The construction budget, carried from Volume 6
and re-measured* is a Volume 6 measurement or a stated Volume 7 allowance. **The two that bind hardest:**

- **The bracket budget: six in sixty, at ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420, and zero anywhere else.** Volume 6's
  was six in sixty-two at ch 310, 321, 332, 342, 352 and 357 with none at ch 360. **A budget that records zero while
  instances stand is the failure this manuscript has caught itself making twice, and a budget that records a count
  where there is none is the same lie with the sign flipped — which is how the Batch 0004 `sending: 7` row was
  wrong and had to be corrected.**
- **The `nobody` family: nobody 88 · no one 4 · not one 50 · total 142** across Volume 6's sixty-two chapters,
  about 2.3 a chapter. **The three intensifiers are the family and they total 142; `family`/`families` is a
  separate word and stands at 6.** *(An earlier record in this file, and the copies inherited by
  `outline/volume-07.md` and `workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, read the last figure as `family 142`,
  which is a transcription of `total 142` and not a measurement. Corrected at the planning review.)*
  **That is the one measured number in Volume 6 above the book's own guidance** and Volume 7's
  allowance is **under sixty across sixty chapters**, which is about one a chapter. The true count is written into
  the continuity block whatever it is.

**Volume 6's zeros, re-measured at this planning phase against `chapters/volume-06/` and unchanged:**
`reconciled` **0** · `plate` **0** · `licence` **0** · `license` **0** · `clearance` **0** ·
`countersignature` **0** · `kiln-born` **0** · `MEMBER ABSENT` **0** · `Ledger` **0** · `tribulation` **0** ·
`Feng` **0** · `nobody` **88**. **360 `#` headings across the manuscript and zero collisions**, against which
Volume 7's sixty titles must be unique. **Wei Ran appears once in Volume 6, at ch 302, and Volume 7 gives her at
most two cards and nothing more than that.**

### What Batch 0001 inherits, in one paragraph

**Day 849, the twenty-sixth of a month beginning on day 824. At a gate and a shed three miles east of a mill whose
wheel has not turned.** Nascent Soul 1, taken by hand and not by sound on day 845 with no bracket, and a reach that
began once and does not come back. Four fingers' grip on one hand, the right arm open half the time, eight of eleven
holds, five counts of breath on a loaded reach, the bar needing two hands, the licence never opened, the plate worn
and undescribed. **No wages, no season, no booking; a gate at one copper a day, bread five days in seven, about half
a box of clay, and the board at the works bare until the season turns. A furnace that lights itself.** Three books
that can read her, one of them a copy made upward in a hand she cannot read; a refusal entered in a counter's book
in a building she cannot reach; her own book on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post. He is readable, findable,
held and sendable, and Volume 7 opens being sent for at ch 402 and pays it out on the page.

### What Batch 0001 must not do, pinned

**One bracket, at ch 371, and no other.** No Hand — first spent ch 402, refused ch 419, both outside this batch. No
frame scene. **Being sent for is not opened, foreshadowed in his mouth, or had happen off-page.** No refusal — the
volume's is ch 417. No office named, no word of the Order, no Wei Ran, no Feng, no Ning Wu, no payment and no
listing. The register is not read, the plate is not described, MEMBER ABSENT is carried and not raised, the year of
non-forgiveness is not mentioned once, and no villain is created.

## Volume 7 planning — close review repair

**Six findings from `logs/volume-07-planning.review.log`, all applied. Every figure was re-measured against
`chapters/volume-06/` before it was written back. No chapter prose was touched, no beat, day, title, batch
range, pinned chapter, mouth, stage, bracket or irreversible step moved, and no Volume 7 chapter exists or was
invented.**

- **The `nobody` family row, in four files** (`workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `outline/volume-07.md`,
  this file, and `state/current.md`), read **`family 142`**. It is **`total 142`** — `nobody` 88, `no one` 4,
  `not one` 50 — and **`family`/`families` measures 6**. The mistranscription mattered: the `under 14 across
  fourteen` allowance is the drafter`s main defence against the bare-intensifier construction, and it was
  specified against a number 24x too high. The rows now name the three intensifiers the allowance governs and
  give both figures.
- **`vouch` is 6, not 4** — ch 357 x4 (lines 39, 79, 105 x2) and **ch 329 line 41**, *you have been vouched once
  already in a building with a stove*. `grep -c` returns 3 at ch 357 because line 105 carries two occurrences.
- **`send for` 4 / `sent for` 2 / `sending` 1**, against a recorded 1 / 0 / 0. At ch 301:65, 336:71, 337:69,
  338:133 (twice on the line), 338:167 and 355:59.
- **All-caps standalone lines is 2, not 0** — ch 338 lines 47 and 51, both `HELD.`. These are the layer`s own
  word set alone as a deliberate beat, not written matter in a hand, and they were never in the class of the seven
  the Volume 6 close repaired; all seven are sentence case and stay that way. The Batch 0001 prompt now carries
  the warning at the point where it orders the drafter to extend into ch 330–345, because ch 338 is the chapter
  that breaks its own rule and would otherwise be inherited as precedent.
- **`outline/volume-07.md:558` said `ch 378`** where its own structural-answer table at `:404` and five state
  files say **`379`**. One digit corrected to the majority and to the outline`s own table.
- **`outline/volume-07.md` is 786 lines**, not the 784 recorded here.
- **Forbidden-string greps now carry word boundaries.** Bare `Scribe` returns a false positive at ch 303 inside
  *described*, and `plate` and `Ledger` will do the same; the Volume 6 close was caught by this on `Ledger`.

**Not touched, and verified as correct rather than missed.** `state/continuity.md` lines 6008-6010 record `vouch`
4, `sending` 0, `send for` 1 and `sent for` 0. **Those rows are scoped to Batch 0004`s fifteen files** and
re-measured across ch 346-360 they return exactly those numbers. The findings were against the Volume 6-wide
rows in the prompt and the outline, not against these, and repairing them would have made a right measurement
wrong.

**Everything the reviewer listed as verified stays verified**: all day arithmetic under `((day - 134) mod 30) + 1`
(845, 848, 849, 899, 886, 924, 960, 999, 1,037, 1,076 and the intervals 41/38/36/39/38/39), the six bracketed layer

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0001 (ch 361–374) — ROOMS BIGGER THAN SHE IS

**Fourteen chapters, 27,114 words of body prose, average ~1,937, day 849 to day 899.** **ONE bracketed layer line,
at ch 371 — bracket 1 of Volume 7's six in sixty.** Chapter titles and section headings excluded. Drafted against
`outline/volume-07.md` and against `workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, which is this batch's card. **The
prompt's corrected handoff was right and the page followed it: the batch opens on day 849, the twenty-sixth, at the
gate, the day after ch 360 closed — not on the stage day and not on the day the strip came.**

Per file, body prose: 361 1,957 · 362 1,984 · 363 1,900 · 364 1,827 · 365 1,953 · 366 1,868 · 367 1,869 · 368 2,172 ·
369 1,885 · 370 2,063 · 371 1,963 · 372 1,899 · 373 1,913 · 374 1,861. **All fourteen inside the 1,800–2,700 guide,
none under the 1,300 floor and none above the ceiling.** Volume 7 file set after this batch: **27,114 words over
fourteen chapters.**

### The batch in one sentence

She carried a strip she could not read to a gate that paid a copper a day, watched a reading leave the only room on
nine days of road that could fetch anybody for it and go east nine miles, found the board full and every line on it a
line her right hand could not take, stood in a yard of nine men and was priced as a body in about two seconds, had a
bar go across her on a bank and stopped it with her own furnace, took her second stage in daylight in front of two
men, and lost the fire that had lit itself every morning for four months.

### The clock and the day table — every pinned day verified under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`

**Months in this batch: the month beginning day 824 runs to day 853, the month beginning day 854 runs to day 883,
and the month beginning day 884 runs to day 913.**

| Ch | Day / days on the page | Month-day | Title |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 361 | **849 (pinned)** | the 26th | The Beginning Of A Habit |
| 362 | 850–851 | the 27th–28th | The Third Sum |
| 363 | 851 | the 28th | Eleven Years Of Copying Down |
| 364 | 852–853 | the 29th–30th | Read Out Of The Room |
| 365 | **853–855** | the 30th, then the 1st and 2nd | Where The Cart Goes |
| 366 | 856–864 | the 3rd–11th | Two Different Facts |
| 367 | **864** | the 11th — **the season turns** | It Comes Back Full |
| 368 | 867–869 | the 14th–16th | **THE YARD OF NINE MEN** — The Ninth Man |
| 369 | 873–875 | the 20th–22nd | Four People Agree Later |
| 370 | **884–885** | the 1st–2nd | The Last Day It Lit Itself |
