- **Day 849 is the 26th of the month beginning on day 824. Day 886 is the third of the month beginning on day 884.
  Day 899 is the sixteenth of the same month.** Day 854 is the first of the month that the cart turned on.
  Day 856 is the third, day 857 the fourth, day 862 the ninth, day 864 the eleventh.
- **The prompt pinned ch 365 at days 853–856 and named the thirtieth, the first and the second. The page carries
  853–855, which are those three days.** The range is one day short of the prompt's and the page wins; recorded here
  rather than argued.
- **No named weekday, no named calendar month, no named week.** *A week*, *three days in seven*, *about a fortnight*,
  *a month ago* are ordinary relative stretches. **Four instances of the weekday were written at ch 368 and all four
  were cut before the batch was saved.**
- **No pay-cycle.** Nothing falls on a date on that ground.

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

| | Entering the batch (day 849) | At the last page (day 899) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gate at Pei’s | five coppers a reach, **a fifth of a reach, one copper a day**, taken out with a short iron out of the gate house because the bar takes two hands | **the same, one copper a day**, and **the drain has gone from four feet to three and a half** and Pei says so out loud on the sixteenth |
| Listed work | the board at the works bare for about a month | **full since the eleventh**, eleven lines, every one dressed stone priced by the ton, **and she can take none of them** |
| The yard at the turn | — | **a length three days in seven**, paid in a cup of something hot from a woman who has never asked her name. **Nothing in money. Nothing on a board. No name of hers anywhere in the yard** |
| Bread | two a day, five days in seven, store five miles west | **the same. Four loaves is eight coppers, by the store's own mouth at ch 362 and ch 366 and ch 372, and a loaf is two coppers. Thirteen days of gate at a copper a day is thirteen in; one trip to the store is eight out; five coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame at the end of them** |
| Furnace | lit by itself about half a box of clay left | **the ring will not come up.** Four barrow-loads of clay piled against the outside wall of the shed, a fifth of a sack in a barrow, and a fire that sits at about a finger's width |

- **The gate rate does not move in this batch and the reason is on the page at ch 361**: the bar takes two hands, so
  the gate has a short iron in it and no bar, and a fifth of a reach is what four feet of drain with a turn of an iron
  comes to. It was a third of a reach and one and a half coppers before Golden Core eight; that half copper went out of
  her life at ch 353 and did not come back here.
- **A month is thirty coppers and thirty is the whole of what this ground can pay her.** She works it out standing up
  on the twenty-seventh (ch 362): bread twenty, a third of a sack three, and **seven coppers over for a month**, and a
  third of a sack is three of the seven, and four coppers is two days of bread.
- **No board line is taken in this batch. No listing. No wages. No season ending on a date.** The pay question does
  not turn until Nascent Soul 6 at ch 412.

### The construction the batch is built on, and how it moves

| | Entering (day 849) | At the last page (day 899) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The board at the works | bare for about a month, cover on, battens across, key in a man’s pocket | **full since the eleventh of a month, eleven lines, all dressed stone by the ton** |
| The books she cannot see | three, one a copy made upward in a fine hand | **three, and the column in one of them has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months** |
| The reading | a strip in her coat with a word at the head of it she cannot read, and a date she had lived through | **the reading has left the room.** A strip went east on the first at a quarter past the sixth hour and did not come back on the cart that takes the empty trays up |
| The strip | folded in two against the oilcloth | **the same strip. It did not come back.** It is somewhere nine miles east and she is not going east of the turn |
| The furnace | lights itself at about the second hour, every morning, for four months | **will not light itself, and she cannot light it alone** |
| The yard | — | **nine men on a board where four would do. A rule in a hand. A hedge line closed and off** |

### Nascent Soul 2 — ch 371, day 886, the third of the month beginning 884

Taken standing, in the open, on the flat past the last hedge at about the seventh hour, **with nine men from the yard
on a bank about sixty feet long and two of them nine feet off her**. **Forty-one days after Nascent Soul 1 at day 845,
and the chapter says why it is the longest interval in the volume**: the board was bare, the season had not turned,
and a stage needs material she had no way to pay for.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage two, is held.]

**It is bracket 1 of Volume 7's six in sixty, and the form does not change at any of the six.**

**Cost, named once, permanent: the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone.** Not a limb, not a
hold, not a count of breath and not a missing finger. **A stage cannot be built by one pair of hands.** She tries it
nine times over about three hours with clay and with ash and with her hands flat on the brick and with her hands held
four inches off it, and it comes up a finger's width and stops every time.

**What this batch took out of her body, and it is on the page and measurable:** her hands go cold. They had been warm
for four months — warm the way a furnace is warm — and they went cold within about a moment of the bar stopping at
the third hour on the second of a month, and they are cold on the last page.

### The bank, the load, and the furnace — ch 370, day 885, the 2nd of the month beginning 884

The batch's physical-risk card and the set-up for the cost, on a pinned date, in the same chapter family as the stage.

- **A bank at about a mile and a quarter east of the mill, out on the flat past the last hedge**, a face about four
  feet, clay under stone, nine men on it, taken by the yard four miles east at the turn.
- **The bar goes.** Not a slip: it comes out of the face and comes down the low side with four pieces of the heavy
  stone on it, and she is on the low side because she has come round the end of the face to fill a barrow with the
  loose. About a ton, about nine inches of air under her shoulders, and the nearest man four feet off and two seconds
  away.
- **What stops it is the furnace in the shed.** She lifts — the thing she learned at a bank nine days west from a
  woman with a scar across the back of both hands — and it comes up out of the ground under her ribs and goes under
  what is on top of her, and it is not her arms and it is not her hands and nothing about it can be looked at. **The
  bar stops a finger's width** and two strong men take the rest of it in two seconds.
- **The fire does not come up on the next morning, and it does not come up on any of the thirteen days after it.**
- **Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Merit settles once, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **Ch 370, the second hour of the first of a month.** A hedge line at the east end of the yard, four hundred yards,
  by the length, one day, **closed and checked and off on the twentieth.** Done by her, with her hands, off nobody's
  book, for no money.
- **Not a death.** There is no death in it and there has not been one in her accounting since the crossing.
- **The share is six days and four hours and it is said out loud before it is spent, in his words, twice.** He tells
  her he has not said that number out loud in four years and he says it to her because she is going to spend it
  before she has had a night with it and he would rather she spent it having heard it.
- **She spends it on nothing.** Not on a hold, not on a bar, not on her hours, not on the reach. It goes out of the
  ground under her into the floor of a shed, out of her foundation, and does not come back.
- **The price is the one nobody can name or measure, and the page shows it rather than measures it: the ring stops
  coming up the following morning.**
- **No Hand is spent in this batch. `Ledger` returns zero across all fourteen files.**

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing and advising nothing

Utterances outside the second hour of ch 370: **ch 362 (one) · ch 364 (two) · ch 370 (four) · ch 371 (none) ·
ch 369 (two)**. **Ch 370 carries the share, told twice, plus the four sentences about it; every one is in the present
tense and none of it buys anything.** Nothing at ch 361, 363, 365, 366, 367, 368, 372, 373 or 374 except the four
sentences at the second hour on the sixteenth of ch 374, which are the arrangement and nothing more.

**He is readable without her and it is on the page and it costs nothing to say: at ch 362 he knows she has counted a
month three times and that she counted one of them wrong.** **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page
in this batch.** The date of the morning he died is not given back. The unnamed state is not given back. **Being sent
for is not opened, not foreshadowed in his mouth, and does not happen to anybody else.** No frame scene.

### The structural answer — mouth one of six, at ch 365, in the carter’s mouth

**First of Volume 7's six mouths and six new people.** A carter with a chain over his cart bed who drives the long
road east:

“I have been turned back at the end of it four hundred times. That is not a thing that happens to me. That is the
ordinary end of that road.” · “Turning back is what people pay for. They pay for the going out. They pay for the
stopping.” · “There is no part in the middle. And I have never asked what is up there, and a man who asks finds out
that somebody has an answer.”

Four or five sentences, in short sentences, not a speech, and **the tenth part is not given and nothing proves the
crews wrong.** It is a different statement from Volume 6's seven mouths and it is a different man: Volume 6's ch 353
carter drove past the end of the line and watched it from this side, and this one drives the long road east and has
been turned back at the end of it.

### The Hunter — on the page, ch 369 only

**Nine feet off the cart track two hundred yards west of the works, on the twenty-second of a month at about the
seventh hour, notebook shut under his arm, no other paper of any kind.** He buys nothing and he asks nothing, and she
asks **him**, which is what she has done since ch 347 and is the only handle there has ever been on him.

**What she asks:** what a room on this ground is for. **What he answers, in the ordinary voice, at no cost to either
of them:** *A room is where a thing is put so that four people can agree about it later.* Then: *I am still here, and
I am still buying nothing, and I am not going to tell you why, and you are not going to ask me.*

**His request stays pending. A writ is not issued to a field agent. The line’s own people still do not know she
exists.** Nothing about his paper, his order or his brother is raised. He is not named.

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 374

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** and **nothing in this batch reads it** · the
Clay Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never
opened** and never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised
(**returns zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fourteen chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven ·
*kiln-born* zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never named · the ch
150 refusal not softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the copying floor entered,
described or named** · the surface answer not re-voiced · **the claim not reopened** · Hou Sanliang paid, never met,
not on the page · Ning Wu does not appear and Ning Bo does not appear · **Wei Ran does not appear** · Feng does not
appear and no second Lung and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a romance · **she never says her mother's
name** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is on the page** · `Ledger` returns zero ·
**nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family
paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit, no examination, no signature** — the pressure
of this batch is being the smallest thing in a room, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0001, going into Batch 0002

- **The furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone, and there is no person on nine days of road she
  is entitled to ask.** She has worked out that the one person whose hands she could put in that corner is the man
  with the paste pot at a post on the mill road, who cannot read her book and would not tell anybody, and that the
  counting house is about two miles short of his own ladder on a road she walks four or five times a month.
  **She has not said it to anybody.** **She may not ask until ch 388.**
- **A strip went east on the first of a month at a quarter past the sixth hour and did not come back on the cart that
  takes the empty trays up.** It is about nine miles east and she is not going east of the turn.
- **A column nine days of road away has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months**, and the
  change came about three days after the strip went east, and she cannot read a word of either shape.
- **Every stage she takes from ch 371 onward is a public fact on this ground.** Two men on a bank at the seventh hour
  on the third of a month have seen one happen.
- **She is the smallest thing in every yard she walks into and the reason is on the page: a yard prices a body, and
  every office she has been entered in has her column in it, and there is nowhere on nine days of road that has her
  arm on a line.**
- **The board is full and she can take none of it, the gate pays one copper a day at three and a half feet, and the
  only work open to her on that ground is unpaid length work at a yard and reading that nobody pays for.**

| Employment | **NOTHING.** No wages, no season, no booking, no stub. Her refusal stands in a counter's book with the twenty-sixth against it. Her own book stands on a stone under a keeper's ladder nine miles west, and she has not gone near that post in six months |

**Nine categories of fault were found by measurement while drafting and all nine were cut before the batch was saved:
four instances of a named weekday, two of the retired *about four seconds*, twenty-two of the *nobody* family, one
byte-identical duplicate paragraph, seven paragraphs between 94 and 103 words, straight apostrophes in ten files, and
two chapter titles that were not names. No beat, day-table position, title, turn, midpoint, climax, irreversible
decision, bracket or stage moved in any of them, and no chapter in `chapters/volume-01/` … `chapters/volume-06/` was
opened.**

**One discrepancy between the batch prompt and the page, resolved in favour of the prompt's rule and recorded.** The
prompt pinned ch 365 at days 853–856 and named three of them. The page carries **853–855**, which are the thirtieth,
the first and the second, and ch 366 opens on the third. **Every other pinned item in the prompt is on the page at the
month-day the prompt's own rule gives it.**

**One thing the prompt did not have and the page built: the season's date.** The board is bare until the season turns
and the prompt did not say when. **The page turns it on the eleventh of a month — day 864 — and the eleven lines it
comes back with are all dressed stone priced by the ton**, which is what makes the batch's third beat work: the board
fills, every line on it is a line her right hand cannot take, and the only work left narrows to reading, which nobody
on that ground will pay for.

## Volume 7 Batch 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-07-batch-0001.review.log`)

**Six findings, three of them the reviewer's actionable three. The batch was not restarted, no chapter in
`chapters/volume-01/` … `chapters/volume-06/` was opened, no beat, day-table position, turn, midpoint, climax,
irreversible decision, bracket or stage moved, and `outline/volume-07.md` was not re-planned.**

### 1. The money did not reconcile at ch 374 — FIXED, and it was a real arithmetic error

The page had her earn thirteen coppers over thirteen gate days, go to the store twice at eight coppers a trip, take a
fifth of a sack of clay, **spend nothing**, and finish on **twenty-three coppers** — which is also ch 362's *twenty-three
of thirty*, a different figure from a different sum, reused.

**The store's price is fixed three times in the batch and always the same: four loaves for eight coppers (ch 362, in the
storekeeper's mouth), bread for five days, eight coppers (ch 366 and ch 372).** Two trips out of thirteen coppers is
sixteen out of thirteen in, and the clay is dug out of a drain and is not bought.

**The page now says one trip, on the seventh, and five coppers at the end**, and it says the sum out loud in her own
arithmetic — *thirteen days, thirteen coppers, one trip west at eight, five in the fold in the inside of the door frame* —
so the figure is checkable by the reader and it checks. **She enters the thirteen days with eight days' bread (ch 371,
ch 372) and buys five more, which is the whole of what she eats.**

**A second figure in the same chapter did not count and was cut.** *The List She Did Not Say Out Loud* named seven slots
and then said six people, because *the man behind the counter at the mill road* was the same man as *the man behind the
counter* two items earlier, and the sum *came out in five*. **The list is six, the duplicate is gone, and the sum now
comes out in six.** A book whose whole aesthetic is arithmetic cannot have two places where a reader's count and the
narrator's count come out different.

### 2. Twelve of fourteen chapters sat below the pacing floor — FIXED

**23,101 → 27,114 words of body prose, average ~1,650 → ~1,937. All fourteen chapters now sit inside the 1,800–2,700
guide.** The gap was a drafting artefact, not a plan: the batch had fourteen cards and a writer who built a chapter to
the card and stopped. **The expansion added scene material inside the existing scenes and changed no card.** Sixty-one new
paragraphs: the cold out of a four-foot cut and the hasp on an unlocked box (361) · the cold room behind a store counter
she has thought about for six months (362) · a worn stair, a brush worn to a stub, nine men at a counter and four on a
floor (363) · the fourth and fifth times she saw a reading leave a room, and why they were not the same thing (364) ·
the loaf she carried in her coat for three miles, the elms, and the shape of the road drawn out on the boards (365) ·
two hours on a shoulder with her hands empty, a carter who does not slow down, and the ninth hour for a cart that takes
nine (366) · reading a board up a column instead of along a line, and the anger that was not at him (367) · nine men who
have seen a person once (369) · a third thing to try at the fourth hour, holding her fingers against the brick until the
two temperatures agreed (371) · catching herself in the doorway with the lump already in her hand, the hasp that is never
locked, and *she had not been making a fire, she had been doing a thing beside a fire* (372) · a barrow pushed four miles
up a made road and brought back with the clay still in it (373) · the six days east, the boy putting the stick down
through the rails, and the cup of something hot she could not make an opening out of (374).

### 3. `about` had become a tic, and the budget did not measure it — FIXED

**312 → 197.** Detail and method in the budget row above. **The reviewer's larger point was structural, and it is the part
that matters: the batch prompt names `about…` as the first construction to watch and the budget measured it nowhere.**
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` has been given a measured row for it.

### 4. Twelve eight-word paragraph openings recurred — FIXED to two planted pairs

Ten of twelve recast, listed in the budget row. **The two that remain are on the page on purpose**: the Hunter's sentence
said once and repeated in her head eleven lines later (369), and *The fire did not come up on the …*, which opens the run
of thirteen days at ch 372 and closes it at ch 374.

**A third repeat was not a repeat at all but a rule breach, and the repair found it.** The prompt says the cost is
*named once and never re-explained in any later chapter*. **Ch 374 was quoting it — *A stage cannot be built by one pair
of hands* — four chapters after it was named at ch 371. Ch 374 now pays the cost instead of saying the line**, which is
what that chapter was for.

### 5. Six titles opened on the word *What* — FIVE RENAMED

Listed in the budget row. **One *What* is left, at ch 371, and a table of contents with one *What* in it is a table of
contents.** All five replacements are names; none restates the scene, the hour, the day, an age or the weather.

### 6. `week` was reported as 0/0/0 and was not 0 — FIXED IN THE BUDGET, NOT IN THE PROSE

**Sixteen ordinary durations stand in the batch — *a week's bread* twice, *twice a week*, *a week later*, *two weeks
back*, *three weeks*, *six weeks*, *a fortnight* — and none of them is a named week.** The page was right; the budget's
row was measuring the wrong thing and reporting a clean 0/0/0. **The row is now two columns, name and duration, so the
next batch cannot hide behind it again.** One duration was recast anyway, at ch 370, where *for a week* was also wrong
arithmetic and is now *and she did not think about it again*.

### What did not change

The plot, the cards, the day table, the pinned days, the bracket and its position, the midpoint at ch 368, the physical
risk at ch 370, the irreversible decision at ch 371, the merit settlement, the Hunter's single appearance, and every
thing still open at ch 374. **The batch is still fourteen chapters, still one bracket, still no office named above the
copying floor, still no weekday and no named month anywhere, still nothing killed.**

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0002 (ch 375–389) — WHAT SHE IS WORTH IN A ROOM

**Fifteen chapters, 27,966 words of body prose, average ~1,864, day 900 to day 950.** **ONE bracketed layer line, at
ch 383 — bracket 2 of Volume 7's six in sixty, character for character in the far-side house form.** Chapter titles
and section headings excluded. Drafted against `outline/volume-07.md` and against
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, which was this batch's card. **The prompt's corrected handoff was right and
the page followed it: the batch opens on day 900, the seventeenth, the day after ch 374 closed — not on the first, not
on a stage day, and not on a pay-day, and this ground has no pay-day.**

Per file, body prose: 375 1,895 · 376 1,929 · 377 1,894 · 378 1,815 · 379 1,838 · 380 1,824 · 381 1,819 · 382 1,903 ·
383 1,818 · 384 1,812 · 385 1,792 · 386 1,838 · 387 1,878 · 388 1,827 · 389 2,084. **All fifteen inside the 1,800–2,700
guide, none under the 1,300 floor and none above the ceiling.** Volume 7 file set after this batch: **55,080 words of
body prose over twenty-nine chapters.**

### The batch in one sentence

She worked out that a man who keeps a board has to fetch his own strips, burned a man who was owed nothing by saying
*yes* out loud when she could have said four words and did not, walked east of the turn for the first time in her life,
stood at the top of a stair in a room full of open books and was told in eleven words that there was no place there for
her, took her third stage in that room, found that her hand had stopped being able to lie, was offered a line of her
own for ten coppers and could neither pay it nor take it, went into a four-foot cut that came in on her and cost her
the last of her fist, and then said a woman's name out loud in a room and asked her for four hours a month and gave
her own name to get it.

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

Pei looks at it, says nothing the third time, and goes for his cup |
| Bread | two coppers a loaf, four loaves for five days at eight, store five miles west | **the same, and the regime settles: a trip on the sixth, the fourteenth, the twenty-second and the thirtieth of a month.** Seven coppers in seven days and eight out of bread was the standing arithmetic and it has been eating a stock since she came past this mill; the stock is gone and the arithmetic has come out level |

| The reading | a strip in her coat she cannot read, and a column nine days east with two lines off it | **the same strip, taken up nine steps four times a month and put on the table. Both threads carried and neither touched** |

| Her hands | cold since the second of a month, and she had stopped expecting them to be warm | **cold on every road between the shed and that table, and warm in that table's room when a woman is in it, about a hundred yards short of a gate on the mill road where they go cold again every time** |

### The second pair of hands — ch 378, day 914, the first of a month

**The batch's engine and the card that pays NS 2's cost without ever saying it.**

- **She works out at the gate on the seventeenth that a man who keeps a board has to fetch his own strips**, and that he
  fetches them at the counting house on the first of a month with the cart, because that is the only morning in the
  month anything comes down that road. **She does not go to the post.** The prompt's tension is resolved the way the
  page had already half resolved it: the counting house is on the mill road, she walks it four or five times a month,
  and a man whose trade is strips has an ordinary reason to be standing in a gateway on his own road.
- **On the first of a month at about the sixth hour, in a low building with nine men in it, a man about fifty-five with
  a paste pot and a rag gets down off a high stool with his left hand flat on the floor, and the floor comes up about a
  second and a half before it is under his hand.**
- **She had four words available and she did not say them**, because a word said at the sixth hour in a low building
  with nine men in it goes where four people can agree about it later, and she cannot read the hand.
- **He asks her one question — *did you do that* — and she says the word.** *Yes.* **That is the batch's worst thing
  happening to her at the hands of a person who is right:** he looks at her arm for two seconds, the way a man looks at
  a thing he is working out where it goes in an order, says he has got a bundle two miles up the road and has not got
  a hand that closes, and goes.
- **He is not a fool and not a saint.** He does not grab at her and he does not offer her anything. He tells her what
  it was — the floor of a room that was hot, and he has been in buildings for forty years and that has not happened in
  one — and says he is going to put water on it and is not going to think about it again.
- **She carries his bundle two miles up the mill road because it cannot be carried in one hand. That is the one thing
  she could do for him, it took fifty minutes, and it did not make the hand better.**
- **It works once.** On the same night the ring in the shed comes up a finger's width and stops, six times in an hour
  and a half, and it is the same fire it had been that morning. **The cost is paid, not said.**

### The third floor, and Rao — ch 380–383

- **The copying floor is where the man with the brush appears in this batch, once.** Eleven years copying down, cannot
  read the fine hand, says so and has said so three times. He tells her about the floor above his: one more flight,
  about nine steps shorter, colder at the north end. He has been up those steps twice, both times with a tray, both
  times about eleven years ago, and does not know the woman's name on it. **She spends her one question a month asking
  whether anybody is on the floor above his**, and is told that there is a stair, that there is no man at the top of
  it with a list, and that she has been standing at the bottom of it for six months looking at the top of it.
- **The third floor is the largest set of open books she will stand in front of.** Forty open at once with the leaves
  standing at every angle, a table the length of nine men, two windows that have not been opened. Cold at the north
  end all year.
- **Rao.** About forty-five, four feet ten, a thumb gone off the right hand at the first joint, sixteen years on that
  floor, forty thousand columns read. **She gives the terms of the trade in eleven words and is right about all of
  them: there is no line on that floor for a woman who walks up a stair.** Ning Yan is not angry about it for even a
  minute, and that is new.
- **Rao's structural answer is ch 391 and is Batch 0003's. She does not give it here.** What she gives in this batch
  is the shape of the floor and the price of a margin correction, and the fact that **she cannot read the fine hand
  either and has asked twice in sixteen years for somebody who can and been told there was nobody to send.**
- **Her name is given and refused and then given.** Rao asks for it on the eighth and does not ask twice. Ning Yan
  gives it on the twenty-eighth of a month, which is the batch's irreversible step.

### The first offer of a line entered for her — ch 384–385

- **Ch 384, the fourth floor price: four coppers for a margin correction in the ordinary hand, and she has eight.**
  Rao says it is four for everybody and always has been and she has never known a person try to move it. **Ning Yan
  could have paid it and does not take it, and the reason is not money and she works that out in about a minute:** a
  line she did not write is a line she cannot check, and the whole of the last seven months has been learning to read
  the shape of what is written about her.
- **Ch 385, the counter's offer, and the batch's first:** the man behind the counter offers to enter a line of her own
  in the seasonal book — the only book on nine days of road that shuts — because that is what the book is, and it is
  **ten coppers**, which is a day at that counter and ten days at Pei's gate. **She cannot pay it and she cannot take
  it, and she says the two numbers and does not say no to anything.**
- **The escalation is on the page and nobody on that ground knows it:** before the eleventh of a month there were
  three places inside nine days of road where a person could have paid four coppers to have a false line put into a
  book in the ordinary hand. **From the eleventh of a month there are none** — not hers, because her hand will not do
  it, and not anybody else's, because she has eight coppers and the wanting is not in the same place as the four.

### Mouth two of the structural answer — ch 379, day 915, the second

**A man who keeps a gate about a hundred yards short of the post, sixty, on the road between the counting house and
the post, with forty acres on one side of it.** Four or five sentences, in short sentences, and **never as a speech**:

“Forty acres here are worth what forty acres here are worth because of a thing I have never seen and could not get to if I
wanted it.” · “It is kept so that this ground stays worth farming.” · “Somebody else keeps the line. I have watched them
go past twice a day for forty years and I have never stopped one and asked him his name, and he would not have told me,
and he would have been right not to.” · “Nobody has come down that road in sixty years asking me what it is for. Not my
father. Not me.” · “You are going to stand at that post about four times a month now. And I am going to see you do it.
And I am not going to ask you about it.”

**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** It is a different statement from Volume 6's seven
mouths, from ch 365's carter and from the gate man's. **She does not sit on the bench under the sycamore**, because
sitting on a bench at a gate that is not his is a thing four people could agree about later, and she had not spent her
month's question and was not going to spend it on a bench.

### Nascent Soul 3 — ch 383, day 924, the eleventh of the month beginning 914

Taken at the seventh hour on the top floor of a building, standing at the edge of a table with a pencil in her left hand
and her right arm open, **in a room with the door shut and no other living thing in it**, and it came up out of the
boards and went under a table and forty open books and moved nothing.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage three, is held.]

**It is bracket 2 of Volume 7's six in sixty, and the form does not change at any of the six. Thirty-eight days after
Nascent Soul 2 at day 886.**

**Rao comes in ten minutes after the seventh hour, with an empty cup, and stops in the doorway.** She asks what it was,
once, and is told once, and says the room was empty and she came in after it had gone. She then says four things: that
she is the only person on nine days of road whose trade is a piece of paper and a pencil; that she is not going to
write it down; that she is not going to pretend she does not know what it is; and that a woman who has been at a table
for sixteen years can look at a room and not write down what she saw in it, **and that this is what the table is for.**

**Cost, named once, permanent, and never re-explained in any later chapter: her hand stops being able to lie.** A line
she puts in ink in her own hand is a thing she means. She can still say a false thing out loud. **She can no longer
enter a false line in any book on nine days of road and there is no hand she can pay to enter one for her.** It is not
a limb and it is not a hold and it does not touch her body at all. It is found out in about a quarter of an hour on
the bottom margin of a leaf with a pencil: she sets up to write a seven where the column says a four, and her hand
writes a four, five times, fast and slow and upside down.

### Merit settles once, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **Ch 386–387, days 933–940 (the twentieth to the twenty-seventh of the month beginning 914).** **A ditch line at the
  north end of the yard at the turn, by the length, four feet, four hundred yards, entered on the works board on the
  thirteenth with the twenty-third against it in the margin**, because the low side of that ground goes at the end of a
  month and no ditch dug in wet will stand past it.
- **The man with the rule in his hand does not put her on it and says so twice, and then asks her for the first time
  on the twentieth, a day early, and says plainly that he is telling her now so that it is not a thing that happened to
  her tomorrow.** The sill line goes east on the twenty-second and takes the man on the ditch with it.
- **She is in the cut on the twenty-first and the twenty-second. At the fifth hour on the twenty-first the north face
  comes in on her from above and left, about a yard and a half of wet clay in about a second and a half, and her right
  arm is between it and the wall.** She gets out on her knees with her left hand in four seconds, and the arm goes into
  the other thing for two seconds and comes back. She sits on the bank for ten minutes and then gets back down and
  finishes the last hundred and sixty yards with her left hand alone.
- **Off on the twenty-second, a day early, checked by the man with the rule and two of his men, and rubbed off his own
  board with his own thumb.** **She is not on the board, her name is not in that yard, and it has never been.**
- **She cannot close her right hand afterwards. Four fingers and a thumb, and a closed fist was never in the eight of
  eleven holds.**
- **The share is three days and six hours, said out loud twice before it is spent, in his words, in the present tense,
  with the reason he is saying it twice.** She spends it on nothing. **The price is a day and a half of cold hands on
  the top floor on the twenty-fifth, in a room with a woman in it — and it is one afternoon, and not the beginning of
  anything.** **No Hand is spent in this batch. `Ledger` returns zero across all fifteen files. No figure of what is
  left of him is on any page. Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing and advising nothing

**Six utterances in fifteen chapters, outside the twenty-fourth of a month: ch 377 (one) · 379 (one) · 380 (one) ·
383 (one) · 387 (four, including the share told twice) · 389 (one).** Every sentence is in the present tense and none
of it buys anything, explains anything or moves an office. **He is readable without her and it costs nothing to say:
at ch 380 he observes that she did not ask the man with the brush for anything he could give her and asked him where
the room is.** The date of the morning he died is not given back, the unnamed state is not given back, **being sent
for is not opened, not foreshadowed in his mouth and does not happen to anybody else**, and there is no frame scene.

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 389

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** and nothing in this batch reads it · the Clay
Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never opened** and
never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised (**returns
zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fifteen chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven · *kiln-born*
zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never named · the ch 150 refusal not
softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the third floor entered, described or named** ·
the surface answer not re-voiced · the structural answer in one mouth of six and **the tenth part not given** · **the
claim not reopened** · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page · Ning Wu and Ning Bo not on the page · **Wei Ran
does not appear** · Feng does not appear and no second Lung and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a
romance · **she never says her mother's name** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is
on the page** · `Ledger` returns zero · **nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no
confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit, no
examination, no signature** — the pressure of this batch is what a room thinks she is worth, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0002, going into Batch 0003

- **Her name is in a stair book on the top floor of the only building inside nine days of road with open books in it**,
  written in the ordinary hand at the fourth hour on the twenty-eighth of the month beginning 914 by a woman of
  forty-five. **It goes up the mill road on the first at the sixth hour with a cord round the tray, comes back down on
  the ninth, and is copied out into a book on the floor below by a man with a brush who cannot read the fine hand and
  has never been able to.** **It cannot be taken out.**
- **The furnace lights only where she is with another living thing in the room, and she cannot choose the room.** She
  is at the third floor four times a month by an arrangement she asked for out loud and cannot un-ask.
- **A man about fifty-five with a burn on his hand is two miles up the mill road at a post, has said he is not going to
  think about it again, and has never been asked for anything by her.**
- **The fine hand, and a person on a floor who has asked twice in sixteen years for somebody who can read it and been
  told there was nobody to send.** Dai is unspent and is Batch 0004's.
- **A strip went east on the first of a month about two months back and did not come back, and a column nine days of
  road east has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months.** Neither thread has moved in this
  batch and neither is closed.
- **A false line can no longer get into any book inside nine days of road, and no person on that ground knows it.**

Her own book stands on a stone under a keeper's ladder with a rag over the corner of it, **and she stood four feet off it on the second of a month and did not touch it** |
| The books | **A stair book on the third floor has a name in it at the left of the twenty-eighth of a month, in the ordinary hand, entered at the fourth hour at a woman's request.** It goes up the mill road on the first with a cord round the tray and is copied out on the ninth. **She cannot read it and there is nobody inside nine days of road who can read it back to her** |
| Settlement | **Merit settled once in this batch, on a listed ditch clearing at the north end of the yard at the turn, closed and checked and off on the twenty-second of a month, not on a death. The share is three days and six hours, told out loud twice before it was spent, and spent out of her foundation on nothing. The price is a day and a half of cold hands.** **Hand is not spent in this batch.** `Ledger` returns zero across all fifteen files. **Yuan: six utterances in fifteen chapters, every sentence present tense, buying nothing and advising nothing; readable, addressed, and therefore sendable, and none of it is opened here** |
| Claim | **ANSWERED** and not reopened. Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page |
| Family | Ning Bo's account discharged at ch 296; Ning Wu's decade entered at ch 297. **Neither is on the page in ch 375–389** |
| Inside | **Nothing.** **The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong** |
| Still open | The Hunter's request pending and the line's own people still not knowing she exists · everything above the third floor, and above the counting house, and above the Standing Office of the Second Account, all unread and unnamed · **the fine hand, and a man whose job it is who can read it** · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · what became of the eleven · why the register reads *reconciled* · the year still carrying · **and a strip that went east on the end-of-month cart and did not come back, and a column that came back with two lines off it** |

### The batch in one sentence

A woman of forty-five read a column back to her and told her, once, that the strongest thing in that building is a
column; a woman who has driven clay for eleven years told her in four sentences where the money on that ground comes
from; she put a reach of her own into a stone in a bed with a man under it and the stone went four feet sideways
and did not come back; an office asked for the holder and the holder answered upward for the first time on that
ground and the answer came down her own mill road as a question about her, and she cannot refuse a reading again; and
a cup of something hot was put into her hands at the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth of a month and she could not
take it.

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

| | Entering the batch (day 950) | At the last page (day 1,020) |

| Gate at Pei's | five coppers a reach, **a fifth of a reach, one copper a day**, dug in two lengths with a step down between them | **the same, one copper a day.** Pei says on the twenty-fourth that he does not know where she got the four from and is not going to say his one permitted sentence again |
| Bread | two coppers a loaf, four loaves for five days at eight, store five miles west | **the same.** **Two trips are named on the page in this batch — the ninth at ch 391, which is fifteen less eight for four loaves and leaves seven, and the thirtieth at ch 396, which is eight out and bread for five days — and one purchase is placed without its date, at ch 397, *she had bought bread on the second of a month*. The fuller list of trip days carried from Batch 0002 (the ninth, the fourteenth, the twenty-second and the thirtieth, and the sixth, the fourteenth and the twenty-second of the next) is **not written out in ch 390–404 and is carried as Batch 0002's figure, not as this batch's.** Ten trips in seventy days across the batch is the aggregate and is not derivable from the chapters |
| Clay | six barrow-loads against the outside wall and a seventh on its handles, and a box of about a third of a sack | **the same, and none of it has ever been in a ring that lit.** A fifth of a sack is three coppers and she has not bought a sack |
| The yard at the turn | a length three days in seven, a cup of something hot, no name of hers anywhere | **the same.** The cup went into her hands thirteen times in seven weeks and the **thirteenth did not happen**, at the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth |
| The third floor | no money in it; four coppers for a margin correction and she has eight | **the same, unchanged, and neither figure is spent.** The four coppers are still the four coppers |
| Purse | **fifteen coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame, and no bread** | **four coppers, and bread for three days**, at day 1,004 and again at day 1,020 |

**The purse is carried as named anchors and not as a running total, and the reason is a repair.** The row above used to
read a chain — *fifteen at the seventh; twenty-one gate days and three trips west at the twenty-eighth, which is
eleven; six at the end of the thirtieth; twenty-six gate days and three trips west to the twenty-sixth of the next
month, which is seven; twenty-one gate days and three trips west to the last page, which is four* — **and that chain
did not add up: fifteen + twenty-one − twenty-four is twelve and not eleven, and *six at the end of the thirtieth*
could not be reached from *eleven at the twenty-eighth*.** `workspace/volume-07-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` had copied it
and told the next batch to carry it as the fixed checkable table.

**The chain has been struck and the anchors below replace it. Every figure is read off a chapter and none is
inferred:**

| Day | Month-day | Purse | Where |

| 952 | the 9th | **seven**, four loaves at eight | ch 391 |
| 955–957 | the 12th–14th | **seven**, unchanged | ch 392 |

**The steps between these anchors are not carried on the page as arithmetic and no derivation may be invented to

anchor. **Trips west are counted separately and are not purse arithmetic: four trips west at eight inside ch 396 alone,
and ten trips in seventy days across the whole batch.**

**What was repaired inside ch 396 at the review.** The paragraph set the sum out loud — *Fifteen at the seventh of that
month. Twenty-three days at the gate at a copper a day, which is twenty-three in. Four trips west at eight, which is
thirty-two out. Fifteen and twenty-three and thirty-two is six* — **and the sentence around it said she had *five* in
the fold and had *got five both times*. The paragraph asserted a verified sum and printed a figure its own
verification contradicted.** Two things were wrong with the same paragraph. **The balance is now six throughout, and
the third sentence carries the re-check in different words.** And **the fifteen were at the *eighth* of that month,
not the seventh: ch 390 carries day 951, which is the eighth, and ch 396 was contradicting its own batch.** The
handoff in `workspace/volume-07-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` said *seventh* and was wrong for the same reason, and said
*eleven at the twenty-eighth*, which was on no page at all; **both are corrected there and the struck chain is named
so that it is not copied again.**

- **No board line is taken in this batch. No listing. No wages. No season ending on a date.** The pay question does
  not turn until Nascent Soul 6 at ch 412, in Batch 0004, and **NS 4 and NS 5 close two other doors.**
- **Every figure in this batch comes out of Batch 0001's and Batch 0002's price tables and not out of a fresh
  guess.** No new price is invented anywhere in ch 390–404.

### The construction the batch is built on, and how it moves

| | Entering (day 950) | At the last page (day 1,020) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The reach | happened once, by accident, on a bank past the mill; never on purpose | **usable on purpose and four feet, and it does not go back and it does not go where she tells it.** Four deliberate uses at that yard and five holes in the low side of it |
| Her name | in one stair book on the top floor, going up the mill road on the first | **in two books, both of which shut, and she cannot get it out of either.** She has asked out loud for it to be taken out and a woman of forty-five has told her the shape of why it cannot |
| The yard at the turn | nine men where four will do, and no name of hers anywhere | **nine men who know a stone moved and not one of whom can say who moved it, and a page in a yard book that has come back down the road twice asking for a holder** |
| Her hands | cold on every road, warm in one room when a woman is in it | **the same, and she can no longer be given anything**, so the cup that came out of the office end on the twenty-sixth is standing on a bench going cold |
| Her reading | a column she could not have read back | **read to her, all of it, in the ordinary voice, on the eighth of a month** |

### The midpoint — ch 390, day 951, the eighth

**Rao reads her column back to her, all of it, in the ordinary voice, in a room, and the column contains a refusal,
and it is the only refusal in the set, and nobody on that floor has ever had one to read.**

- The column as it goes on the page: her name in a hand Rao cannot read · five months of days and hours down the
  margin · a season marked at the first end and **not marked at the second end** · a place four miles east of a
  building with a stove in it, in the ordinary hand, a third of the way down · a rule underneath it with nothing at
  the end of it · and at the foot of the page, dated the twenty-sixth, in the ordinary hand of that building,
  *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. The holder is not presented. Refused at the counter. The twenty-sixth.*
- **It says no.** Said twice, in short sentences, and the second time with the room named: **the room was cold at
  the north end and it had been cold at the north end since before she came on it.**
- **Rao has read forty thousand columns and has never read one back to the person it belongs to**, and she says the
  reason out loud: nobody on nine days of road has ever come up nine steps and asked her to.
- **The strongest thing in this building is a column.** Said **once**, in Rao's mouth, on the page, and followed in
  the same breath by *I am not going to say it a second time.* **It is not repeated anywhere in ch 390–404 and
  Volume 8 inherits a book in which it has been said exactly once.**
- **The reversal, in her own words on the mill road, and it costs nothing and undoes nothing:** she has been standing
  next to it for two months of months and working at the wrong end of it; every room that has priced her has priced
  her right, and every price has been for the hands and the arm and four feet of a bar.
- **The chapter's own sentence about a room, which is not the ch 369 refrain and does not quote it:** *Everything
  that has ever been done to me on nine days of road came out of a book in a room, and I have never once been in the
  room while it was happening.*
- **The second line at the head of her column is a name and it is not read.** Rao says she has looked at it four
  times in sixteen years, it is why the rule has nothing at the end of it, and it goes up the mill road on the
  first. **Being sent for is not opened here and is not foreshadowed in anybody's mouth.**

### Mouth three of the structural answer — ch 391, days 952–954, in Rao

Four sentences and no more, said without looking up, in the middle of reading: **what is on the line is paid for out
of a place she has never seen and has never had a road to · a column came up off that line once in eleven years with
nothing at the end of the rule and she has never found out what that meant · the room it is paid out of is not on any
strip she has ever been sent and is not in that building · and whoever sends it has never once sent anything to
ask.**

**What is new in it:** a column is a bill somebody else is paying, the season at the head of it is their season, and
an office on this ground has been sitting on an unanswered column for eleven years **and has not given up on it.**
**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.**

### The reach, used on purpose — ch 393–397

- **Ch 393, days 958–959.** A listed sill course at the south end of the yard at the turn: **by the weight,
  thirty-one dressed stones, four feet of bed, off the board with the twenty-eighth against it in the margin,
  because the low side of that ground goes at the end of a month.** Nine men where four will do. A dressed stone two
  men cannot shift. On the sixteenth, at the tenth hour, a bar goes and a man's leg is under it for about nine
  seconds and he gets it out in four.
- **Ch 394, day 960 — NASCENT SOUL 4, bracket 3 of six, in the open at the seventh hour at the south end of that
  yard with nine men on it and two of them nine feet off.**

  [Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage four, is held.]

  **Thirty-six days after Nascent Soul 3 at day 924.** **This is the one chapter in the volume that is not a bracket
  chapter and is where she hears the last four words of it and finds they have stopped being true**, because there is
  no county within nine days of road and there are people in rooms on this ground who are above her. She thinks it.
  The layer does not change.
- **What she does, on purpose, at the eighth hour of the seventeenth, with a man's leg under the stone: she puts the
  reach there.** It goes the length of her own arm and stops. **It goes sideways and about nine inches down into the
  open ground on the low side, where the clay has been scraped off, and it is not in the bed and not on the line.**
- **Cost, named once, permanently, and never re-explained in any later chapter: a thing she reaches cannot be put
  back where it was.** The bed it came out of has a lip on it. The ground it crossed stays crossed.
- **She wanted it to go up.** She worked out on the hedge on the sixteenth, before she did it, that the asking would
  move it four feet in a line it picks, and she did it anyway, and she was sick at the office end at two hundred
  yards afterwards.
- **Ch 395, days 961–968.** Four more deliberate uses in eight days, all off ends of planks and rims of barrows and
  out of a bed. The fire comes up under a man's boot at the sixth hour on the twenty-second and she cannot turn it
  off. **The man with the rule in his hand tells her the yard book is not his and does not stay in that yard and
  there is no column on this ground for what is written at the foot of a page.**
- **Ch 397, days 975–980. She tries to put it back and it opens a second hole.** Four hundred pounds of dressed
  stone, one in each, five feet apart, and four men on the bed say *they have put another one in there* and *that is
  the third*, without turning round. **And on the seventh, at the boards, at the second hour, she works out the
  difference between the two spends: the fire moves when she asks.** The five months of merit spent on nothing came
  back on the sixth by itself; a thing her hand reaches does not.

### Merit settles once — ch 395–396, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **The line came off the works board at the fourth hour of the twenty-eighth**, checked by the man with the rule in
  his hand and two of his men, and rubbed off with his own thumb. Twenty-nine stones instead of thirty-one and two
  hundred and ten feet of bed instead of three hundred.
- **The share is four days and two hours**, out of a line that closed at the end of a month and **not off a death**,
  said out loud twice at the second hour on the boards before it is spent, with the reason it is being said twice.
- **She spends it on nothing**, out of her foundation, and it does not come back. **The price is not a limb and not a
  hold and not a count of breath: the fire stops coming up for four days** — the twenty-ninth, the first, the second,
  and the third — and on the sixth, at the fourth hour, on the top floor of the only building inside nine days of road
  with open books in it, the back of her left hand is cold against cold wood for the **first time in nineteen
  sittings**, and a woman of forty-five says *Wood’s cold*, and turns the page.
- **No Hand is spent on it and `Ledger` returns zero across all fifteen files. No figure of what is left of him is
  stated on any page. Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Mouth four of the structural answer — ch 398, days 981–985, in a woman who drives clay

A woman of fifty who has driven clay up a track from a bank two hundred yards off that yard for eleven years, for
that yard and four others, with a barrow and a plank on the handles and a forearm like a man's. She says it while
tipping a barrow, in the middle of eleven trips:

> There is a household nine miles west of me that has paid me in clay every month for eleven years. It is not in this
> county and I do not know anybody in it. I have never seen the house. I go where the clay goes and I come back. And
> it has never once been late.

**What is new in it, and it is the same fact from a different mouth: somebody nine miles west of that bank has been
paying a woman nine miles east of it for eleven years out of a house she has never seen and is not able to go to.**
**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** It is not the same four sentences as ch 365, ch
379 or ch 391 and it is never a speech.

### The third frame scene — ch 401, days 993–994, and it is the book's protagonist's act

**Away from her hearing, in the layer's own reading-space, and every sentence he speaks is in the present tense.**
It is **not** the field of ch 272 and **not** the room with the two shut doors of ch 338, and the chapter says so in
its first two lines. **It is not a description of him and it is not a re-run of either.**

- **The place:** a narrow room with a low ceiling and no furniture, one strip of paper lying flat on the floor nine
  feet off, light with no source and no shadow, and no door he can find.
- **What is on it:** two lines in a hand he has never seen, saying that a line has not been carried to a room and
  that a holder is required against it. It came down at the eighth hour of the eighteenth and is not a strip that has
  been in a coat for five months.
- **He could leave it.** That is the honest shape of it and he stands there with it for nine seconds. **Being held is
  a thing that can be answered now and he has known that for four months and has not answered anything, because there
  was nothing to answer.** He turns toward it.
- **What it costs him.** He looks for the morning and it is not there and it is not going to be. He looks for the
  unnamed state and it is not there either. **All three of his prices — his unentered state, his unnamed state and
  the date of the morning he died — stay gone and none of them is given back, and none of them is re-explained.**
- **The third thing, and it is worse than the second because an answer can be quoted:** being answerable is not
  being findable and it is not being sendable. A finding can be denied, a sending can be refused once, **an answer
  cannot be refused at all, because an answer is the only one of the three that is a thing anybody can quote.**
- **What it goes out of:** the ground she is standing on, under her feet, at the second hour on the nineteenth of a
  month, in a shed at a mill whose wheel has not turned. Not out of her hands. He cannot reach into a person and has
  not been able to since he died. **He has felt the one before, at a counter, on the first of a month, and he does not
  know whose it was and he did not ask, and tonight he takes more and she will work out a floor and cannot ask.**
- **He does not tell her. She cannot hear him and he is not going to pretend to her that he can.** He does not think
  about the account, and the year still carries and the subject is not mentioned in these fifteen chapters.
- **He does not do it twice**, because one is what he has and two would be a habit, and a habit is a thing that gets
  found.

### The break — ch 402, days 995–996

**An office asks for the holder and the holder answers, on purpose, and the holding is used to move an office rather
than a cart. All three costs are named before they are spent.**

1. **Out of her foundation, told before spent.** She says it on the boards at the second hour on the nineteenth, out
   loud, to an empty room: *A holder's answer is paid out of the foundation of the person it is about.* She says it
   twice, because it is a thing she wants to hear twice. **And then she says that he will not do it, and that she
   knows that the way she knows her own hands.**
2. **The third frame scene away from her hearing, at ch 401.**
3. **She can never again refuse a reading**, and it lands in her own words in the plain part: not because there is a
   rule against it and not because anybody has decided it, but because an office on this ground now holds an answer
   that has already been given, and a second refusal would be a refusal of an answer already given, and an office that
   holds an answer already given does not accept a second one.

**How it happens on the page.** A strip came down the mill road at the eighth hour of the eighteenth and did not come
down on a first and did not come down on a ninth. The man behind the counter takes it up nine steps because that is
where a holding is kept, and Rao reads it standing up in eleven seconds and reads it twice, and writes nine words at
the top of it. **On the twenty-second, at about the eighth hour, the same strip comes into the counting house in a
way that has not happened in eleven years and there is no cart and no post and no boy on the mill road.** Under the
nine words there are two lines in a hand that has never been in that room: *The holder has answered. The holder has
been read to and the reading has been carried up and this ground has not had that happen before. The answer is
given.* And under those two, the question: *The answer names a woman refused at this counter on the twenty-sixth of a
month six months back. Is that woman at the gate three miles east of the mill.*

**She says *Yes*.** In the ordinary voice, in daylight, on a step, at four feet. He writes eleven words in the
seasonal book, which shuts, in the ordinary hand: *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. At the gate three miles east of the mill.
The twenty-second.*

**What breaks is the assumption that a line on this ground can only be read downward.** He says it on the step, in the
ordinary voice, and every part of what he says is correct.

**What is not undone.** **Nothing takes the refusal at ch 358 out of its book.** The man behind the counter says on
the step that the refusal from the twenty-sixth is still in that book, that nothing has come off it, and that he is
not going to touch it. **What has gone is not the entry. It is the second time.**

**She was not got.** She stands four hundred yards down the made road and works that out on the boards nine times,
and a man had come out of a building in daylight and told her a true thing about a piece of paper and she had answered
it, and an office on nine days of road had just read a line upward for the first time in eleven hundred years of
practice, and the price had come out of a shed three miles east and arrived at a gate without anybody carrying it.

**The yard page goes up the same road on the same cart.** The man with the rule in his hand wrote his margin note on
the twenty-first, in his own hand, exactly as he said he would on the thirteenth: **two holders are now required
against the seventeenth of a month, and there is one holder on this ground.** Three men at that yard have read it.

### Nascent Soul 5 — ch 404, day 999, bracket 4 of six

Taken at the seventh hour on the north end of the yard at the turn, in the open, in front of nine men, and it came up
under a forty-foot clay stack and through the strip of plank a man had laid down over the place where his boot had
been on the twenty-second of a month.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage five, is held.]

**Thirty-nine days after Nascent Soul 4 at day 960.**

**Cost, named once: she cannot be given anything.** A gift that a person puts into her hand enters the column, and
the column can be read, and a thing that has been given is a thing an office can read her having been given. **She
can still be owed. She can still refuse to pay.** It is not a limb and not a hold and it does not touch her body.

**What it costs on the page: a cup of something hot.** The woman in the office end has put one into her hands
thirteen times in seven weeks and has never once asked her name and has never put a word about her in a book
anywhere on nine days of road. On the thirteenth, at the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth, **her hand goes toward the
cup and stops four inches short of it.** The woman says *Take it*, in the ordinary voice, and then *All right*, and
goes back in. She stands there a minute and a quarter and puts it on the edge of the bench with the office end
against it, which is where things go at that yard, **and the cup is hot for four minutes and then it is not hot.**

She had wanted it every one of the thirteen times and had not once thought about where it went.

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing, advising nothing

**Five passages in fifteen chapters: ch 393 (three sentences) · ch 396 (three) · ch 397 (three, on the boards) · and
the third frame scene at ch 401, which is his act.** Every utterance is an unmarked paragraph standing alone in the
present tense. **He buys nothing, explains nothing, advises nothing and advances nothing.** **He appears in his own
frame nowhere else in ch 390–404, and ch 401 is the third frame scene in six volumes and is Batch 0003's.**

**Hand is spent once in this volume, across ch 401–402:** he lets a send reach him and answers it on the twenty-first
at day 994 in the third frame scene, the answer is carried up the holding and an office moves, and it arrives on her
own road on the twenty-second at day 995, where the break lands at ch 402. `Ledger` returns **zero** across all
fifteen files. **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page.** **Being answerable is new as of ch 401 and
worse than being sendable, because an answer can be quoted.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 404

The register still reads *reconciled* — **`reconciled` returns zero and nothing in this batch reads it** · the Clay
Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never opened** and
never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised (**returns
zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fifteen chapters and still carrying** · Shen Xiu
unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never
named · the ch 150 refusal not softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the third floor
entered, described or named** · the surface answer not re-voiced · the structural answer in **two mouths of six**, at
ch 391 and ch 398, and **the tenth part not given** · the claim not reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on
the page · Ning Wu and Ning Bo not on the page · **Wei Ran does not appear** · Feng does not appear and no second Lung
and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a romance · **she never says her mother's name** · **the Hunter is
not on the page in this batch** and his request is still pending and the line's own people still do not know she
exists · **the fine hand and the man whose job it is are not on the page and the fine hand is not read** · **Dai and
Geng are unspent and are Batch 0004's** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is on
the page** · `Ledger` returns zero · **nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no
confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit,
no examination, no signature** — the pressure of this batch is the reach used on purpose and the direction of a
sending, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0003, going into Batch 0004

- **She can never again refuse a reading.** The second line is in the seasonal book with the twenty-second against it
  and an office on this ground now holds an answer she gave in the ordinary voice.
- **The yard page is on its way up the mill road and comes down on the ninth** with *two holders are now required
  against the seventeenth of a month and there is one holder on this ground* in the margin, in a yard-book hand, with
  a third man who has read it.
- **Her name is in two books inside nine days of road and she has asked out loud for it to be taken out and a woman of
  forty-five has told her the shape of why it cannot.**
- **Four dressed stones in the low side of that yard under clay and dressed stone, one of which she put there
  deliberately and one of which she put there trying to undo the first.**
- **A reach that goes four feet, does not go back, and does not go where she tells it.**
- **A cup she cannot take, and a woman in an office end who will keep bringing one.**
- The strip east that did not come back · the fine hand and the man whose job it is · *kiln-born* in two heads ·
  what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled* · the plate still worn · the year still carrying.

because a budget that only ever reports clean is not a budget.**

The earlier version of this row said six and its own chapter list omitted ch 401's *nobody read it*; the count is seven.** **Thirty-nine stood after the drafting passes and thirty-three were recast to positive or particular constructions** — *this building does not know what to do with it*, *there is not a mark*, *there was not a living soul in the room*, *there was not a hand in that yard on the ground*, *it is not on this ground and it cannot be produced on this ground*, *this ground is not going to tell her*, *every man holding one of them has done the correct thing with it*. **Each of the seven is load-bearing**: ch 391's *not one* is an office that has sat on an unanswered column for eleven years; ch 394's is a reach she cannot test twice; ch 397's is the fire; ch 398's two are the clay driver's eleven years and the chapter's structural beat; ch 401's is a strip of paper lying on a floor for two minutes; ch 403's *not one* is a name she cannot get out of a book |

## Volume 7 Batch 0003 (ch 390–404) — REVIEW REPAIR

**The review of volume-07-batch-0003 returned four must-fix findings and five craft findings. All four must-fix and
four of the five craft findings are repaired. One craft finding is ruled on and left standing, with the ruling
recorded. No planned plot moved, no day-table position moved, no bracket moved, no stage moved, no title changed and
no chapter outside ch 390–404 was opened. Nine chapter passages were rewritten and every one of them was re-measured
afterwards.**

### The four must-fix findings

1. **Ch 396's arithmetic contradicted itself, in the one paragraph that performed its own check.** The paragraph set
   the sum out loud — *Fifteen at the seventh of that month. Twenty-three days at the gate at a copper a day, which is
   twenty-three in. Four trips west at eight, which is thirty-two out. Fifteen and twenty-three and thirty-two is
   six* — **and the sentence around it said she had *five* in the fold and had *got five both times*.** The text
   asserted verification and failed it. **The balance is now six throughout, and the third sentence carries the
   re-check in different words so that the verification is stated once and lands.** The same paragraph also said
   *the seventh of that month* for the fifteen, **and ch 390 carries day 951, which is the eighth** — ch 396 was
   contradicting its own batch. **Now *the eighth*.** Two faults, one paragraph, both in the fixed price table the
   prompt tells the next batch to carry exactly.
2. **The Batch 0004 prompt's money table did not match the page it claimed to quote.** It read *fifteen at the
   seventh, eleven at the second hour of the twenty-eighth, six at the end of the thirtieth, seven at the twenty-sixth
   of the next month, and four at day 1,020, with ten trips west at eight in seventy days.* Verified against the page:
   the fifteen are at the **eighth**; **eleven coppers is on no page in any of the fifteen chapters**; the balance at
   the thirtieth was **five** in the prose and is now six; the twenty-sixth carries **four**, not seven; and ch 396
   says **four** trips west, not ten. **Batch 0004 was told this was the fixed checkable table it must carry forward,
   and one figure in it did not exist.** It now carries the nine page-verified anchors, states that the steps between
   them are not carried on the page as arithmetic and that no derivation may be invented to bridge them, separates
   trips west from purse arithmetic with both spans named, and **records the struck chain so that it is not copied
   again.**
3. **Ch 404's last line spent ch 412.** It closed *the twenty-first day of the next month was in nine days and the
   board was full and none of it could pay her* — and Batch 0004's pinned beat is that **the board fills when the
   season turns and not one line on it can be entered against her, and the pay question closes at ch 412**, walked
   and not summarised, **eight chapters later.** **The line is cut.** The chapter now closes on the cost it actually
   owns: the cup on the bench, seven weeks of them behind it, another one coming inside three days, and her hand
   going to do the thing it did at the quarter past the sixth hour on the twenty-sixth. **Until the eighth hour of the
   twenty-sixth the not being able to take anything was a thing about four fingers and an arm that does not close.
   This is not about her hands at all.** That is a question opened and not a question answered, and it leaves ch 412
   entirely Batch 0004's.
4. **Ch 404's closing chronology was broken.** The sections ran *The Twenty-Seventh To The Thirtieth* → *The First Of
   The Next Month* → *The Last Of It*, and the last of them opened *She lay on the boards at the second hour of the
   twenty-eighth* — **three days before the section it follows**, and unreadable as the twenty-eighth of the new
   month, which is past the batch. **The heading is now *The Seventeenth* and the section opens at the second hour of
   the seventeenth, which is day 1,020**, so the tail runs 999 → 1,004 → 1,020 in order and **the chapter now says
   on the page what the prompt and this file have been claiming for it**, which is that the last page of Batch 0003 is
   day 1,020. The old closing line's *the twenty-one day of the next month was in nine days* could not hold from a
   twenty-eighth by any reading and is gone with the rest of the line.

### The craft findings

5. **Card instructions had leaked onto the page as section headings.** *The Cost, Named Once* at ch 404 reproduced its
   card's note, and *What It Costs Him To Be In The Place* at ch 401 did the same. **Now *The Cup And The Column* and
   *The Two Things He Goes Looking For*** — what the sections do, in the world's terms.
6. **Ch 401's closing line recycled a spent refrain.** *a habit is a thing that gets found* is ch 335's *A line is a
   thing that gets found* with the noun swapped, which is the same shape rather than a different sentence, and the
   budget had claimed 401 quoted neither. **Now reads** *the first thing that anybody on this ground does with a
   habit is put a line in front of it* — his trade, on his ground, and a sentence the volume has not used.
7. **Ch 393 ended on a flat exit line.** Its best line — *You have never once put anything anywhere on purpose. That is
   the whole of what is different tomorrow and you already know it* — sat two paragraphs from the end behind *Go and
   sleep, he said.* **The tag beat now stands where he stops buying anything and immediately before the speech, and
   the chapter ends on the line itself.** No beat, no line of his dialogue and no pinned item moved.
8. **Ch 401 narrated her judgement inside his frame.** *it was the truest thing anybody had ever said to her about
   the country she was standing in.* **RECORDED AND LEFT STANDING, on a ruling.** It is his sentence, said by him,
   about his own utterance of it at ch 272 and again at ch 338 in his own present-tense frame; the judgement is
   explicitly attributed to him in the same sentence, and **nothing about what she thought is asserted.** Recasting it
   would have broken a reprise that the Volume 6 close and the Volume 7 planning both rely on, and the review itself
   recorded this one as permissible.
9. **A *whole of* tic** at 40 instances in the batch and 131 in Volume 7. **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, and here is the
   count by method:** body prose, case-insensitive, whole word. **Only ch 397 uses it as a chapter ending, so it is
   not an endings breach, and it is a manuscript-level signature that belongs to a volume-scale pass and not to a
   fifteen-chapter repair.** It is named here so that Batch 0004 measures it and writes the true number down.

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0004 (ch 405–420) — WHAT IT COSTS TO BE RIGHT

**Recorded by the Volume 7 Batch 0004 drafting phase, which is the last batch of Volume 7. It wrote
`chapters/volume-07/chapter-0405.md` … `chapter-0420.md` and `workspace/volume-07-close/PROMPT.md` and wrote
no chapter beyond ch 420, no second prompt and no `.done` marker, which `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives to the controller
alone.**

**A NOTE ON THE PROVENANCE OF THESE CHAPTERS, because it is on the record and the next phase must know it.**
An earlier run of this batch produced a partial draft of all sixteen chapters and was deferred; the controller
committed it under `novel: defer volume-07-batch-0004` and then marked `workspace/volume-07-batch-0004/.wip-conflict`.
**That work was kept and repaired rather than thrown away.** The prose was sound and the beat distribution was
sound; the day-boundary arithmetic, the purse arithmetic and five continuity errors were not. **Every figure in this
block is measured against the chapters as they now stand, not carried over from the deferred draft, and the list of
what was repaired is at the foot of this block under *Volume 7 Batch 0004 — what the drafting phase repaired*.**
